INTRODUCTION

!

STATEMENT BY
THE CHAIRMAN OF
THE MANAGEMENT
BOARD

THE YEAR THAT LATVIA’S ENERGY
SYSTEM BECAME INDEPENDENT
AND BROKE THROUGH
TECHNOLOGICALLY
It is now over a year since the Baltic
States secured their energy independence.
In February 2025, the Baltic energy systems
severed their historical energy ties with
Russia irreversibly and joined the world's
biggest energy system - continental Europe.
We synchronised our energy systems with those
of Europe almost a year earlier than planned.
4


=== pdf p.5 ===
INTRODUCTION

Not only did consumers not feel the transition, but the
Baltic energy transmission grid is now more secure than
ever before, in considerable part due to the vital role
played in the process by the AS Augstsprieguma tīkls
(AST) Group.
In 2025, with a combined value of over €90 million, all
infrastructure projects related to synchronisation were
completed, cybersecurity was reinforced, and five
new and four existing connections to the transmission
network were built and modernised for the integration
of renewable resources in the amount of almost 500
MW. The battery management system that we jointly
developed by in partnership with RTU was recognised
by the Latvian Academy of Sciences as one of the most
notable scientific achievements in 2025. Meanwhile, the
joint work of the Baltic and Polish transmission system
operators won global recognition: the synchronisation of
the Baltic electricity grids won the project management
"Oscar" in the form of the US Project Management
Institute’s prestigious Project of the Year award.
The transition to synchronous operations with Europe,
along with the rapid development of renewable
energy resources, has significantly expanded the
responsibilities and responsibilities of AST in the
planning, management, operation and development
of electricity transmission system regimes. Despite,
efficiently secured EU funding, redirection of congestion

management revenues and the streamlining of
operations have enabled us to keep transmission tariffs
stable for the next three years.
We have no intention of resting on our laurels. On the
contrary, we have ambitious plans for the coming years,
which we plan to implement by continuing to actively
secure EU funding, including for infrastructure security
and resilience projects, as well as for the development of
new interconnections with Lithuania and Estonia.
Significant work has gone into in the integration of the
Baltic power market and the development of balancing
mechanisms: shortly before synchronization, the Baltic
Balancing Capacity Market was launched, a market
in which AST and its Baltic partners jointly buy the
balancing capacity reserves required for the operation
of the system. Thereafter, AST joined the European
automatic frequency restoration reserve energy
exchange platform PICASSO. In October, together
with the rest of Europe, Latvia switched to a 15-minute
interval for power trading, thus increasing trading
accuracy and facilitating more efficient coordination of

cross-border electricity flows. The transition to modern
balancing and market mechanisms led by AST ensures
the integration of renewable energy, improves system
flexibility and security, boosts market efficiency and
transparency, and generates new business opportunities
for market participants.
An essential resource for ensuring Baltic energy
independence is the Inčukalns underground gas
storage facility run by AST subsidiary Conexus Baltic
Grid (Conexus). With the opening of the natural gas
market and the development of the regional market,
the role played by the storage facility has increased,
as a result of which both the flexibility and security
of gas supply in the region have improved. In 2025,
with the objective of improving its functionality and
reducing the environmental impact of the storage
facility's technological processes, the reconstruction
of the storage facility was nearing completion.
Furthermore, last year, Conexus' first publicly available
biomethane entry point at "Džūkste" entered service
- a significant step towards expanding the capabilities
of gas transmission infrastructure and facilitating the
development of sustainable energy in Latvia. In the
next few years, it is planned that at least two more such
points will be built in Latvia.
I would like to take this opportunity to thank all the
Group's employees, customers and cooperation
partners for our joint achievements in 2025! The
Group will continue to provide continuous, secure and
sustainably efficient and competitively-priced energy
transmission, continually improving our performance
and thus fostering Latvia's competitiveness, sustainable
development and long-term value generation.

ROLANDS IRKLIS
Chairman of the Board,
AS Augstsprieguma tīkls

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

1

GENERAL
INFORMATION
ESRS 2

INFORMATION ON
THE STRUCTURE OF
THE GROUP
The principal activity of the AS Augstsprieguma tīkls
Group (hereinafter also referred to as "the Group") is to
fulfil the functions of the electricity transmission system
operator and to ensure the efficient management of
energy supply system assets, natural gas transmission
and storage.
As at 31 December 2025, the structure of the
Augstsprieguma tīkls Group consisted of a group of
commercial companies, in which the Parent Company
AS Augstsprieguma tīkls (hereinafter also referred to
as "AST" or "the Parent Company") has a decisive
influence and which includes the Subsidiary AS
Conexus Baltic Grid (hereinafter also referred to
as "Conexus" or "the Subsidiary"). All shares of AS
Augstsprieguma tīkls (100%) are owned by the Republic
of Latvia.

SCOPE OF
CONSOLIDATION
This is the consolidated sustainability report of the
Augstsprieguma tīkls Group, which, like the consolidated
financial report of the Augstsprieguma tīkls Group,
covers the Parent Company and commercial companies
in which the Parent Company has a decisive (more
than 50%) influence. Based on the aforementioned
principles, the consolidated sustainability report, like the
financial report, covers AS Augstsprieguma tīkls and its
Subsidiary AS Conexus Baltic Grid.
In line with the financial report of the Augstsprieguma
tīkls Group, the reporting period of the consolidated
sustainability report corresponds to the financial year
from 1 January 2025 to 31 December 2025.
The consolidated sustainability report is prepared once
a year and published on the last working day of April
of each year. The report for 2025 was published on the
Group’s website on 24 April 2026: www.ast.lv.

The Parent Company’s investment in the Subsidiary
accounts for 68.46%. 29.06% of Conexus shares are
owned by the Japanese company Marubeni Fund, MM
Infrastructure Investments Europe Limited, and 2.48% by
other shareholders.

7


=== pdf p.8 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

SCOPE OF DISCLOSURE
OF INFORMATION ABOUT
THE VALUE CHAIN
The Group’s operations are not only important for
its own operations, but also for the upstream and
downstream value chain, given that the Group’s
companies are only part of the wider energy market.
The dual materiality assessment underlying the
Sustainability Report captures and considers impacts,
risks and opportunities that arise or could arise not only
in the Group’s operations but also in its value chain.
More detailed information is available in the section on
"Identification and assessment of material impacts, risks
and opportunities".
Data related to the value chain are discussed in the
following sections in particular:
• Climate change – the calculation of indirect emissions
mainly includes upstream value chain data, but in
relation to the downstream value chain, the Group’s
contribution to the transition of the Latvian energy
supply system to the production and use of renewable
resources, as well as ensuring the security, stability
and reliability of energy supply, is detailed;
• Affected communities – this section provides
information on the impact on indirect value chain
participants (broader society or individual groups

thereof, which are also end users of the Group’s
companies’ services), as well as measures taken by
the Group to mitigate the negative impact on these
groups and expand the positive impact;
• Business activities – this section provides
information on cooperation with suppliers and other
counterparties, mainly covering information related to
the upstream value chain. Suppliers play a significant
role in the implementation of the Group’s strategic
projects. Therefore, the sustainability report reflects
information on the upstream value chain, in particular
describing procurement processes, sustainability
criteria set for suppliers, and the impact of suppliers
on achieving the Group’s goals.
Indirect aspects of the value chain are discussed
in the sections on biodiversity and circular economy.
In regard to the management of the working
environment, the information provided mainly reflects
the Group’s own operations.
The Group’s policies, activities and targets in the
aforementioned areas are closely integrated with
the value chain, and in accordance with the most
significant aspects, this information is disclosed in
conformity with the requirements of the thematic
standards of the European Sustainability Reporting
Standards (hereinafter also referred to as "ESRS").
Where necessary and possible, the indicators disclosed
include data on the value chain. At the same time, the
Group continues to improve data collection processes,
gradually improving the accumulation and presentation
of data related to the value chain in the future.

LIMITED ASSURANCE
REVIEW
The independent auditor’s limited assurance report
on the 2025 Sustainability Report has been provided
by SIA KPMG Baltics, which is also the auditor of
AS Augstsprieguma tīkls 2025 financial statements,
as well as the auditor of the Subsidiary's financial
statements. The independent auditor's limited assurance
report is available on page 165. of this report.

The quality of information and data has been gradually
improved in the 2025 Report, improving the structure
of the report, the comprehensibility of the contents,
the detailing of the information provided and the
methodological approach to data acquisition,
processing and presentation.
All indicators and information disclosed in the report
have been prepared to ensure their accuracy and
reliability. Comparative data is presented for a twoyear period, i.e. 2024 and 2023. In certain data items,

data for 2023 is not available and will not be presented
when preparing the 2025 sustainability report. Data
comparability over a three-year period will be fully

Unless otherwise specified in the content of the report,
the Group uses the time frames specified by the ESRS
for the presentation of information and data:
• Short term – within 1 year;

The measurements of the quantitative indicators
included in the report have not been confirmed other
than courtesy of an independent auditor’s limited
assurance report on the sustainability report as a
whole. The Group’s GHG emissions calculation
includes the Subsidiary’s indicators for natural gas from
the Subsidiary’s GHG emissions report (emissions
related only to natural gas storage), which is subject to
annual external verification and approved by the State
Environmental Service.
The reliability of the data is facilitated by the fact that
AST has developed, implemented and maintains
the company’s management system and processes
in accordance with the standards ISO 9001:2015
(Quality Management Systems), ISO 14001:2015
(Environmental Management Systems), ISO 45001:2018
(Occupational Health and Safety Management Systems)
and ISO 50001:2018 (Energy Management Systems).
Conexus has developed, implemented and maintains
an integrated management system for the company
in accordance with ISO 14001:2015 (Environmental
Management Systems), ISO 45001:2028 (Occupational
Health and Safety Management Systems) and ISO
50001:2028 (Energy Management Systems).

• Medium term – 2-5 years;

In preparing the 2025 Sustainability Report, the
Group exercises the option not to disclose or only
partially disclose financial impact indicators related to
sustainability areas, as provided for in Commission
Delegated Regulation (EU) 2025/1416 the specified
exemption (the "quick fix" decision on the application
of ESRS standards).

• Long term – more than 5 years.
The contents of the report contain references to the
Group’s Medium-Term Strategy, which covers a fixed
five-year period (the Group’s Medium-Term Strategy for
2021-2025 applies to this report).
Quantitative information related to the value chain is
mostly included in the sections on Climate change and
Business activities. In both sections, the information
used is available in the Group’s accounting systems and
data on the volume, number of purchases, organised
business trips, etc. Direct information from suppliers on
the amount of GHG emissions from their products or
services is not requested.
The Group’s Sustainability Report does not include
classified, sensitive, specific knowledge and innovationrelated information, the disclosure of which is either
limited by the Group’s obligations in managing critical
infrastructure or is classified as a trade secret. The
non‑inclusion of such information does not affect the
compliance of the sustainability report content with
ESRS requirements.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

BUSINESS MODEL
AND STRATEGY
ESRS SBM-1

The Group is one of the largest energy supply
undertakings in the Baltics. Its core business is
comprised of fulfilling the functions of the electricity
transmission system operator and ensuring the efficient
management of energy supply system assets, and the
transmission and storage of natural gas.
As at 31 December 2025, the structure of the Group
consisted of a group of commercial companies in which
the Parent Company AS Augstsprieguma tīkls exercises
decisive influence, and which includes the Subsidiary
AS Conexus Baltic Grid. All (100%) shares of AS
Augstsprieguma tīkls are owned by the State, and the
shareholder is the Ministry of Climate and Energy of the
Republic of Latvia.

to the Group are located elsewhere in Riga and
throughout Latvia.
The Parent Company is responsible for the backbone
of Latvia’s electricity system — the transmission
network, which consists of 330 kV and 110 kV electricity
transmission lines and substations required for the
further transmission of electricity to transmission system
users, i.e. large individual consumers, producers and
distribution network operators. The transmission network
connects Latvia’s power plants to the energy systems
of neighbouring countries and with distribution network
companies, thereby ensuring that consumers receive
the required volume of electricity in real time, as well
as enabling producers to export and traders to import
electricity from neighbouring countries.

In geographical terms, the Group operates within
the territory of Latvia. The registered office of the
Augstsprieguma tīkls Group is at 86 Dārzciema iela,
Riga, LV-1073. Structural units of companies belonging

=== pdf p.11 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

The Subsidiary’s gas transmission pipelines consist of
regional gas pipelines, intended for supplying Latvia,
and international gas pipelines, which ensure gas transit
to neighbouring countries, as well as their branches.
The total length of the gas transmission pipelines,
including their branches, is 1,190 km. Latvia’s natural
gas transmission system is directly connected to the
natural gas transmission systems of Lithuania and
Estonia, ensuring both the transmission of natural
gas through pipelines within the territory of Latvia and
interconnections with the natural gas transmission
systems of neighbouring countries.
In 2025, the Baltic electricity systems regional
coordination centre Baltic RCC OÜ (hereinafter
also referred to as Baltic RCC), founded by
AS Augstsprieguma tīkls together with the electricity
transmission system operators of Estonia and Lithuania
and registered in Estonia, continued on its operations.
The governance of the regional coordination centre is
ensured in accordance with the principle of equality
among all three Baltic States, i.e. each operator holds
an equal number of shares in the new structure, with
AS Augstsprieguma tīkls holding a 33.33% ownership
stake. The main task of Baltic RCC is to coordinate the
planning of the development of electricity systems and to
align certain daily operational activities of the operators
in order to guarantee the security of electricity supply.
The activities of Baltic RCC are not included within the
scope of the Consolidated Sustainability Report of the
Augstsprieguma tīkls Group.

The Group’s core operations are divided into three
segments: electricity transmission, natural gas
transmission, and natural gas storage. This division is
based on the Group’s internal organisational structure,
which ensures effective monitoring and control of the
operating results of the segments.

On 8 August 2025, the Letter of Expectations of the

tīkls Group is to ensure the security of Latvia’s energy
supply, provide continuous, high-quality and accessible
energy transmission services, and ensure the
sustainable management of energy supply assets that
are strategically important to the state, while facilitating
their integration into the European Union’s internal
energy market.

OPERATING SEGMENTS

ELECTRICITY
TRANSMISSION SEGMENT

The AST is responsible for ensuring:

In accordance with the issued Licence No. E13001 and
Section 11, Paragraph one of the Electricity Market Law,
the joint stock company Augstsprieguma tīkls is the sole
electricity transmission system operator (hereinafter
also referred to as "the TSO") in Latvia, and the area
of operation of its licence covers the entire territory of
Latvia. AS Augstsprieguma tīkls ensures continuous and
high-quality electricity transmission services, carries
out the sustainable and efficient management and
development of energy supply assets, and facilitates
integration into the European Union internal market.
In accordance with Section 5 of the Energy Law,
electricity transmission is a regulated sector.
The Parent Company carries out its activities within one
main operating segment — electricity transmission.
AST is responsible for the backbone of Latvia’s
electricity system — the transmission network, which
includes interconnected networks and equipment,
including cross-border interconnections, with a voltage
of 110 kilovolts and above, and which are used for
transmission to the respective distribution system or
to users. The Parent Company ensures the operation,
maintenance and repair of high-voltage electricity
transmission lines (hereinafter also referred to as

=== pdf p.14 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

In 2025, 5,798 gigawatt-hours (GWh) of electricity were
generated in Latvia, which is 2% less than in 2024,
when 5,906 GWh were produced. In 2025, electricity
generation at solar power plants continued to increase,
rising by 70% compared with the previous year to 678
GWh. As a result, the volume of electricity generated by
solar power plants maintained the position attained in
the previous year, ranking third after hydroelectric power
plants (HPPs) and natural gas power plants, accounting
for 11.7% of total electricity generation, an increase of
5 per cent compared with 2024. At the same time, the
volume of electricity generated from other production
sources decreased. The most significant decline was
observed in wind power plants, where generation fell by
27%, while electricity produced at biogas plants declined
by 24%. Electricity generation at natural gas and
biomass power plants was 2% lower than in 2024, while
generation at hydroelectric power plants fell by 8%. The
decrease in electricity generation at HPPs is explained
by the flood season—in the past year, there was no
widespread flooding.

On 1 October 2025, the entire European electricity
market transitioned to a 15-minute trading period in
the day-ahead electricity market, enabling market
participants to forecast and plan their production and
consumption much more accurately and to leverage
market opportunities to a greater extent.

Electricity consumption in Latvia in 2025, compared
with 2024, increased by 3%, from 6,980 GWh to 7,201
GWh. It should be noted that, considering the installation
of solar generation equipment for self-consumption
in households and businesses, the actual volume of
electricity generated and consumed in Latvia in 2025
was higher, as part of the electricity produced is not
supplied to the grid but used immediately for selfconsumption.

Synchronisation of the Baltic States
with Continental Europe

In 2025, the average electricity price in Latvia decreased
by 2% compared with 2024, reaching EUR 85.72 per
megawatt-hour (EUR/MWh).

In 2025, the Parent Company ensured the transmission
of 6,118 GWh of electricity to users in Latvia, which is
1.7% more than in 2024 (6,015 GWh). With electricity
transmission tariffs remaining stable, revenues from
transmission services increased by 0.6% in 2025
compared with 2024 (EUR 94.2 million in 2025; EUR
93.7 million in 2024).
Detailed annual electricity market reviews are available
on the AST website: www.ast.lv.

DEVELOPMENT

At 2:05 PM on 9 February 2025, Estonia, Latvia, and
Lithuania successfully synchronised their electricity
systems with continental Europe. This is a significant
historical step for the Baltic States and for Europe as a
whole, strengthening the region’s energy resilience
and independence.
Synchronisation enables the Baltic States, in close
cooperation with other continental European countries,
to manage their electricity networks, ensuring stable and

reliable frequency control, thereby significantly improving
regional energy security. Previously, the Baltic States
relied on Russia’s system for frequency management,
but they have now joined the European network,
which serves more than 400 million consumers.
All interconnection lines with Russia and Belarus
have been disconnected irreversibly.
This process is the result of several years of careful
preparation and cooperation between the Baltic
transmission system operators — AS Elering (Estonia),
AS Augstsprieguma tīkls (AST, Latvia), and AS Litgrid
(Lithuania) — and their partners in the European
synchronous area. To achieve this result, significant
investments were made in the development and
reinforcement of infrastructure in the Baltic States
and Poland.
In 2025, with financial support from the European
Commission, and in addition to the investments
made previously, AST continued and completed
the implementation of significant synchronisation
infrastructure and IT projects:
• Delivery and installation of three synchronous
compensators at Grobiņa, Ventspils, and Līksna
substations;
• Implementation of high-capacity electricity storage
systems at Rēzekne and Tume substations;
• Implementation of active booking solutions for
dispatch control systems;
• Arranging international commercial accounts, etc.

As the final step in establishing independent
synchronous operation with the continental European
electricity transmission system, at the end of 2025,
the Synchronous Area Framework Agreement for
Regional Group Continental Europe was concluded
with the transmission system operators of the Regional
Group Continental Europe and the European Network
of Transmission System Operators for Electricity
(ENTSO-E).

Development of the electricity
transmission system
To ensure the efficient development of the transmission
system and the secure provision of electricity
transmission system services, each year AST prepares
an electricity transmission system development plan
for the next ten years (hereinafter also referred to
as "the Development Plan"). In accordance with the
"Regulations on the Electricity Transmission System
Development Plan" approved by the Public Utilities
Commission (hereinafter also referred to as "PUC"),
the Development Plan prepared by AST is submitted
for approval to PUC every two years.
Detailed information on the Development Plan for
2025–2034, approved by the PUC, (the PUC Council
of 31 October 2024 Decision No. 79) is available on
the AST website (see Development Plan). In 2026, it is
planned to submit the Development Plan for 2027–2036
to PUC for approval.
The Development Plan for 2026–2035, approved
by the AST Management Board, has been prepared
in accordance with AST’s strategic objective of
14


=== pdf p.15 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

strengthening Latvia’s energy security, ensuring
compliance with the principles of security and cost
efficiency. The Plan provides for the following measures:
• Protection of critical synchronisation infrastructure,
which will require significant investments in the
protection of the established infrastructure and the
ability to quickly restore it;
• Moving towards the digitalisation of the electricity
system, including the introduction of integrated
balance management (BMS) and network model
management (NMM) information systems, as well
as cybersecurity and IT infrastructure system
improvement solutions;
• Green transformation, including the transition from
SF6 equipment to more environmentally friendly
equipment, as well as the integration of new
renewable energy producers into the electricity
transmission system, by building more than ten new
facilities for connecting such power plants;
• Maintenance and development of the operational
capacity of the existing infrastructure, ensuring
efficient and high-quality electricity transmission
system services at the lowest possible service tariffs,
including rebuilding 24 110kV substations, replacing
seven autotransformers and 49 transformers,
installing 575 new 330kV power transmission
line supports and 2061 new 110kV power
transmission line supports, and conducting
other requisite measures.

The approved Development Plan for 2026–2035
defines the development of the transmission system
and the required financial investments in transmission
infrastructure for the next ten years, providing for EUR
908 million to be invested in the development of the
electricity transmission system.

Strategically important interconnections
Alongside the projects for the renewal of the existing
transmission network, AST continues to assess and
develop strategically important interconnection projects,
including the fourth Latvia–Estonia interconnection, a
new interconnection with Lithuania (the 330 kV electricity
transmission line Ventspils – Brocēni – Varduva), as well
as the Latvia–Sweden LaSGo Link interconnection and
the Baltic–Germany BalticHub interconnection, which
could significantly influence the connectivity of Latvia’s
electricity system and its regional role.
The implementation of these projects will increase
the security and stability of Latvia’s electricity supply,
strengthen the energy independence of the Baltic
States, and enable the connection of a larger volume of
renewable energy resources to the grid.
The advancement of these projects is closely linked to:

On 2 April 2025, a call was made for energy
infrastructure projects under the Connecting Europe
Facility (CEF). Within this call, in September 2025,
the Parent Company submitted two applications:
1. The Baltic synchronisation critical infrastructure
project (CIP – Critical Infrastructure Protection), which
is being prepared jointly with the Baltic and Polish
transmission system operators (TSOs);
2. An application for the environmental impact
assessment (EIA) and route study of the fourth
Estonia–Latvia interconnection, which is being
prepared jointly with the Estonian TSO AS Elering.
It is anticipated that these activities could receive
European Union CEF co-financing of up to 50% of the
eligible costs.

Renewable electricity generation –
connections to the transmission system
The development of renewable energy power plants
in Latvia is an important step towards addressing the
current energy, security, and climate challenges in
the Baltic region. In 2025, strong interest in electricity
generation using renewable energy resources continued
in Latvia.

• Political and strategic decisions by several countries,
• Common European energy strategy and market
readiness,

Considering that the planned new capacities to be
introduced had reached the technical limit of the
electricity transmission system (6 GW), amendments to
the Electricity Market Law were adopted in 2025, which:

• Availability of EU support instruments.
• allow the introduction of a "flexible transmission

system service" — a transmission system service
provided to an electricity producer with the possibility
of limiting it for up to 876 hours within one calendar
year. Accordingly, by combining such limitations with
the alignment of electricity generation profiles, it has
become possible to connect an additional 5.8 GW to
the electricity transmission system;
• provide the possibility for projects whose
implementation is not progressing as planned to
withdraw from their implementation, thereby making
room for other developers. This opportunity was
taken up by 12 project developers, with a total
planned capacity of 2 GW.
In 2025, 30 agreements were concluded for the
establishment of new connections or for changes to the
technical parameters of existing connections, providing
for the connection of 2.9 GW of renewable energy
capacity to the electricity transmission system: 0.6 GW
of wind power plants (WPPs), 0.8 GW of solar power
plants (SPPs), and 1.6 GW of power plants using
various technologies.

Development of the Baltic electricity market
and balancing mechanisms
Following the synchronisation of the Baltic electricity
transmission systems with the European power system,
together with AS Elering of Estonia and AS Litgrid
of Lithuania (jointly "the Baltic transmission system
operators"), AST has assumed a new responsibility — to
ensure frequency regulation and the continuous balance
between electricity consumption and generation in the
Baltic region.
15


=== pdf p.16 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

These changes, as well as the rapid increase in
connections of wind and solar power plants, significantly
increase the need for balancing reserves, which ensure
the stable operation of the power system.

Creation of a regional balancing market
To ensure the secure and efficient operation of
the system, AST is one of the main initiators and
implementers of the Baltic balancing capacity market,
which started operating on 4 February 2025.
The new market enables AST, together with its Baltic
partners, to procure and coordinate the balancing
capacity reserves required for system operations, in
compliance with the European balancing guidelines,
and by using a cross-border transmission capacity
optimisation algorithm developed by the Baltic
transmission system operators.
In 2025, the total demand for balancing capacity in the
Baltics reaches approximately 1,500 MW, and AST
forecasts that this volume will increase as the share of
renewable energy grows and new generating consumers
enter the market.

Participation in European
balancing platforms
To strengthen the integration of the region into the
European energy system, AST has introduced the
automatic frequency restoration reserves market and,
together with its Baltic partners, joined the European
PICASSO platform (Platform for the International
Coordination of Automated Frequency Restoration and

Stable System Operation) in April 2025.
Participation in the PICASSO platform opens up new
opportunities for electricity producers, consumers, and
large-scale battery operators in Latvia, enabling them to
offer balancing services and earn additional revenues.
At the same time, AST will gain broader access to
balancing resources and the possibility to use imbalance
netting, which will reduce the total volume of reserves to
be activated and foster system efficiency.
Joining the PICASSO platform was the next step in the
development of the Baltic balancing market, following
the accession to MARI (Manually Activated Reserves
Initiative) and the establishment of the Baltic balancing
market. Accordingly, AST promotes the broader
development of the balancing services market and
facilitates new investments in reserve infrastructure.
Interest in participating in these markets continues
to grow, confirming the importance of the initiatives
promoted by AST in strengthening the resilience of the
regional energy system.

Transition to a 15-minute
market model
To ensure the broad development of renewable energy
generation and introduce greater competition, both
by expanding the market geographically and by
introducing new types of services, for several years
now, European transmission system operators and
electricity exchanges have been working on improving
the electricity market model.

Since the liberalisation of the electricity market, which
began in Europe 30 years ago, electricity wholesale and
retail trading, with prices linked to the variable exchange
price, has taken place in 60-minute periods. To date
electricity meters have also recorded consumption in
60-minute intervals, enabling consumers and producers
to settle according to the prices set on the electricity
exchange for each hour of the day.

The electricity market model is the main tool by which
the transmission system operator coordinates the
operation of the energy system. AST has played a
leading role in the transition to a 15-minute trading and
imbalance settlement period across the Baltics. The
transition to 15-minute trading is an important step
towards a single European energy market with more
efficient coordination of cross-border electricity flows.

In order to increase the precision and granularity of
trading, a transition from 60-minute trading to 15-minute
trading is being introduced both in electricity exchanges
and balancing markets. As part of this process, the next
step — the transition to a 15-minute trading period in
the day-ahead electricity market across Europe — took
place on 30 September 2025, with the first electricity
delivery day under the new regime following on
1 October.

Strengthening competition and
market openness

Along with the changes in the day-ahead electricity
market, a 15-minute trading period is also being
introduced in the Baltic balancing capacity market.
This change will allow the Baltic transmission system
operators to plan and procure balancing capacity
reserves more accurately, as well as to reserve
interconnection capacities required for balancing the
electricity system.
For participants in the balancing capacity market,
particularly those whose portfolios include renewable
energy resources or highly flexible resources, this will
provide the opportunity to offer capacity availability for
shorter periods and to plan capacity provision periods
more precisely.

Together with partners, Baltic electricity transmission
system operators (TSOs), including AST, have
implemented a solution that allows multiple electricity
exchanges to operate in the market (Multi NEMO
Arrangement or MNA model). Competition between
electricity exchanges will bring benefits to market
participants, providing wider choice and better services.
From 25 November, a new operator, EPEX SPOT,
started operating in the intraday market in Estonia,
Latvia, and Lithuania. Until now, Nord Pool was the
sole nominated electricity market operator in the Baltic
electricity market.
The entry of the second exchange does not affect the
general principles of connecting European markets –
the determination of cross-border transmission capacity
and the network congestion management process in the
Baltic countries remain unchanged. Electricity market
pricing principles also remain unchanged, with trading
offers submitted on both electricity exchanges collected
and used centrally.

16


=== pdf p.17 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

In 2026, the Baltic TSOs and market operators will
complete the implementation of the solution so that
multiple exchanges can also operate in day-ahead and
intraday auctions. The solution of parallel operation
of competing power exchanges is implemented
in accordance with the EU guidelines on capacity
calculation and congestion management, which aim to
foster competition between power exchanges in central
European markets, moving towards a fully integrated
and competitive European electricity market. Such
solutions have already been implemented in several
Central and Western European countries, as well as in
the Nordic countries.

Harmonisation and future projects
AST participates in the development of a common
European balancing capacity procurement algorithm,
which envisages uniform, transparent and coordinated
procurements across Europe.
In light of the accelerating development of solar and wind
power plants, while their output depends on weather
conditions and given that the accuracy of weather
forecasts is higher in the short term, it is necessary to
ensure that electricity can be bought and sold closer to
the time of delivery in the electricity market. Therefore,
as part of the continued development of the electricity
market, the possibility to buy and sell electricity up to 30
minutes before delivery will be introduced across Europe.
European transmission system operators will gradually
implement this solution by 2029. The transmission
system operators of Latvia and the other Baltic States will
introduce this solution at the Latvia–Estonia border and
the Latvia–Lithuania border in 2026.

These changes are part of a broader reform of the
electricity market design, the aim of which is to make the
market more efficient, more flexible and better adapted
to changing production and consumption conditions
across Europe. By shortening the intraday market gate
closure time from 60 to 30 minutes before the start of
electricity delivery, market participants are provided
with the opportunity to respond closer to real time. This
improves market flexibility and fosters more accurate
price formation, with transactions reflect the current
production and consumption situation more accurately.
These changes are particularly important for the
integration of weather-dependent renewable energy
sources — wind and solar electricity. The possibility to
trade closer to the delivery time reduces forecasting
errors, allowing producers and consumers to adjust their
positions more accurately to actual conditions. As a
result, there is a decrease in the need for balancing and
the associated system costs, while at the same time the
electricity system is rendered more efficient and secure.
To ensure the introduction of the 30-minute cross-zonal
gate closure time, AST and other European transmission
system operators have carried out significant
improvements to their operational and control systems,
working in close collaboration at cross-border level.

AST’s contribution to a sustainable
energy future
All the aforementioned initiatives demonstrate AST’s
strategic role in the transformation of the Baltic
electricity market and the integration of the
European energy system.

The transition to modern balancing and market
mechanisms led by the Company:

green transition (some of which will continue in 2026),
including:

• Fosters the integration of renewable energy,
• Improves system flexibility and security,
• Increases market efficiency and transparency,
• Creates new economic opportunities for market
participants.

• Environmental impact assessment for the new 330
kV Ventspils–Brocēni–Varduva line, which is being
carried out with RePowerEU European funding;

Thus, through its initiatives, AS Augstsprieguma tīkls is
not only reinforcing the security of the Latvian and Baltic
energy systems, but also fostering a sustainable and
competitive electricity market at European level.

Innovation and research
To ensure the development of the Parent Company,
and recognising the essential role of research and
innovation in ensuring successful operations, AST
actively conducts research necessary for the Latvian
electricity system, including participation in the work of
the System Development Committee and the Research,
Development and Innovation Committee of the
European Network of Transmission System Operators
for Electricity (ENTSO-E).
Under the aegis of the ENTSO-E Research,
Development and Innovation Committee, activities
are carried out aimed at transforming the existing
electricity systems across Europe in order to achieve the
objectives set by the European Union, primarily in the
areas of flexibility, digitalisation and the green transition.
In the field of research and innovation, in 2025,
several studies were conducted related to network
modernisation, security and efficiency, as well as the

• In-depth research was jointly conducted with the
Lithuanian TSO into the development opportunities
and necessary projects of the Latvian–Lithuanian
electricity transmission network, as well as into the
possible use of innovations in modernisation projects;
• Research was initiated together with the Estonian
TSO Elering to select the technological solution for
the 4th Estonian–Latvian interconnection;
• Research work continues into the development of the
Baltic–German project, performing calculations of the
benefits of the interconnection.

NATURAL GAS
TRANSMISSION AND
STORAGE SEGMENTS
The Group’s Subsidiary is an independent combined
natural gas transmission and storage system operator
in Latvia, which manages one of the most modern
natural gas storage facilities in Europe — the Inčukalns
17


=== pdf p.18 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

Underground Gas Storage facility (hereinafter referred
to as "Inčukalns UGS" or "the storage facility") and the
main natural gas transmission system, which connects
the Latvian natural gas market to Lithuania and Estonia.

NATURAL GAS TRANSMISSION SEGMENT
The Group’s Subsidiary ensures the maintenance of the
natural gas transmission system, its safe and continuous
operation, and interconnections with the natural gas
transmission systems of other countries, enabling
system users to utilise the natural gas transmission
system for natural gas trading. Since the spring of 2024,
biomethane produced in Latvia has also been injected
into the Latvian gas supply system.
As the natural gas transmission system operator,
the Subsidiary:
• ensures the stability of the transmission network
and is responsible for technical balancing, thereby
enabling continuous and secure natural gas supply
to system users;
• ensures the continuity of natural gas transmission
system services by maintaining the infrastructure in
proper technical condition;
• in accordance with the conditions of the liberalised
Latvian natural gas market, ensures nondiscriminatory access for system users to the gas
transmission system by offering transmission capacity
products, enabling market participants to utilise

the capacity of the main transmission network for
wholesale trading and the supply of natural gas to
customers;
• together with the Estonian transmission system
operator Elering, maintains a virtual trading point
(VTP) within the unified Latvian–Estonian natural gas
transmission system balancing zone, enabling system
users to exchange gas within the system
on a commercial basis;
• since spring 2024, domestically produced biomethane
has also been injected into the Latvian natural gas
supply system. Since 1 July 2023, biomethane
producers have been able to obtain gas guarantees
of origin.
In 2025, the total volume of natural gas transmitted
reached 25.5 TWh, which represents an increase of
1% compared with the previous year (25.2 TWh).
The volume of gas consumed for the needs of Latvian
users (the volume of natural gas injected into the natural
gas distribution system) decreased by approximately 1%,
amounting to 8.7 TWh (8.8 TWh in 2024). In 2025,
0.17 TWh of biomethane, produced in Latvia,
was injected into the Latvian gas supply system,
approximately 143% more than in the previous year,
when 0.07 TWh of biomethane was injected.

DEVELOPMENT
On 9 October 2025, the PUC approved the natural gas
transmission system development plan for the period

2026–2035, prepared by the Subsidiary. The objective
of the Conexus’ Development Plan is to ensure the
sustainable and secure operation and development of
infrastructure by investing in the renewal of the existing
natural gas transmission system and the creation of new
transmission system elements, as well as to promote
the development of the renewable and low-carbon gas
market. The Development Plan provides for investments
of approximately EUR 138 million during the period from
2026 to 2035.
As part of the international project for the development
of the Nordic–Baltic Hydrogen Corridor, during the
reporting period, the European gas transmission system
operators Conexus, Gasgrid vetyverkot (a Subsidiary
of Gasgrid Finland, Finland), Elering(Estonia), Amber
Grid (Lithuania), GAZ-SYSTEM (Poland) and ONTRAS
(Germany) concluded a co-financing agreement with
the European Climate, Infrastructure and Environment
Executive Agency (CINEA) for a feasibility study of the
Nordic–Baltic Hydrogen Corridor.
The study will enable the partners of the Nordic–
Baltic Hydrogen Corridor project to carry out in-depth
technical, economic, regulatory and environmental
analyses, assessing the possibilities for developing a
large-scale hydrogen pipeline network in the Baltic Sea
region, which will play a decisive role in achieving the
decarbonisation objectives of the European Union.
Considering the significant potential in the region for
the production of renewable and low-carbon hydrogen,
export potential to Central Europe by 2040 could
reach approximately 2.7 million tonnes. The project
is an important component of Europe’s transition to a

sustainable and decarbonised energy system.

Biomethane development
To foster biomethane production in Latvia, during
the reporting period Conexus implemented the
project "7.1.1.3.i Increasing the share of biomethane
in final consumption" and commissioned the first
publicly accessible biomethane injection point (BIP)
at Džūkste — a solution that enables the injection of
biomethane into the natural gas transmission system
for producers whose existing or planned biomethane
plants are located quite far from the existing natural gas
transmission infrastructure.
The project was implemented with funding from the
European Union Recovery and Resilience Facility.
As a result of the project, biomethane transported in
compressed form from production facilities to the BIP
using container transport is injected into the natural gas
transmission system. BIP Džūkste has the capacity to
inject up to 10 million normal cubic metres (Nm³), or
100 GWh of biomethane per year.
The Natural Gas Transmission System Development
Plan for 2026–2035 provides for the continued
development of biomethane injection points (BIPs)
in other locations across the territory of Latvia in
order to ensure access to the transmission system in
different regions of the country and to expand access
opportunities for small and medium-sized biomethane
producers. It is planned to develop such points in
Rēzekne and Ragana, and potentially also at Skrunda.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT
Direct biomethane input connections
to the transmission and
distribution system
In 2025, nine technical specifications were issued for
new direct connections: five connections to the gas
transmission system and four connections to the gas
distribution system.
A growing market interest can be observed specifically
in the biomethane sector — potential clients are
increasingly actively assessing opportunities to
convert existing biogas plants into biomethane
production facilities with a connection to the natural gas
transmission or distribution system. This trend indicates
a gradual transition towards the use of renewable gases,
facilitating the integration of local resources and the
implementation of decarbonisation objectives.

NATURAL GAS STORAGE SEGMENT
The Subsidiary’s structure includes the Inčukalns
Underground Gas Storage facility (UGS), which ensures
the injection, storage and withdrawal of natural gas,
delivering it into the main gas pipelines.
The central area of the Inčukalns UGS and the
equipment necessary for technological processes —
three gas gathering points (hereinafter also referred
to as "GGPs") and 180 gas storage wells (control,
monitoring and 93 operational wells used for the
injection and withdrawal of natural gas) — occupy an
area of approximately 8,400 hectares. The geological
structure (reservoir layer) of the Inčukalns UGS covers

an area of approximately 25 km² and is located within
the municipalities of Sigulda, Saulkrasti and Ropaži.
In accordance with the Cabinet Regulation No. 773 of
13 December 2016 "Regulations on the Designation
of an Area of Subsoil of State Importance ‘Inčukalns
Natural Gas Storage Facility’", the area of the subsoil
site is 83.722 km².
According to the technological design of the Inčukalns
UGS, the maximum possible volume of active natural
gas storage is approximately 25 TWh.
The task of the gas storage facility is to ensure a
continuous gas supply to consumers regardless of
seasonal fluctuations in consumption, by injecting
natural gas during the summer and withdrawing it during
the winter. The Inčukalns UGS is the only operating
underground gas storage facility in the Baltic States and
has ensured stable regional gas supply since 1968.
With the opening of the natural gas market and the
development of the regional market, the role of the
Inčukalns UGS has increased, improving both supply
flexibility and security of gas supply in the region. In the
unified natural gas market, the Inčukalns UGS promotes
competition among suppliers and contributes to the
stabilisation of natural gas prices in the region.
Conexus, as the storage operator:
• ensures the continuous operation of the Inčukalns
UGS by monitoring and controlling storage stability,
inspecting the equipment, preventing damage to the
infrastructure and investing in the development and
safety of the storage facility;

• ensures fair and equal access to the Inčukalns UGS
for system users, providing them with the opportunity
to store natural gas in accordance with the Inčukalns
UGS usage rules approved by the PUC.

the dependence of the storage facility’s performance on
the volume of natural gas stored during the withdrawal
season, and reduce the environmental impact of the
storage facility’s technological processes.

The method for reserving storage capacity is an auction
procedure with a uniform premium, which ensures
transparency in storage capacity reservations in line
with market conditions and equal access to storage
services. In the 2025/2026 storage cycle, capacity of
17.4 TWh has been reserved, including 1.8 TWh of
energy supply security reserves.

Under the aegis of the project, a new compressor will be
installed, which will enable compression withdrawal from
the storage facility, meaning that it will make it possible
to ensure an outlet pressure of up to 50 bar at the
Inčukalns UGS interconnection with the transmission
system and maintain continuous gas flow even when
the pressure in the storage reservoir is lower than the
pressure in the transmission system.

The Inčukalns UGS capacity auctions for the
2025/2026 storage cycle started on 11 February 2025
and continued until 25 September. As a result of the
auctions, 9.4 TWh of capacity was reserved. In total, 24
market participants from the three Baltic States, Finland,
Norway, Germany, Poland and Switzerland participated
in the auctions and were allocated storage capacity.

DEVELOPMENT
On 23 January 2019, the European Commission
approved 50% co-financing for the modernisation of
the storage facility. The investments will significantly
strengthen the role of the Inčukalns UGS as a modern
and important natural gas infrastructure facility, not only
in Latvia but also in the Baltic region and Finland.
The objective of the Inčukalns UGS development
project is to improve the operation of the storage facility
so that it can ensure functionality after an increase of
pressure in the Baltic gas transmission system, reduce

To ensure that the wells and above-ground technological
equipment operate with sufficient capacity under the
new regime, certain upgrades are required, which form
an integral part of the project. In addition, the project will
reduce the storage facility’s environmental impact by
lowering CO2, NOx and other emissions.
The total cost of the project is EUR 99.5 million. The
following activities are planned within the project until
2026:
• improvement of surface facilities (reconstruction
of gas collection point No. 3) – EUR 30.5 million;
• renovation of 36 wells – EUR 25.5 million;
• modernisation of the existing five gas pumping units
and installation of one additional new gas pumping
unit – EUR 43.5 million.

=== pdf p.21 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

Considering the specific nature of the Group’s
operations and its significant role in ensuring Latvia’s
energy supply, the Group’s value chain is extensive and
includes a wide range of stakeholders. The upstream
value chain consists of:
• Entities determining the Group’s activities, primarily
policy-makers (e.g. the Ministry of Climate and
Energy) and regulators (e.g. the Public Utilities
Commission), who set the requirements and
criteria for the development of energy supply, the
provision of infrastructure, the involvement of other
stakeholders, the integration of energy resources into
the grid, and other related matters. This category
also includes general policy-makers who determine
the requirements for the governance of state-owned
enterprises (e.g. the State Chancellery, the Ministr
of Finance, etc.);
• Resource providers — including both suppliers of key
goods and services and providers of balancing and
regulation reserves that help to ensure the stability
and continuity of the energy system’s operation.
In 2025, AST had active contracts with 1,170
counterparties. This category includes providers of
financial resources (including investors in the Parent
Company’s Green Bonds — 36 investors), as well as
educational and research institutions that provide the
future workforce and intellectual resources necessary
for the Group’s operations. For example, the Group
cooperates with Riga Technical University, Riga State
Technical School, Riga Technical College,
and regional technical schools, such as Kandava.

The Group’s own operations consist of three core
business areas — electricity transmission, natural gas
transmission, and natural gas storage, as well as the
related processes required to support development,
infrastructure maintenance, and day-to-day operational
activities, and to establish and maintain cooperation with
participants in both the upstream and downstream value
chains. Accordingly, the Group ensures the security of
energy supply, stable delivery of energy resources, and
access to them not only in Latvia, but throughout the
Baltic Sea region.
Similarly to the upstream value chain, the Group’s
downstream value chain can also be divided into
several subcategories:
• Direct clients and cooperation partners —
considering that the Group’s companies are
infrastructure management companies, their direct
clients are primarily other companies, including
energy producers and traders in the Baltic Sea region
(the Parent Company had more than 30 such clients
in 2025), distribution system operators (in Latvia,
AS Sadales tīkls for electricity and AS Gaso for
natural gas), and certain large energy consumers
that are provided with a direct connection to the
transmission system. In 2025, the Parent Company
had six such directly connected clients,
all electricity users.
The category of counterparties also includes
transmission system operators from other countries
(given that the transmission systems of both Group
companies are interconnected with the systems of
neighbouring countries), the Nord Pool electricity

exchange, the European-level organisation of
electricity transmission system operators ENTSO-E,
as well as other cooperation partners. This category
also includes important stakeholders in the form of
various supervisory institutions that oversee and
monitor the activities of the Group’s companies, as
well as trade unions, which represent the interests
of employees working in the Group’s companies and
with which close dialogue is maintained. In the area
of employee representation, the Group’s cooperation
partners include the independent units of the Latvian
trade union Enerģija — the trade union organisations
of the employees of AS Augstsprieguma tīkls and
AS Conexus Baltic Grid;
• Indirectly, the end users of the energy system, i.e.
households and businesses that consume electricity
and natural gas or their alternatives, are also part
of the Group companies’ value chain. Although the
Group companies do not have direct interaction with
these users, their ability to access and use energy
resources depends directly on the Group’s activities
in maintaining and developing infrastructure and
coordinating system balancing;
• Finally, the downstream value chain also includes
activities related to the end-of-life stage of the Group
companies’ operations — primarily such that are
related to the management of generated waste
and other end products of infrastructure operations
(including emissions management, wastewater
management, and others).

THE GROUP’S STRATEGY
The overall strategic objective of the Augstsprieguma
tīkls Group, in accordance with Cabinet Order No. 309
of 27 May 2025, is:
To ensure the security of Latvia’s energy supply,
provide continuous, high-quality and accessible
energy transmission services, as well as ensure the
sustainable management of energy supply assets
that are strategically important to the State and
promote their integration into the European Union’s
internal energy market.
In order to achieve the Group’s strategic objective —
to build a sustainable, secure and future-oriented energy
system in Latvia — the contribution of each company
within the Group is essential. As owners and developers
of nationally significant infrastructure, AST and Conexus
perform distinct but complementary functions in the
electricity and natural gas sectors. Each company has
defined its mission, vision and values, which reflect its
role in the energy system and strengthen the Group’s
overall direction towards achieving its strategic objective.

=== pdf p.22 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT
THE GROUP’S FOCUS IS
ON CREATING LONG-TERM
VALUE IN LATVIA
To promote the country’s
economic growth
As a manager of energy infrastructure, the AST Group
is one of the cornerstones of the national economy,
ensuring the secure, efficient and sustainable
transmission of electricity and natural gas, as well as
gas storage. The transmission and storage infrastructure
forms a stable foundation for economic growth, securing
investments, and the transformation of the energy
sector. The development of networks and the availability
of capacity are essential for ensuring a competitive and
export-oriented economy.

Facilitate the development of
the energy sector and the transition
to climate neutrality
The Group actively facilitates the integration of
renewable energy sources (RES) into transmission
networks and develops infrastructure for the use of
biomethane and, in future, hydrogen. Accordingly,
Latvia’s energy independence is strengthened, carbon
emissions are reduced, and regional market integration
is supported. The Group provides infrastructure that
serves as a platform for innovation, new technologies,
and sustainable business opportunities in the energy
sector.
A stable and secure electricity and natural gas
transmission system, as well as reliable storage

capacity, is a prerequisite for economic development
and societal well-being. The AST Group continuously
modernises its networks and storage infrastructure,
introducing digital solutions, such as smart system
management technologies and automation. This enables
adaptation to changing conditions in energy production
and consumption, strengthens the resilience of energy
supply, and improves system efficiency.

SDGs have been identified as high-priority and aligned
with the Group’s core activities, while through its
activities, the Group also contributes to other SDGs.

These priorities reflect the Group’s role as a strategic
partner in the development of Latvia’s energy sector
— building secure, modern and climate-neutral energy
infrastructure that supports economic growth and
sustainability.
In 2021, the Medium-Term Operational Strategy of the
Augstsprieguma tīkls Group for 2021–2025 (hereinafter
also referred to as "the Strategy") was developed and
approved. The Strategy defines the Group’s strategic
direction and sets financial and non-financial objectives
for the Parent Company and the Subsidiary for the
following five years.
The defined objectives, projects already initiated, and
directions of action also outline the framework for future
objectives — moving towards a climate-neutral economy
within the framework of the European Green Deal and
achieving the objectives set out in the Latvia National
Energy and Climate Plan for 2021–2030.
Recognising its role and contribution to sustainable
development, the Group strives in its operations for
processes, products and services that contribute to
achieving the United Nations Sustainable Development
Goals (hereinafter also referred to as "SDGs"). Three

THE PARENT COMPANY’S
STRATEGY
The central element of the Parent Company’s strategy
remains the Digital and Green Transformation,
aimed at improving operational efficiency, supporting
development, and reducing environmental impact. These
principles are integrated into all company processes,
with particular attention paid to the assessment of
environmental impacts and sustainable development.
A key priority for AST is the implementation of the
largest energy development project in the Baltics —
synchronisation with continental Europe. As a result,
the energy independence of the Baltic States has been
strengthened, and a competitive ancillary services
market is emerging.

=== pdf p.27 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

THE SUBSIDIARY’S
STRATEGY
In accordance with the main objective of the European
Union’s Green Deal, climate neutrality, in the form of an
economy with net-zero greenhouse gas emissions, must
be achieved by 2050. All parts of society and economic
sectors have a role to play, including the gas sector,
whose objective is to gradually reduce the use of natural
gas to an extent that does not endanger the region’s
competitiveness, while ensuring maximum progress
towards the decarbonisation of the gas market. The gas
transmission system and the related infrastructure will be
an important factor in achieving climate neutrality.
Russia’s invasion of Ukraine completely transformed the
European natural gas sector, including in Latvia, which
banned the import of Russian natural gas for national
needs from 1 January 2023. These circumstances
created new challenges related to sources of natural gas
imports and business processes, with security of supply
becoming the key dimension of the natural gas sector at
both national and regional levels.
The objectives to be achieved by Conexus derive both
from the Group’s overall strategic objective, from energy
policy planning documents, and from the Group’s
medium-term operational strategy for 2021–2025.
In light of the significant developments in the natural gas
sector, the Subsidiary updated its strategic directions
and priorities in 2022. For the strategic planning period
2023–2027, Conexus has identified strategic objectives
in three areas:

MARKET DEVELOPMENT:

SECURITY:

SUSTAINABILITY:

Promote the development and further
integration of the gas market, while
fostering the growth of hydrogen and
other gaseous energy carrier markets.

Ensuring the availability of safe
infrastructure while simultaneously
promoting options for adapting systems
to enable gas injection

Focus on climate and environment
sustainability aspects

• Promote regional market integration.

• Implement joint interest projects.

Fokusing on sustainability, Conexus will pay
main attention to the environmental aspects:

• Foster cooperation with regional TSOs
to develop a common position on the
integration of biogas and hydrogen into
transmission networks, and to support
biomethane injection into the transmission
system.

• Undertake R&D initiatives to assess
technical feasibility and investment
requirements for adapting existing
infrastructure to accommodate natural
gas/hydrogen blends or pure hydrogen,
including the construction of dedicated
hydrogen infrastructure.

• Continue the development of Inčukalns
UGS services, ensuring increased
operational flexibility, including the
possibility of compression removal.

• Asset management addressing
future challenges

• E — Regional market integration that
promotes the development of renewable
gases through secure transmission and
storage infrastructure, with a focus on
reducing NOx and GHG (greenhouse gas)
emissions.
• S — A safety-focused culture, with a
professional and development-oriented
team.
• G — Compliance with Latvian corporate
governance standards.

In the strategic initiative indicators for 2025, the Subsidiary’s Supervisory Board has included hydrogen studies,
the establishment of biomethane injection points, and other sustainability-related aspects, including those
related to accounting and the reduction of greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions.

CORPORATE
GOVERNANCE
ESRS GOV-1, GOV-2

THE GROUP’S
STRUCTURE AND
MANAGEMENT
The governance of the Group is carried out by
the Parent Company, whose governing bodies —
the Shareholders’ Meeting, the Audit Committee,
the Supervisory Board, and the Management Board —
operate based on the company’s Articles of Association,
the regulations of the Supervisory Board and the
Management Board, the medium-term operational
strategy, as well as in accordance with the relevant laws
and regulations of the Republic of Latvia, including the
Law on the Governance of Capital Shares and Capital
Companies of a Public Person and the Commercial Law.
The Group’s corporate governance model is developed
in accordance with good governance practices,
applicable laws and regulations, the Latvian Corporate
Governance Code, as well as the recommendations
of the European Union and the Organisation for
Economic Co-operation and Development (OECD).
These principles are reflected in the Group’s Corporate
Governance Policy.

Section 31, Paragraph 6, of the Law on
Governance of Capital Shares of Public Persons
and Capital Companies.

1

In accordance with the Corporate Governance Policy,
the Parent Company’s investments in other capital
companies are implemented based on good governance
principles. In the governance of the Subsidiary, AST also
complies with the rights and obligations of a shareholder
stipulated in the Law on the Governance of Capital
Shares and Capital Companies of a Public Person and
the Commercial Law, including the appointment of the
Supervisory Board of Conexus.
The corporate governance structure of the Subsidiary
consists of its Shareholders’ Meeting, Supervisory Board
and Management Board. As the Supervisory Board and
Management Board perform governance duties in full,
Conexus has not established separate committees.
The main principles for effective and sustainable
governance and operational transparency are defined
in the Conexus Corporate Governance Policy.
The number of members of the AST Supervisory Board,
Audit Committee and Management Board, as well
as the nomination and appointment procedures, are
determined by legal acts.¹; ² The composition of these
bodies is formed in a way that ensures all necessary
competencies, as well as a sufficiently critical and
independent approach to the evaluation and adoption
of decisions.

Section 55.6, Paragraph 3 of the Financial
Instruments Market Law.

SUPERVISORY BOARD
In accordance with laws and regulations and the
provisions set out in the AST Articles of Association,
the AST Supervisory Board consists of three members,
each serving a five-year term of office.
Each year, the Supervisory Board approves the work
plan and objectives, as well as the individual key
performance indicators (KPIs) to be achieved by each
member of the Management Board in the following year.
The implementation of the work plan and objectives is
evaluated once a year, simultaneous to the approval of
the annual report.

the financial and risk management system.
The main duties and functions of the Supervisory Board
are set out in the company's articles of association,
the Supervisory Board’s regulations, and in laws and
regulations.

MAIN RESPONSIBILITIES
• Approval of the medium-term business strategy,
monitor its implementation;
• Approval of the budget and monitor its execution;

In accordance with AST Management Board regulations,
the Supervisory Board receives a quarterly report on
the results of economic activities and the progress
in achieving the set objectives. In addition, once a
month, the AST Management Board informs the
Supervisory Board about major developments, including
sustainability-related matters.

KEY ROLE
Represents the interests of the shareholder in between
shareholder meetings, supervises the activities of
the Management Board, engages in the strategic
development of AST, and monitors the effectiveness of

• Determination of the strategic direction, goals, and
policies of the capital company;
• Constantly monitoring the activities of the Board,
electing and recalling members of the Management
Board, the Chair of the Management Board, and
determining their remuneration;
• Monitoring the effectiveness of the internal control
and risk management system;
• Monitoring that the capital company operates in
accordance with the laws, the Articles of Association,
and decisions of the Shareholders' Meeting.

Due to the fact that the statutory term during which
a temporary Supervisory Board Member may serve
expired for the temporary member Līga Rozentāle on
31 December 2025, by its decision of 30 December
2025, the Extraordinary Shareholders’ Meeting of AST
removed Līga Rozentāle from office and elected Juris
Zīvarts as a temporary member of the Supervisory
Board as of 1 January 2026.
An open competition will be organised for the vacant
positions of Supervisory Board Members.

As part of the continued improvement of AST’s
operational efficiency, its shareholder – the Ministry of
Climate and Energy – decided on 27 December 2024
to further reduce the number of AST Council members
from five to three.

AUDIT COMMITTEE
The AST Audit Committee was established in 2021
in accordance with the legal requirements applicable
to capital companies that issue securities. The Audit
Committee consists of three members and includes
two independent Audit Committee members selected
through an open competition, as well as one member of
the AST Supervisory Board. The objectives and tasks
of the Committee, its operating principles, rights, and
responsibilities are defined in the AST Audit Committee
Regulations, which were approved by a decision of the
AST Shareholders’ Meeting.
An authorisation agreement is concluded with the
members of the Audit Committee regarding the
performance of their duties as members of the Audit
Committee. Remuneration is paid to the members of the
Audit Committee, with the exception of the Supervisory
Board Member elected to the Committee, with the
remuneration amount determined by the Shareholders’
Meeting of the Parent Company.

KEY ROLE
To ensure the protection of the interests of the
shareholder and the Shareholders’ Meeting, especially
with regard to the preparation of the annual report,
its audit, the effectiveness of the internal control, risk

management and internal audit system, to guarantee the
reliability and objectivity of the report.

MAIN RESPONSIBILITIES
• Monitoring the process of preparing financial
statements;
• Monitoring the effectiveness of the internal controls
and risk management system insofar as this relates to
ensuring the integrity and completeness of the annual
report and consolidated annual report;

• Independent Member of the Audit Committee Inga
Liepa, term of office until 31 March 2028 (inclusive);
• Member of the Audit Committee, dependent on the
AST Supervisory Board, Olga Bogdanova, term of
office until 31 March 2028 (inclusive), but not longer
than until the end of the term of office of the AST
Supervisory Board Member.
In 2025, the AST Audit Committee consisted of
two independent members (66.67%) and one nonindependent member (33.33%).
Information on the experience of the members of the
Audit Committee is available on the Parent Company’s
website (see Audit Committee).

• Checking and monitoring compliance with the
independence and objectivity requirements set for the
auditor of the annual report.
Given that the term of office of the previous composition
of the AST Supervisory Board expired on 30 December
2024, and that Supervisory Board Member Aigars
Ģērmanis also performed the duties of a member of the
AST Audit Committee, in order to ensure the appropriate
number of members of the Committee, the AST Audit
Committee was re-elected in a new composition by a
decision of the Extraordinary Shareholders’ Meeting of
AST on 31 March 2025:
• Independent Chair of the Audit Committee Roberts
Ikaunieks, term of office until 31 March 2028
(inclusive);

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

MANAGEMENT BOARD
In 2025, the AST Management Board consisted of five
members, elected by the AST Supervisory Board for
a five-year term of office, in accordance with applicable
laws and regulations and following an assessment of the
competence and experience of the candidates.
The Management Board operates according to the
functional principle, and each member is responsible
for a specific area of activity. Organisational units are
subordinated to the members of the Management Board
and have their own regulations defining their objectives,
structure, and tasks.
All AST Management Board Members are independent
in their activities and do not hold ownership interests in
the capital of cooperation partners or related companies.
The Chair and members of the AST Management
Board receive remuneration, bonuses, and severance
compensation in accordance with the Law on the
Governance of Capital Shares and Capital Companies
of a Public Person.
Once a month, the Management Board receives a
report on the results of economic activities, as well as
on the progress of capital investment implementation. In
addition, a report is provided on indicators characterising
the quality and performance of electricity transmission
services, which includes various data, including safety
indicators such as occupational accidents and aspects
of occupational safety and the working environment.
Once a quarter, the Management Board reviews the
AST report on the management of significant risks, as

well as the report on financial results and progress in
achieving targets, including sustainability targets. The
management of sustainability aspects in AST is ensured
by several organisational units in accordance with the
distribution of their respective competencies.

KEY DECISIONS IN THE
2025 REPORTING YEAR
• Developed the Medium-Term Operating Strategy of
the High Voltage Network Group for 2026-2030;

KEY ROLE
To ensure the management of capital shares in daily
operations, jointly managing and representing AST.
The activities of the Management Board are regulated by
regulatory enactments, its tasks and responsibilities are
set out in the Parent Company's Articles of Association
and the Management Board's regulations.

• Developed the AST Human Resources Management
Strategy;
• Reviewed the new edition of the AST Corporate
Governance Policy;
• The Group’s material sustainability aspects were
reconfirmed ;

MAIN RESPONSIBILITIES

• Monitored the progress of the implementation of
AST Internal Audit recommendations (quarterly),
reviewed the Internal Audit Activity Report for 2024,
and the General Opinion on the effectiveness of
AST's internal control and risk management
systems in 2024;

• Manage and represent the company;

• Approved the report on AST's environmental and
energy management in 2024;

• Be responsible for the company's operations, as well
as for the compliance of accounting with legal acts;

• Changes were made to AST’s organisational
structure;

• Manage the company's assets;

• Evaluation of the report on fraud, corruption, and
conflict of interest risk management in 2024;

• Implement the company's strategic direction,
development plans, goals and policies.

• Issues that provide for measures to improve and
improve the efficiency of AST's operations were
reviewed;
• The Electricity Transmission System Development
Plan for the next decade was approved;
• The Integrated Management System Review Report
for 2024 was approved;
• A number of decisions were made to ensure the
implementation of capital investment projects in
accordance with the development plan, including
decisions on the establishment of new connections
for electricity producers from renewable energy
sources.
