SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

4

SUSTAINABILITY
GOVERNANCE
ESRS GOV-1, GOV-2, GOV-3, GOV-4, GOV-5

The Group has not developed a comprehensive
sustainability management policy. In 2023, AST
approved the Sustainability Governance Framework
and established the Sustainability Governance
Committee. The Committee includes representatives
of organisational units responsible for capital investment
planning, implementation and supervision, financial
resource planning, human resource management,
organisation of procurement processes, corporate
governance matters, as well as data collection
and disclosure.
The Sustainability Governance Committee is chaired
by the Chair of the AST Management Board and
is responsible for the management of sustainability
processes and the coordination of sustainability
objectives and their implementation. In accordance
with the Sustainability Governance Framework, the
Committee prepares a quarterly report for the AST
Management Board and Supervisory Board on the
implementation of the work plan and sustainability
objectives, analysing the progress in achieving the set
objectives, identifying risks and proposing solutions
for further progress.
As part of the ongoing improvement of the sustainability
governance process, in 2025, the Parent Company
reviewed the existing Sustainability Governance Process
Framework and defined the distribution of duties
and responsibilities within the Group, including those
related to ensuring data reliability and quality. In 2025,
the Sustainability Committee, in cooperation with the
consultant SIA Futurcene, actively worked on improving
the sustainability governance process — including the
development and approval of the Taxonomy Reporting

Guidelines, GHG emissions calculation methodology, as
well as the review of the double materiality assessment
carried out at the end of 2024, with a decision made
to continue using it for the preparation of the 2025
Sustainability Report.
In addition, the Corporate Governance Policy was
reviewed and approved in 2025. The Corporate Social
Responsibility Policy was also reviewed in 2025, and its
new version is planned to be approved in 2026.
The Corporate Governance Policy provides that in cases
where AST policies must be specified in the context
of the Subsidiary’s operations, or where AST has not
developed a policy in a particular area important to
the Subsidiary, Conexus will develop its own policy.
Accordingly, during 2025, Conexus reviewed its
Metrology Policy, Cybersecurity Policy, and Internal
Audit Policy.
In addition, to ensure compliance with the requirements
of the AST Green Bond Framework (ICMA Green Bond
Framework), AST has established a Green Committee,
which identifies and approves projects in accordance
with the Green Bond Framework and assesses
the compliance of investments implemented in the
transmission system with the requirements of the EU
Taxonomy (for details see the section on "Compliance
with EU Taxonomy Requirements").

=== pdf p.46 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

DEVELOPMENT OF
COMPETENCIES AND
SKILLS IN THE AREA OF
SUSTAINABILITY
The competences and professional experience
required of candidates for the Supervisory Board are
determined by the holder of the capital shares, while the
requirements for candidates for the Management Board
are determined by the Supervisory Board. Although no
selection procedures took place during the reporting
period, it is planned that sustainability knowledge criteria
will be included in the evaluation of candidates in the
future. To deepen their understanding of sustainability
governance issues, members of the Group’s
Supervisory Board and Management Board participated
in various seminars and conferences addressing
sustainability-related matters during the reporting period.
The Parent Company has joined the Swedbank
Business Sustainability Council, where leaders
of Latvian-origin companies from different sectors
exchange knowledge, deepen their understanding
of sustainability, and jointly promote a more sustainable
business environment in Latvia. Within the Business
Sustainability Council, AST is represented by
the Chair of the Management Board, while other
Group specialists participate in meetings depending
on the topics discussed.

In 2025, the workshops of the Business Sustainability
Council focused on the disclosure requirements
regarding corporate value chains set out in the European
Sustainability Reporting Standards (ESRS), considering
that value chain analysis remains one of the most
complex tasks in practical implementation.
For example, on 21 August 2025, AST participated in the
workshop "Value Chain ABC: Key Elements, Risks and
Opportunities", on 25 September in "Responsible and
Forward-Looking Supplier Management", and on
16 October in "Disclosure Obligations Regarding
the Value Chain."
Each year, AST prepares a training plan and defines
the competences and skills to be developed, including
individual training plans for managers. In addition,
lectures are organised for all company employees, which
are also available online on the internal platform Viszinis.
Both AST and Conexus have joined the Family-Friendly
Workplace initiative, while AST also participates in the
Diversity is Strength movement and the Mission Zero
occupational health and safety initiative. Under these
initiatives, the company not only assesses its current
performance, but also participates in educational
activities and discussions with experts.
The implementation of new regulatory requirements
requires substantial capacity for the development of
competencies and the acquisition of information.
Therefore, in 2024, following a procurement procedure,
consultants SIA Sustinere Latvija were engaged to
work with the company to define the value chain map
of AST and the Group, including the identification and
assessment of impacts, risks and opportunities, and to

conduct a double materiality assessment (impact and
financial).
Under the aegis of this cooperation, existing procedures
were evaluated, guidelines for necessary changes
were drawn up, and consultations were provided
regarding sustainability governance processes and
reporting requirements. The cooperation also included
regular meetings with the AST working group and
members of the Management Board, during which the
consultants shared information about the requirements
of the directive regarding sustainability reporting
and its implementation, as well as best practices in
sustainability governance.
Continuing to improve sustainability governance,
cooperation with SIA Futurcene was initiated in 2025.
Futurcene is a Latvia-based consulting firm with
professional experience in sustainability governance,
including climate risk assessment, supply chain analysis,
the implementation of sustainable reporting practices,
and the development of financial instruments. Its team
consists of experienced experts who rely on data
analysis and strategic advisory services for clients.

company’s strategic goals, as well as transparency,
ethics and management accountability in corporate
governance.
In addition, SIA Futurcene provided consulting services
on improving sustainability governance processes
within the Group. This included an evaluation of the
2024 Sustainability Report and recommendations for
improving its contents. In the area of the value chain,
value chain mapping and improvement were carried
out, incorporating all key operational stages and related
companies.
Furthermore, in the area of process implementation,
a sustainability reporting framework was developed
and implemented, responsibilities and data flows were
defined, and training on sustainability management
methodology was provided.

In March 2025, SIA Futurcene conducted training
sessions for the Group’s management and specialists
on current developments in corporate governance
and sustainable corporate management. The training
focused on integrating corporate governance and
sustainability principles into company operations.
Topics covered included ESG (Environmental, Social,
Governance) management systems and current
developments, responsible decision-making and risk
management, linking sustainability objectives with the

=== pdf p.47 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

SUSTAINABILITY
ISSUES CONSIDERED BY
GOVERNANCE BODIES
At the Group level, sustainability governance is ensured
by the parent company’s governance structures.
The AST Shareholders’ Meeting approves the Letter of
Expectations, which defines the development directions
of the Group, strategic objectives, and financial and nonfinancial targets, including those related to sustainability.
The AST Supervisory Board, in turn, approves the
medium-term operational strategy, integrating the
objectives defined in the Letter of Expectations, and
supervises the implementation of the strategy.
By a decision of the Extraordinary Shareholders’
Meeting of AST on 1 August 2025, the Letter of
Expectations of the holder of the capital shares
of AST was approved, setting out expectations
regarding the operational directions and priorities of
the Augstsprieguma tīkls Group, as well as strategic
financial and non-financial objectives, including
sustainable governance and the development of
sustainable energy.
During the reporting year, with the involvement of
the representative of the holder of capital shares, the
Supervisory Board, and the Management Board, a draft
of the AST Group’s medium-term operational strategy
for 2026–2030 was drawn up, which also includes

strategic sustainability-related objectives.
Once a year, the AST Shareholders’ Meeting is informed
about sustainability matters, when approving the
consolidated annual report of the Augstsprieguma tīkls
Group and the annual report of AS Augstsprieguma
tīkls, which includes the sustainability report as part
of the management report. Once a year, the AST
Supervisory Board reviews and provides its opinion
on the annual report before it is submitted to the
Shareholders’ Meeting.
Once a quarter, the AST Management Board and
Supervisory Board are informed about progress in
achieving AST's sustainability objectives, which are
included in the report on the implementation of the
work plan and objectives. Parallel to this, once a
quarter the Management Board reviews a report on
indicators characterising the operation of the electricity
transmission system, including safety indicators, such
as occupational accidents, registered environmental
incidents, and unauthorised or unlawful activities at
infrastructure facilities.
In addition, every month the AST Supervisory Board is
informed about AST's major developments, including
sustainability-related matters, e.g. electricity generation
from renewable energy sources and the construction of
environmentally friendly green substations.

In 2025, the Supervisory Board of the Parent Company
approved the following decisions on the following
significant sustainability-related matters:
• approval of the medium-term operational strategy
of the AST Group for 2026-2030;
• approval of the new version of the AST Corporate
Governance Policy;
• evaluation of the AST Human Resources Strategy;
• as part of the Parent Company’s annual objectives
and the Management Board’s key performance
indicators (KPIs), AST sustainability targets for 2025
have been approved (for more detailed information,

performance in incentive schemes").

In 2025, the Subsidiary’s Supervisory Board reviewed

RISK MANAGEMENT
PRINCIPLES AND
MECHANISMS
The principles of AST’s risk management are defined
in the company’s Risk Management Policy, which
clearly sets out the roles and responsibilities of
the Supervisory Board, the Audit Committee, the
Internal Audit Department, the Management Board,
Management Board Members, the Quality System and
Risk Management Department, as well as heads of
organisational units and employees in risk management.
The policy includes not only requirements regarding
reporting deadlines, decision-making processes and risk
monitoring, but also the requirement to regularly assess
the effectiveness of the risk management system itself.
Additional regulation is set out in the Risk Management
Concept, which defines risk appetite criteria and
establishes the process for planning the necessary
actions to mitigate risks. Detailed risk management
provisions are included in regulations developed
for specific risk areas, such as the Financial Risk
Management Regulations, which identify specific risk
indicators, and the Fraud, Corruption and Conflict of
Interest Risk Management Regulations, which establish
zero tolerance for any violations.
Within the strategic planning process, AST
systematically assesses risks, opportunities and
potential impacts arising from internal and external

environmental factors in order to ensure the
achievement of the set objectives. The flow of risk
management information is ensured through quarterly
reports on the most significant risks, as well as through
in-depth reviews of each risk area, which are conducted
at least once every 1–3 years, depending on the specific
nature of the area concerned. Such regular risk analysis
enables the Management Board to make informed and
timely decisions.
In 2025, the most significant risks for the Group
were related to the accelerated synchronisation with
continental Europe in February 2025, cybersecurity
threats, physical security risks, fraud risks, and the
impact of Russia’s military aggression. At the same
time, opportunities were also identified, including the
broader use of renewable energy resources and the
implementation of advanced technological solutions,
such as remote control systems for managing the
microclimate of substations.
The prioritisation of risk management measures is
based on risk appetite criteria and assessments of
investment efficiency, ensuring the effective allocation
of resources. To mitigate the potential impact of
risks, several measures have been implemented,
including employee training, scenario simulations,
the strengthening of technical resources, and the
introduction of new solutions. This approach ensures
not only the identification and mitigation of risks, but
also contributes to AST’s long-term security and
sustainability.
The fundamental principles of risk management
at Conexus are defined in the Company’s Risk

Management Policy, which sets out the roles and
responsibilities of the Supervisory Board, the
Internal Audit Unit, the Management Board, the Risk
Management Manager, as well as those of department
managers and employees in risk management.
The risk management process at Conexus is carried out
in accordance with the risk management procedures
approved by the Company’s Management Board,
which establish uniform risk management principles
across all risk areas and define risk appetite criteria.
Information on risk management, changes in the most
significant risks, and their compliance with risk appetite
criteria is provided through semi‑annual reports to the
Company’s Management Board and annual reports to
the Company’s Supervisory Board.

RISK MANAGEMENT AND
INTERNAL CONTROL
OVER SUSTAINABILITY
REPORTING
GOV-5
Within the Group, an internal control system (including
internal audit) and a risk management system have
been established and are continually improved.
The Supervisory Board ensures oversight of the

effectiveness of the internal control, risk management
and compliance systems, as well as the activities of the
Internal Audit Department.
The Audit Committee provides oversight insofar as
it relates to ensuring the reliability and objectivity
of the annual report and the consolidated annual
report. The implementation of the internal control, risk
management and compliance systems is ensured by
the Management Board, while their independent and
objective assessment is carried out by the Internal Audit
Department.
The internal control processes related to the preparation
of the sustainability report, ensuring the reliability and
accuracy of the data included therein, are defined in the
AST Sustainability Governance Process Framework:
• Organisational units that collect and provide data for
sustainability reporting purposes are responsible for
the reliability of the data and provide the data in an
unaltered form.;
• The Sustainability Governance Committee ensures
the correct representation of the received data in
the AST sustainability report and in reports on the
achievement of AST sustainability objectives;
• In the preparation of the sustainability report, the
organisational unit responsible for the respective
sustainability aspect implements internal control
processes, ensuring the reliability of the data;
• The assessment of the compliance of the report
may be carried out in cooperation with an external
49


=== pdf p.50 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

consultant to ensure an independent evaluation and
additional assurance.
Pursuant to the AST Sustainability Management
Process Procedure, for the purposes of sustainability
report preparation, by 31 May each year a coordinator
responsible for the preparation of the report and a list of
employees responsible for specific sections of the report
are prepared and approved by an order of a member of
the Management Board.
In 2025, amendments were made to the Sustainability
Governance Process Framework, integrating the
responsibilities of both companies of the Group, defining
the approach to determining the scope (materiality) of
information, the responsibilities for the collection and
provision of information, as well as the coordinating
function for the collection of sustainability report
information.
The internal audit function performs audits of
organisational units and processes (including in 2025
the Sustainability Governance Process, the Human
Resources Management Process and the Transmission
Asset Maintenance Process), within which assurance
is obtained regarding the compliance of the processes
managed by the organisational unit, which in turn affects
the reliability of the data. Upon completion of the internal
audit, the Management Board and Supervisory Board
of the Parent Company are informed about the results
and findings. Once a quarter, the Management Board
and Supervisory Board of the Parent Company are
informed about progress in implementing internal audit
recommendations.

Any incidents, including those that may relate to
sustainability matters, are recorded in the relevant
registers maintained by the responsible organisational
units. The information is included both in quarterly
reports on significant risks to the Management Board
and Supervisory Board and in separate reports in the
respective areas. No significant findings were registered
in 2025.

INCLUSION OF
SUSTAINABILITY-RELATED
PERFORMANCE IN
INCENTIVE SCHEMES

To ensure that the information provided by the Group is
accurate and complete, integrated information systems
are being developed, and data quality monitoring
is carried out. The Group’s financial information is
prepared in accordance with International Financial
Reporting Standards, and its financial statements are
audited by an international audit firm with relevant
experience in the energy sector. The Group’s
management regularly informs employees about longterm and short-term plans and operational results,
including by organising online meetings with employees.

ESRS GOV-3
The remuneration of the Supervisory Board and
Management Board of the Parent Company is
determined by the Law on the Governance of Capital
Shares and Capital Companies of a Public Person and
the Cabinet regulations issued on the basis thereof.
The legal acts establish a uniform framework for the
remuneration of supervisory board and management
board members of capital companies owned by
a public person.
The remuneration and compensation of the members
of the Management Board of the Subsidiary are
determined by the Conexus Supervisory Board,
taking into account the Remuneration Policy for the
Management Board and Supervisory Board Members
of Conexus, the principles set out in the Law on the
Governance of Capital Shares and Capital Companies
of a Public Person, as well as the maximum amounts
specified in the Cabinet regulations issued on the basis
of this law, aligning the level of remuneration with the
scope of the duties of the Management Board Members.
The remuneration of the Supervisory Board Members
of the Group companies is determined by their

shareholders by adopting a commensurate decision
and taking into account the aforementioned internal
and external regulatory acts. All decisions of the
Shareholders’ Meeting are publicly available on the
websites of the Parent Company and the Subsidiary.
Information on the remuneration of the Management
Board and Supervisory Board of the Parent Company
and the Subsidiary is published in their respective
annual reports.
Authorisation agreements are concluded with the Chair
and members of the Supervisory Board of the Parent
Company, and the provisions of the AST collective
labour agreement do not apply to them. The monthly
remuneration of the Chair of the Supervisory Board
and the Chair of the Management Board of the Parent
Company is linked to the average monthly gross salary
of employees in the country for the previous year, as
published by the Central Statistical Bureau of Latvia,
to which a coefficient is applied in accordance with
indicators characterising the size of the capital company
(turnover, assets and number of employees). No
bonuses are paid to the Chair and members of the AST
Supervisory Board, and they do not receive severance
pay or any other type of compensation.
The performance evaluation of the Management Board
and Supervisory Board of the Group companies is
carried out in accordance with Cabinet regulations and
the guidelines developed by the Coordination Centre,
ensuring a transparent and structured approach.
The individual performance evaluation of Management
Board Members of the Group companies is based
on the achievement of objectives linked to the overall
50


=== pdf p.51 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

performance of the company, while the performance
of Supervisory Board Members is assessed on the
basis of an annual self-assessment. The annual selfassessments, which are submitted to the Shareholders’
Meeting, reflect the contribution of the Supervisory
Board and the Management Board to strengthening
sustainability governance and achieving the company’s
strategic objectives.
In addition, the Audit Committee provides an annual
report on the quality of the internal control and
risk management systems and the preparation of
sustainability reports.
Based on the sustainable development objectives
defined in the company’s strategy, each year the
Supervisory Board, along with the approval of the
budget for the following year, approves both financial
and non-financial targets, as well as the individual
target indicators (Key Performance Indicators – KPI) for
each Management Board Member, which influence the
variable part of their remuneration.
Once a year, following the approval of the annual report,
the Supervisory Board may decide on the payment of
bonuses to the Management Board Members. When
determining the bonus, three main criteria are taken
into account: the company’s performance results
in the previous reporting year (budget execution);
implementation of the medium-term operational
strategy and the company’s performance in accordance
with the defined objectives, including sustainability
objectives; and the individual performance results of the
Management Board Member and the achievement of
their individual objectives.

Bonuses for Management Board Members may not
exceed two monthly salaries of a Management Board
Member, while, in accordance with legal requirements,
Supervisory Board Members are not paid bonuses and
the remuneration of Supervisory Board Members is not
linked to sustainability governance.
The annual objectives of the Management Board
Members of the Parent Company include the
organisation’s sustainable development objectives,
depending on the area of responsibility of the respective
Management Board Member. Sustainability objectives
accounted for 20% of the total objectives of the Parent
Company in 2025. The Parent Company regularly
monitors the achievement of the set objectives by
reviewing a report on their progress once a quarter.
In 2025, the following key sustainability objectives were
set in the individual targets of the Management Board
Members of the Parent Company:
• Reduction of AST’s emissions, aiming to achieve
the EU climate neutrality targets for 2030
(45% reduction) and for 2050 (climate neutrality);
• Development of the network to connect electricity
producers from renewable energy sources;
• Implementation of the innovative project Green
Substation Carnikava, reducing CO2 emissions.
The defined sustainability objectives are further
integrated into the objectives of the companies’
organisational units and into the individual objectives
of employees, forming part of the broader employee

motivation system.
In 2025, strategic initiatives of the Subsidiary with
sustainability aspects — hydrogen studies, biomethane
and others — accounted for 15% of the Subsidiary’s
overall performance indicators.
In the coming periods, as the management of
sustainability-related matters continues to be improved,
it is planned that the involvement of the Management
Board and Supervisory Board in sustainability
governance will be further developed.

STATEMENT ON DUE
DILIGENCE
ESRS GOV-4
Compliance with the due diligence principle in
sustainability governance ensures:
• Timely identification of potential negative impacts
on the environment or society (particularly affected
communities);
• Ensuring a consultation process (as proactively
as possible) with affected communities or other
stakeholders regarding potential or actual negative
impacts on the environment or society;

• Determining appropriate measures to mitigate
or prevent negative impacts;
• Evaluation of the effectiveness of the measures
implemented;
• Ensuring feedback to affected communities and other
stakeholders so that they have the opportunity to
express their concerns, complaints or proposals;
• Organising learning processes within the organisation
to prevent the recurrence or occurrence of similar
negative impacts or to identify the potential for similar
adverse impact in a timely manner.
The due diligence principle within the Group is applied
both in relation to its own operations and in the selection
and management of cooperation partners, bearing in
mind that the actions of cooperation partners may also
result in adverse impacts to the environment or society.
The negative impacts identified in the operations of the
AST Group (in accordance with the double materiality
assessment) may be related to:
• Increased greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions
generated by the Group and its value chain,
which have a negative impact on the environment,
contribute to climate change and thereby gradually
affect the living environment and well-being
of society;
• Loss of biodiversity that may be caused by the
construction and maintenance activities of the Group’s
infrastructure throughout the territory of Latvia,
51


=== pdf p.52 ===
affecting both the surrounding environment and,
consequently, the living environment and resources
of society;
• Use of primary raw materials and materials,
which may still be associated with the use of
environmentally harmful substances (e.g. SF6);
• Occupational health and safety risks, particularly
when working at heights, with hazardous electrical
equipment and natural gas transmission equipment,
or when participating in construction works. These
activities may involve both Group employees and
subcontractors and their employees;
• Employee involvement in responding to emergency
situations, which are often unpredictable and require
immediate mobilisation regardless of the time of
day, as well as increased vigilance and prolonged
concentration when working in atypical and potentially
hazardous conditions. The likelihood of such work
increases as climate risks and their frequency
increase;

cases, impacts may be created both on the environment
and on surrounding communities, whose usual
environment is altered — both temporarily (e.g. transport
restrictions or changes to traffic organisation during
construction) and in the long term (significant changes
to the surrounding landscape and living environment).
In addition to the factors identified in the double
materiality assessment process, there is a possibility
that the activities of the Group’s companies may
also result in other types of negative impacts on the
environment or society that are currently not identified or
that have not previously been deemed to be sufficiently
material.
AST has developed an interactive process map that
integrates management, core business and support
processes and ensures the connection of the control
systems related to process management. Responsible
managers and a support team have been assigned to
the processes.

• Restrictions on land use resulting from the
maintenance of infrastructure managed by the
Group’s companies, for example, applicable to
farmers whose land is crossed by high-voltage
lines or where other significant energy transmission
infrastructure is located.
Restrictions on land use, as well as possible changes to
the landscape, may also result from the implementation
of infrastructure development works, particularly the
construction of new infrastructure facilities. In such

corrective and/or preventive actions are developed
and implemented where necessary;

working conditions that affect employee well-being
and safety both under normal working conditions and
in emergency situations;
• An Idea Register has been established to enable
employee engagement, where proposals for
improvements can be submitted, including
anonymously. Entries in the Idea Register are
evaluated by the responsible organisational units
and, if a proposal cannot be fully implemented,
responses are published. Submitted proposals
remain anonymous unless the submitter has
indicated their name;

• Ensuring the functioning of the occupational safety
system within the Group’s companies and setting
strict requirements for subcontractors, including the
application of sanctions for violations of occupational
safety rules, thereby ensuring that subcontractor
employees are also properly informed of the
requirements and that their compliance is monitored;
• Conducting workplace risk assessments, organising
monthly technical training sessions, occupational
safety days, inspections of compliance with
occupational safety requirements, conducting
unannounced inspections of work teams at
workplaces and evaluates the operation of equipment.
The results of inspections are documented and
discussed with staff and responsible specialists.
Where necessary, corrective and/or preventive
actions are implemented;
• Establishing requirements for employees of the
Group’s companies and subcontractors performing
infrastructure maintenance work, for example,
regarding the observance of bird nesting periods,
coordination of works with landowners, compliance
with mowing periods and other aspects that ensure
more environmentally and community-friendly
operations;

5

IMPACT, RISK
AND OPPORTUNITY
MANAGEMENT
ESRS IRO-1, SBM-2, SBM-3

PROCESS FOR
IDENTIFYING AND
ASSESSING MATERIAL
IMPACTS, RISKS AND
OPPORTUNITIES
In accordance with the requirements of the EU
Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive (CSRD)
and the European Sustainability Reporting Standards
(ESRS), in 2024 the Group began implementing a
systematic approach to identifying and assessing
material impacts, risks and opportunities based on
the principle of double materiality. The objective of
the assessment was to identify and evaluate the
Group’s impacts, risks and opportunities both in its own
operations and in its value chain. Issues related to the
rights of indigenous peoples were excluded from the
assessment, as they are not applicable to the operations
of AST and the Group within the territory of Latvia.
The assessment process was based on several steps
implemented by the management and specialists
of the Parent Company in cooperation with external
consultants and involving representatives of Conexus,
in order to ensure that all material impacts, risks and
opportunities of the Group were identified and properly
assessed.
At the beginning of the assessment, the Group’s value

chain was identified and, by evaluating the Group’s
main areas of activity, resources and "hotspots" within
the value chain, such as electricity generation and
gas extraction (see the section "Business model and
strategy"), agreement was reached apropos the scope
of the assessment. Based on this and on the list of
sustainability topics provided in the ESRS, an initial
identification of the Group’s impacts was carried out
in its own operations as well as in the upstream and
downstream value chain. This helped to establish
the foundation for the next stage of the process, in
which external consultants together with the Group’s
specialists identified both positive and negative impacts
and assessed them by analysing:
• Gravity of impact: its scale, scope (the number
of people affected and the geographical area), and
remediability (assessed on a four‑point scale);
• Probability: the likelihood that the specific potential
impact will occur in the short, medium or long term,
using the time frames specified in the ESRS for the
assessment (assessed on a five‑point scale).
The severity and likelihood parameters were summed,
and a threshold score of 7 was set for determining the
materiality of a specific impact (i.e., all impacts with an
aggregate score of 7 or above are deemed material).
The process relied not only on the company’s internal
documents and available information regarding
stakeholders’ views and concerns, but also on
industry standards and reports issued by international
organisations. In addition, several stakeholders, such as
employees and suppliers, were involved in interviews in
56


=== pdf p.57 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

order to validate the initial assessments and refine them
where necessary.
In the financial materiality assessment, the Group’s
specialists identified risks and opportunities that could
affect the company’s financial position, cash flows,
access to capital or cost of capital.

impacts were assessed using a qualitative approach, but
starting from 2026 it is planned to gradually introduce a
quantitative assessment scale for evaluating risks and
opportunities.
In 2025, the materiality assessment conducted
previously was reviewed and re-approved at a meeting
of the Sustainability Committee.

Each risk and opportunity was assessed on a five‑point
scale based on its likelihood and potential financial
impact on the company. The likelihood and financial
impact scores were multiplied, and a threshold score of
10 was established for determining the materiality of a
given risk or opportunity (i.e., all risks or opportunities
with a combined score of 10 or above are deemed
material).
In this process, consideration was given to the
company’s dependence on critical resources and
suppliers, as well as the potential impact of negative
consequences on financial indicators. Particular
attention was paid to the value chain, including risks
related to supply chain disruptions or the actions of
business partners, as well as potential changes in the
regulatory framework and their impact on the company’s
financial performance.
The Parent Company and the Subsidiary identified
risks and opportunities separately and later assessed
the potential scale of financial impact, likelihood and
time horizon (short, medium or long term) according
to a unified approach. In this process, the Group’s
companies largely relied on the existing approach to
risk management, which is organised based on the
principles set out in ISO 31000. In 2024, financial
57


=== pdf p.58 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

STAKEHOLDER
ENGAGEMENT

and meetings with construction and maintenance
contractors). Before approving draft regulations related
to sector activities, public consultations are regularly
organised, along with public hearings on development
projects and other stakeholder engagement activities.

The Group’s stakeholder engagement aims to
improve the company’s performance in relation to all
stakeholders and society as a whole.

The Group operates in a sector with high safety risks,
and its facilities are located not only on land owned
by the company but also on land owned by other
parties. Therefore, cooperation with local communities
and landowners is particularly important. Before
implementing significant development projects, the
Parent Company organises public consultations
in local municipalities, listening to the views of
local communities. Annually, the Subsidiary sends
informational materials to local municipalities where its
facilities are located, explaining legal and regulatory
requirements. In addition, the Subsidiary, in cooperation
with the State Fire and Rescue Service, organises
civil protection exercises during which the alarm and
notification system is activated, emergency vehicles
move along local and national roads, people are
evacuated, and temporary traffic restrictions may occur.

AST’s stakeholders are mapped according to their level
of influence and interest, determining the methods and
intensity of engagement. AST’s assessment is reviewed
annually, and the forms of engagement and frequency
of communication are adjusted accordingly. Conexus
last reviewed its stakeholder mapping in 2023. As there
have been no significant changes in the company’s
operations that would affect the stakeholder mapping, it
has subsequently remained unchanged.
The Group uses various methods to engage
stakeholders in the development of regulatory
proposals, strategy formation, and other processes,
including the previously described process for
determining material sustainability topics. These
methods include the company website, publications,
individual meetings, public consultations, surveys,
participation in commissions, and visits to the
underground gas storage facility, etc.
The Group organises public meetings with customers,
suppliers, and cooperation partners regularly to inform
them about developments in the sector and company
updates (events include the Electricity Market Forum,
meetings with organisations involved in design work,

In addition to cooperation with stakeholders, the Group
places strong emphasis on public communication and
media relations, informing the public about important
decisions, development projects, and investments.
Alongside traditional communication channels, the
Group and its Subsidiary communicate via social media
platforms such as Facebook and LinkedIn. To assess
communication effectiveness, the Group’s companies
conduct daily media monitoring to track the circulation
of published information in the media space. In 2025,
Latvian media continued to pay increased attention to

the energy sector, and therefore all information released
by the Group was widely covered in the Latvian media
environment.
Media interviews are conducted on a regular basis—
both in response to media interest generated by
information released by the Group and at the initiative
of the Group’s companies. Photo and video materials
prepared by the Group are regularly used in media
publications.
To inform the media about the synchronisation of
the Baltic electricity grids with continental Europe
and related project developments—one of AST’s
most significant priorities in recent years—the
Parent Company organised several media events
in 2025, including a joint press conference with
the Ministry of Climate and Energy on the planned
synchronisation of the electricity grids, the opening
of synchronous compensator stations in Grobiņa and
Līksna, the opening of an electricity storage battery
station in Rēzekne, and interviews with management
representatives in Latvian and international media.
Social media activity, including reposting of information
and comments from stakeholders, is actively monitored.
The Group always responds to questions or remarks
raised on social media.

The results of the materiality assessment carried out in
2024 (and subsequently deemed as still being relevant
in 2025) were summarised in a materiality matrix, which
later served as the basis for the AST Management
Board’s decision on determining the materiality
threshold. The AST Management Board decided to
classify as material those topics for which both the
impact and financial materiality assessments, in line
with the Group’s materiality assessment methodology,
were rated as "critical" or "significant."
As a result, the following topics were identified
as material for the Group:

Significant

Biodiversity and eco systems*
Incoming resources
Safe employment
Working hours*
Community impact related to land use
Corporate culture*

69


=== pdf p.70 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

The Group’s material impacts, risks, and opportunities
arise from its operations in the energy sector,
particularly in relation to climate change mitigation,
energy security, and ecosystem preservation. At the
same time, the Group’s strategy is closely linked to the
development of sustainable energy and the achievement
of sustainability objectives, including promoting energy
transition and the integration of renewable energy
sources.
The Group’s value chain, particularly in relation to the
use of biomass and natural gas, contributes to climate
change. However, strategic investments in renewable
energy support climate change mitigation by promoting
sustainable energy production and use.
Energy stability and security have a significant positive
impact on direct users by ensuring uninterrupted
electricity and natural gas supply. The Group focuses on
infrastructure development that enables integration with
the European grid and helps to strengthen the region’s
energy independence. The implementation of projects
such as synchronisation with continental Europe creates
opportunities to improve energy security across the
Baltic region.
The construction and maintenance of transmission
networks affect land use, may contribute to habitat
fragmentation, and can create risks for the protection of
species. Mindful that such impacts may have negative
consequences for local ecosystems, the Group actively
works to mitigate them. Sustainable planning and
compensation mechanisms help to reduce negative
effects on residents and the natural environment.
The Group’s strategy also includes close cooperation
with affected communities and national supervisory

authorities to ensure that the company’s operations
comply with environmental protection regulations and
minimise any potential harm.
Financial impacts largely arise from the need to adapt
to climate and sustainability requirements, which include
significant investments in the implementation of new
technologies and processes. The Group’s strategy
is focused on sustainable growth, concentrating on
the development of low-emission technologies, the
reduction of greenhouse gas emissions, and increased
use of renewable resources. This is an important step
towards ensuring the company’s future competitiveness
and alignment with the EU Green Deal.
To effectively manage the identified impacts and
risks, the Group actively adapts its strategy, focusing
on innovation, long-term investment plans, and the
transformation of its business model. The Group
continuously monitors and adjusts its operations in line
with changing regulations, market conditions, and new
technological challenges. More information about the
Group’s strategy can be found in the "Business Model
and Strategy" section.
In accordance with the materiality assessment
conducted and the sustainability topics consequently
identified as material, impact, risk, and opportunity
factors have been defined. These are summarised in
a table indicating the type of manifestation (particularly
for impacts—actual or potential, positive or negative),
the location of manifestation (own operations and/or the
value chain), and the time horizon of manifestation in
accordance with the time periods defined by ESRS

ENVIRONMENTAL
INFORMATION

CLIMATE CHANGES
ESRS E1-1

TRANSITION PLAN
FOR CLIMATE CHANGE
MITIGATION
The energy sector plays an important role in society’s
transition towards a sustainable and carbon-neutral
future. In light of the European Green Deal, changes
in EU and national policies, plans and regulations, the
importance of climate-friendly and sustainable energy
solutions, along with stakeholder expectations have
increased significantly.
In 2025, the Group initiated the development of a
Group's transition plan. By the end of the year, a draft
of the plan had been prepared and reviewed at a
meeting of the AST Management Board. However, the
final version of the plan will be further refined in 2026.
At the same time, climate change mitigation has been
defined as one of the three high-priority Sustainable
Development Goals in the Group’s Medium-Term
Operational Strategy for 2021–2025. The Group’s
sustainability objectives and corresponding investments
for both the Parent Company and the Subsidiary derive
from the goals defined in the Strategy.
Among other initiatives, in the context of climate change

mitigation the Parent Company is implementing a project
involving construction of a new dispatch control and
data centre, one of the objectives of which is to ensure
energy-efficient operations. Interconnections are also
being developed, including with neighbouring countries
(in 2025, the development of an interconnection with
Estonia was particularly relevant). The purpose of
these interconnections is both to ensure the security
and continuity of energy supply and to facilitate the
integration of renewable resources across the regional
energy network. Resources are also being allocated to
the development of new connections with renewable
energy producers, thereby contributing to the overall
decarbonisation of the electricity supply sector.
The Group’s Medium-Term Operational Strategy
for the period 2026–2030, drawn up in 2025 and
submitted to the State Chancellery for coordination,
also defines sustainable energy development, including
the promotion of renewable energy availability, as one
of its overarching objectives. In line with the Group’s
objectives, the Parent Company’s strategy for the
new period sets two goals related to the transition in
response to climate change:
1. Energy supply security and resilience, which includes
climate risk management, ensuring service continuity
or the fastest possible restoration even after the
impact of extreme weather events, restoration of the
energy system, including the gradual replacement
of solutions with more environmentally and climatefriendly alternatives;
2. Electricity grid development, which includes
connecting renewable energy generation to the
77

is approved, the Parent Company is implementing the
climate change mitigation-related objectives defined
within the Strategy referred to above. The investments
required to achieve these objectives are included in the
10-year electricity transmission network development
plan approved by the Public Utilities Commission (PUC).
Detailed information on the measures implemented by
the Parent Company and the Subsidiary can be found
in the "Implemented and Planned Activities" section.
Information on the proportion of capital expenditure,
operating expenditure, and turnover of the Parent
Company and the Subsidiary in the 2025 reporting
year that complies with the climate change mitigation
objective of the EU Taxonomy Regulation is provided
in the "Compliance with EU Taxonomy Requirements"

strategic objective from which related objectives and
corresponding investments derive, aimed at facilitating
the decarbonisation of the Subsidiary’s operations.
At the same time, when assessing the Subsidiary’s
impact, it can be concluded that although natural gas
consumption has gradually decreased over the past
decade due to improvements in energy efficiency and
the transition to renewable energy sources in electricity
and heat generation, natural gas still occupies an
important place in Latvia’s primary energy consumption
structure. Consumption is closely linked to fluctuations
in air temperature, natural gas prices on the market, and
the competitiveness of electricity generated from natural
gas in the Baltic and Nordic electricity markets.
In 2025, as part of its decarbonisation activities, the
Subsidiary established one metering, quality control
and odourisation unit at the connection point of a
biomethane production facility, as well as overseeing
the entry into service of the first public biomethane
injection point in Džūkste. Total investments reached
EUR 1.6 million, of which EUR 1.5 million was financed
through the European Union’s Recovery and Resilience
Facility. In the Subsidiary’s near-term development
plans, at least two additional centralised biomethane
injection points are planned in Rēzekne and Ragana.
These points will become part of a sustainable and
scalable infrastructure network that will promote the
involvement of renewable energy producers and
facilitate the integration of biomethane into the national
gas transmission system.

and balancing, challenges for which are created by

The implementation of the aforementioned measures
will help in particular to reduce greenhouse gas
emissions across the entire energy supply network.
With regard to reducing the Group’s own emissions,
the Group is working on defining detailed targets and
measures, which will be compiled in the transition

secured from the European Union. This cooperation
reflects a shared commitment to building a resilient
and sustainable energy future in the Baltic Sea region.
The corridor aims to support the development of
the renewable hydrogen market and integrate it into
Europe’s future energy system.
Methane is a powerful greenhouse gas with a global
warming potential 28 times higher over 100 years
compared with carbon dioxide. Recognising the
environmental impact of the Subsidiary’s operations and
the need for continuous improvement in reducing this
impact, methane emission reduction plans are actively
being implemented. By reducing methane emissions,
it is possible to slow climate change, improve air quality,
and conserve the non-renewable natural resource —
natural gas.
The Group’s companies are not excluded from
the EU Paris Agreement.

Espoo

ESRS IRO-1

ASSESSMENT OF CLIMATERELATED IMPACTS, RISKS
AND OPPORTUNITIES
Climate change is not only an area influenced by the
company through its operations and the emissions
directly or indirectly resulting from them, but also one
whose resultant processes could affect the company’s
activities. In other words, climate change creates new
risks for business operations—both directly
and indirectly.
Companies may be directly affected by so-called
physical risks (primarily comprised of impacts related to
changes in weather patterns or extreme weather events
affecting operations, assets, human resources, etc.).
These include both physical acute and physical chronic
risks. Indirectly, companies may be affected by transition
risks—risks associated with political, technological
and other changes arising from climate change.
For example, changes in technologies or the
introduction of new regulatory requirements in the area
of emission reduction may create a need for additional
investments, operational restructuring, or other
previously unplanned actions.
To determine how such risks affect the operations of
the AST Group, a climate risk assessment was carried
out in 2025, identifying potential risk factors and their
impact on the operations of both AST and Conexus. The

assessment was conducted taking into account climate
change projections developed at the global level by the
Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC),
at the European level by the European Environment
Agency, and at national level by the State Limited
Liability Company Latvian Environment, Geology and
Meteorology Centre (LVĢMC). The identification and
assessment of the Group’s climate risks considered
SSP2-4.5 and SSP3-7.0 climate change scenarios
(according to the IPCC classification, these scenarios
are considered to be plausible and project moderate to
significant climate change).

EstLink2
650 MW DC

EstLink1
350 MW DC
EstLink3

Tume

Given that the infrastructure managed by the AST
Group is developed and maintained for long-term use,
the climate risk assessment analysed potential changes
for at least the period from 2040 to 2070, i.e. the middle
of this century, while also considering the possible
dynamics of further changes towards the end of the
century.
According to these projections, weather conditions in
Latvia are expected to become warmer over the course
of the century and, unlike in some other European
regions, wetter. Gradual increases in average and
maximum air temperatures are projected, as well as an
increase in the frequency and duration of heatwaves
and so-called tropical nights. At the same time, total
annual precipitation is not expected to increase
significantly. Nevertheless, the number of days with
heavy precipitation is expected to rise, often occurring
simultaneously with strong wind gusts or storms. An
increase is also projected in the frequency of socalled "compound" weather events, where extreme
precipitation, winds, floods and other events occur

In assessing the climate risks of the AST Group, it was
taken into account that the infrastructure of both AST
and Conexus is located across a wide area of Latvia
(with AST’s coverage being broader).
In assessing climate risks, the types of climate
hazards specified in Regulation (EU) 2020/852 (the EU
Taxonomy Regulation) and its delegated acts were taken
into account, evaluating the impacts of those hazards
that are characteristic of Latvia and its neighbouring
countries.

Potential climate risks and their impacts were assessed
at the locations of the Group’s most significant assets
(primarily in Riga, where the central offices of both
companies are located; in Krimulda Parish of Sigulda
Municipality, where the Inčukalns UGS facility is located;
as well as in the largest cities that serve as regional
energy supply hubs).
Climate risk identification was carried out for the
following areas and fields of operation:

LE Vilnius

POLIJA

Biomethane input point
Conexus gas transmission and storage infrastructure

Elk

Potential biomethane input point

• Electricity transmission;
• Transmission of natural gas and its alternatives
(e.g., biomethane);
• Storage of natural gas and its alternatives;
• Support functions required to ensure the
aforementioned areas of operation—for example,
management of real estate, vehicle fleet, internal
energy supply and other processes, occupational
health and safety, IT infrastructure, and other areas.
The risks identified were assessed in accordance with

the Group’s climate risk assessment methodology,
which is based on AST’s general risk management
methodology. Each risk is evaluated on a 5-point scale,
assessing its probability, as well as the vulnerability of
the Group or individual companies within the Group
and their assets (i.e. how significant the consequences
may be if the risk materializes—including how it may
affect the availability and security of energy supply
services, the processes and employees of the Group’s
companies, and whether, and to what extent, financial
losses may result from the occurrence of the risk).
In addition, those involved in the climate risk
assessment were asked to evaluate the scope of each
risk, i.e. how broadly the risk may affect the operations
of the company or the Group. If this indicator is higher
than vulnerability, the significance (value) of such a risk
may potentially increase in the future. Unlike traditional
business risks, the probability of climate risks is
assessed over a longer time horizon—the assessment
scale covers a period of up to five years or more.
It should also be noted that at the level of individual
companies, the climate risk assessment may differ (AST
and Conexus use different assessment parameters for
various areas of operation). However, for the purposes
of the sustainability report, climate risk assessments
have been consolidated using a unified assessment
scale adapted to the risk management approach applied
by AST.
In total, 42 potential risks were assessed. Based on the
views of the Group’s management and specialists, the
climate risks that were assessed as critical, high, or
medium at the level of individual Group companies or
the Group as a whole, are summarised below.

=== pdf p.83 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

It can be concluded that the most significant risks in
AST’s operations are physical risks that may cause
damage or disruptions to infrastructure operations
and/or hinder access to infrastructure. In contrast, in
Conexus’s operations—given its role in the natural
gas segment—transition risks are considered to be
more significant, as they could create substantial
challenges related to natural gas demand, pricing, and
infrastructure maintenance costs.
To mitigate the impact of these risks, the Group’s
companies already apply solutions adapted to various
weather conditions (e.g. purchasing equipment capable
of operating efficiently at outdoor temperatures ranging
from −40 to +40 degrees Celsius), plan grid balancing
measures adjusted to the increasing share of renewable
energy sources (particularly in the electricity sector),
and adapt infrastructure to enable the integration of
renewable energy sources into the system (e.g. by
developing biomethane injection points connected to
the overall gas transmission infrastructure). At the same
time, it should be noted that broader adaptation of the
natural gas infrastructure managed by the Subsidiary
(e.g. for hydrogen transmission) would require significant
investments, and such investments currently carry a
high level of risk. Decisions on the development of
hydrogen as an energy resource in Latvia must be taken
at national level, considering the substantial investments
and risks associated with hydrogen infrastructure
development. Meanwhile, despite climate-related
challenges, the stability and security of natural gas
supply currently have a positive effect on energy
market stability by ensuring reliable and uninterrupted
energy supply.

The Parent Company’s transition risks are more closely
related to the availability of financial resources and
their impact on tariffs in order to implement the climate
neutrality plans set by the EU and Latvia, as well as
to new technological solutions and the predictability of
their lifecycles. When planning capital investments in
electricity transmission lines or buildings, construction
climatology standards are taken into account; new
equipment is selected in accordance with IEC
standards, and transformers are designed with the
possibility of adding additional cooling systems.
During flood periods, AST introduces a special
operational regime that ensures enhanced monitoring
and the ability to carry out emergency work. When
planning capital investments, the location of electricity
transmission infrastructure is selected with the aim of
minimising the adverse impacts of climate risks.
Opportunities arising from climate change are mainly
related to the modernisation of substations (including
the replacement of technologies with more
environmentally friendly alternatives, such as more
energy-efficient transformers, more energy-efficient
buildings, the gradual replacement of insulation gas
with alternatives with lower emission factors, and
biodegradable insulating oils), the integration and
use of renewable energy sources in the grid, and
improvements in energy efficiency.

ESRS E1-2

POLICY ON CLIMATERELATED IMPACTS
The Parent Company has developed an Environmental
and Energy Management Policy that describes
the Parent Company’s policy and core principles
regarding climate change mitigation and climate
change adaptation. The Parent Company’s objective
in the field of environmental and energy management
is to continually improve AST’s environmental and
energy performance by implementing the most optimal
technical methods and technologies, reducing resource
losses and the amount of waste generated, emissions
of pollutants into the environment, as well as negative
impacts on climate change and biodiversity. The
principles set out in the policy must also be observed
by AST’s contractual partners when providing services
or delivering supplies to AST—contractual partners are
appropriately informed or instructed (particularly when
they are required to perform work at AST facilities).
Detailed information about AST’s policies is available
in the "Business Conduct Policy and Corporate
Culture" section, which provides information about
policies and other internal regulatory documents
through which AS Augstsprieguma tīkls manages
material sustainability areas. Additional information on
stakeholder engagement can be found in the section on
"Stakeholder Engagement".

Based on the Environmental and Energy Management
Policy, the AST integrated process map includes a
separate Environmental Protection Process, under
the auspices of which monthly monitoring of energy
consumption is carried out, the dynamics of heat
energy and natural gas consumption are measured,
and performance indicators are evaluated—such
as electricity transmission system losses and the
level of CO2 equivalent emissions. Changes in these
indicators are reflected in the Environmental and Energy
Management Report of the respective year.
In addition, in accordance with the policy, an
Environmental Protection and Energy Efficiency
Action Plan is prepared each year. This plan includes
measures aimed at reducing energy consumption,
replacing used resources with more environmentally
and climate-friendly alternatives, and improving energy
efficiency. The action plan identifies the planned
reduction in energy consumption (lifecycle savings in
MWh).
The Subsidiary has developed an Occupational Health,
Safety and Environmental Management Policy, the
objective of which is to establish an effective framework
for the preventive identification and management
of potential risks that may affect the health and
safety of the Subsidiary’s employees, the integrity
of infrastructure, the surrounding environment and
society. This includes the implementation of appropriate
environmental and energy management practices that
contribute to climate change mitigation. The internal
implementation of the policy by the Subsidiary is the
responsibility of the Subsidiary’s Management Board.

83


=== pdf p.84 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

The Subsidiary’s occupational health, safety and
environmental management policy does not specifically
address climate change adaptation or the promotion
of renewable energy use, and stakeholders were not
involved in the policy development process. All Conexus
employees are introduced to the occupational health,
safety and environmental management policy, and
contractors working at the Subsidiary’s facilities are
also informed about its core principles. This introduction
takes place during annual briefings or during initial
induction training. Conexus implements, maintains
and continually improves its management system in
accordance with the guidelines specified in international
standards: LVS ISO 45001:2018 "Occupational health
and safety management systems. Requirements
with guidance for use", LVS EN ISO 14001:2017
"Environmental management systems. Requirements
with guidance for use (ISO 14001:2015)", and LVS
EN ISO 50001:2018 "Energy management systems.
Requirements with guidance for use (ISO 50001:2018)".
The Subsidiary has also approved an Asset
Management Policy, under which a Methane Emissions
Reduction Plan has been developed. This plan is
directly related to the reduction of greenhouse gas
emissions characteristic of the natural gas system.
Detailed information about AST’s policies is available in
the section on "Business Conduct Policy and Corporate
Culture", which provides information on policies and
other internal regulatory documents through which
AS Augstsprieguma tīkls manages material
sustainability areas. Additional information on
stakeholder engagement can be found in the
"Stakeholder Engagement" section.

ESRS E1-3

IMPLEMENTED AND
PLANNED ACTIVITIES
The Parent Company’s core activities focus on
ensuring the availability of electricity by investing in
the maintenance and development of the electricity
transmission system, including connecting renewable
energy sources to the grid. At the same time, the
company ensures that its operations in all day-today processes comply with sustainability principles,
including contributing to the achievement of
environmental sustainability objectives.
Medium- and long-term activities of the Parent Company
are implemented in accordance with the Development
Plan, within the framework of which the following
activities are carried out:
• System interconnections are improved, increasing
system transmission capacity and thereby enabling
electricity producers from renewable energy sources
to connect to the grid;
• Connections to the transmission electricity network
are constructed for electricity producers from
renewable energy sources, with requirements set
for the use of green electrical equipment (instrument
transformers and power circuit breakers) in the
construction of these connections;
• Existing 110 kV substations are reconstructed in order

to ensure resilient and secure electricity transmission
services, while moving towards the use of more
environmentally friendly equipment in substations;
• Power transformers and autotransformers are
replaced, thereby significantly reducing electricity
transmission losses.
The Parent Company’s annual environmental and
energy efficiency activities are compiled into a single
Environmental Protection and Energy Efficiency
Action Plan, which includes the replacement of power
transformers, energy efficiency improvements in
buildings and engineering networks (including lighting),
and the resulting energy savings both annually and over
the lifecycle of the measures. To reduce pollution risks
and adverse environmental impacts, systems for the
collection and drainage of oil from defects and accidents
are renewed and improved. Implementation reports
are prepared on the execution of the action plans,
also identifying financial sources, including AST’s own
funding, co-financing from EU funds, and financing from
renewable energy developers. Detailed information on
sustainable financial instruments used—green bonds—
is provided in the section "Overview of Green Projects".
Additional information can be found in the section
"Compliance with EU Taxonomy Requirements".
The Parent Company’s most significant activities in
2025 are as follows:
• Digital4 substation Kuldīga, total projected investments
EUR 5.8 million, including EUR 1.9 million invested
in 2025. EU co-financing has been attracted to the
project within the framework of the Recovery and

Resilience Mechanism. Construction of the substation
completed in 2025;
• Green5 substation Carnikava, total projected
investments EUR 2.7 million, including EUR
0.9 million invested in 2025. EU co-financing has
been attracted to the project within the framework
of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism.
Construction of the substation completed in 2025;
• Replacement of autotransformers at substations
TEC-1 and Imanta, total estimated investments EUR
10.1 million, including EUR 0.1 million in 2025. The
projects are planned to be completed in 2026;
• Reconstruction of AST office and production
premises and construction of a data centre at
Dārzciema iela 86, Riga. Total estimated investments
of EUR 47.6 million, including EUR 15.9 million
invested in 2025. The project has attracted EU cofinancing within the framework of the Recovery and
Resilience Mechanism. It is expected that as a result
of the project implementation, the average annual
energy consumption in buildings will decrease by
27.64%, including the total possible energy reduction,
taking into account the energy produced by solar
panels, will decrease by 53.55%. The project is due to
be completed in 2027;
• Environmental impact assessment for the
construction of a new 330 kV PTL from Ventspils–
Brocēni–Varduva. The total environmental impact
assessment costs are planned to be up to EUR
0.9 million, with EUR 0.5 million invested in 2025.
The assessment is planned to be completed in 2026.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

The environmental impact assessment is being
carried out with the financial support of the European
Union RePowerEU.
In addition to its own projects, AST also provides
connections for renewable energy producers, integrating
their generation capacity into the overall electricity
transmission network. Accordingly, AST indirectly
contributes to the decarbonisation efforts of the entire
energy supply sector and its resource users. In 2025,
six such connections were established, and two existing
connections were modified, enabling the connection of
505.8 MWh of capacity to the transmission network.
Another indirectly related measure is the study on
rainwater use and the development of a standard
project. At the end of 2024, a study on the use of
rainwater at substations and the development of a
standard project was initiated. As a result of the study,
it is planned to identify solutions for using rainwater in
substation water supply systems. The collection and
use of rainwater provides an opportunity to conserve
water resources, thereby supporting sustainable
development goals. In the initial phase of the project,
EUR 71 thousand have been allocated for investment in
the study and the development of the standard project.
The implementation of such a solution would reduce
the consumption of water resources and the resources
required for their supply.

In the digital substation pilot project, AST integrates
solutions (including monitoring sensors) that allow for
remote control of necessary maintenance, renovation,
and replacement work, thus ensuring more efficient

4

• Reduced direct methane emissions by 70% from
2020 levels by 2028;

construction of a new 330 kV Estonia–Latvia fourth
interconnection in the sea are planned to begin in 2026.
To support its own operations, the company also plans
to purchase new vehicles, including electric vehicles and
hybrid vehicles.

ESRS E1-4

CLIMATE-RELATED
TARGET INDICATORS
Climate-related targets in the Group’s operations are
measured against 2020, which is the first year for which
Scope 1 and Scope 2 emissions data are available for
both Group companies. In 2025, the first calculations for
Scope 3 were carried out; therefore, 2025 will be set as
the base year for Scope 3 emissions reduction targets.
The Parent Company had previously set a target
to achieve an average annual reduction of total
GHG emissions of 3% compared to the 2020 level,
primarily through environmental and energy efficiency
improvement measures. However, at the time this target
was set, AST had not yet completed comprehensive
calculations of GHG emissions, nor fully assessed the
detailed impact caused by the increase in electricity
transmission losses, including those related to the
desynchronisation from BRELL and synchronisation
with the European electricity grid. Taking this into
account, AST has prepared a draft emissions reduction
plan containing a detailed assessment of the current
situation, the measures to be implemented, and target
indicators for the coming years. It is expected that the
plan will be approved in the first half of 2026 and will
serve as the basis for future progress reporting.
The Subsidiary has set a target to achieve a 30%
reduction in GHG emissions (t CO2 eq.) by 2028
compared to the 2020 baseline level. The scope of this

target covers Scope 1 emissions, which the Subsidiary
currently calculates, i.e. CO2 and CH4 emissions from
combustion equipment and technological processes
(expressed together as CO2 eq.). The Subsidiary
calculates the emissions volume annually (t CO2 eq.),
and the GHG emissions reduction target is expressed
as a percentage, taking into account the Subsidiary’s
segmentation, emission source intensity and the type of
GHG emissions (CO2 and CH4).
In 2024, the Subsidiary concluded that the target for
GHG emissions reduction by 2028 is –30%. Interim
targets are identified annually; however, they may
change from year to year until 2028 and are therefore
not specified individually. For example, the target for
2024 was set at –6%, but calculations carried out in
2025 showed that between the baseline year 2020 and
2024 a reduction of –11% in GHG emissions (t CO2 eq.)
had already been achieved.
The reduction of CO2 emissions depends on natural
gas consumption and the technological operation of
equipment, which in turn depends on the preferences
and needs of stakeholders – transmission and storage
users. Storage users are informed about the natural gas
consumption coefficient, and this information is publicly
available. The reduction of CH4 emissions can be
achieved through specific improvements to technological
processes and is calculated in detail depending on the
Subsidiary’s segmentation and the operational intensity
of emission sources. The targets are set in accordance
with the European Green Deal and the Regulation
on the reduction of methane emissions in the energy
sector.

In order to plan GHG emission reductions, possible
technologies for reducing methane emissions have
been assessed for each source, and potential
emission reductions have been calculated for both
gas transmission and for the Inčukalns UGS facility.
Measures to reduce carbon dioxide emissions have
been implemented until 2024, while methane emission
reduction measures have been implemented since 2021.
The measures assessed as having the most significant
potential for reducing GHG emissions in order to
achieve the 30% reduction by 2028 include:
• Replacement of cylinder seals of the gas pumping
units at Compressor Station No.2 of the Inčukalns
UGS facility with low-emission seals (by 2024) to
reduce methane emissions;
• Procurement of a mobile gas pumping unit for the
Inčukalns UGS facility (by 2026) to reduce methane
emissions;
• Procurement of a mobile gas pumping unit for gas
transmission (by 2026) to reduce methane emissions
• Reconstruction of the start systems of the gas
pumping units at Compressor Station No. 2 of the
Inčukalns UGS facility (by 2027) to reduce methane
emissions.
In 2026, upon approval of the Group’s climate
transition plan, new or revised targets will be
established. Information on the targets and the progress
towards achieving them will be reported in future
sustainability reports.
