=== pdf p.95 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

GHG emissions were calculated in accordance with the
methodological guidelines of the GHG Protocol, as well
as the Group’s internal methodological guidelines for
calculating all three emission scopes, which were drawn
up in 2025.
A financial control approach was used in the emissions
calculations, i.e. accounting for emissions related to the
operations of both companies consolidated in the annual
report. Emissions accounting covers the same period
as other information included in this report – from 1
January to 31 December 2025.
Quantitative data are obtained from billing documents
and internal data registers. In order to ensure accurate
emissions calculations based on actual data, emission
factors obtained from reliable sources are used in the
calculations. These include emission factor databases,
national regulations and developed methodologies
– Cabinet Regulation No. 42 of 23 January 2018
"Methodology for Calculating Greenhouse Gas
Emissions", the methodology of the State Limited
Liability Company Latvian Environment, Geology and
Meteorology Centre, the Group’s internal methodology,
as well as DEFRA and IPCC sources. References to the
specific sources used are included in the calculations.
When selecting factors, the specific characteristics
of the Group companies are taken into account.
For example, Conexus’s methane emissions are
calculated using internally developed methodologies
for technological losses of natural gas, as well as
through direct emission measurements and calculations
using specific emission factors. However, for emission
categories common to both companies (e.g. electricity
consumption, heat consumption, transport emissions,

and indirect Scope 3 emissions), a unified approach
to the selection of emission factors and calculation
methods was developed in 2025.
AST Scope 1 emissions consist of transport emissions,
emissions from heating equipment and diesel
generators, emissions from refrigerant leakage, and,
specific to transmission system components, emissions
from SF6 gas and its mixtures due to leakage. Under
normal conditions, leakage of SF6 gas from electrical
equipment does not occur. Nevertheless, like any other
equipment, electrical equipment may have defects
that create a risk of leakage. Due to the high emission
factor of SF6, even small leaks can result in a significant
amount of CO2 equivalent emissions. In 2025, no SF6 or
SF6 mixture leaks were recorded.
Conexus’s main Scope 1 emissions (CO2, CH4 and
refrigerant emissions) are related to natural gas storage
and natural gas transmission. These include direct
methane emissions that occur during repair works and
daily operations of natural gas transmission and storage
systems, carbon dioxide and incomplete combustion
emissions from combustion equipment at gas regulation
stations, combustion equipment including compressors
used to ensure UGS processes, and combustion
equipment used for heating purposes.
The Group’s Scope 1 GHG emissions include the
Subsidiary’s proportional share of emissions from
regulated emission trading systems (%).
The Group’s Scope 2 emissions consist of electricity
consumption and heat supply from district heating
networks, as well as the Parent Company’s electricity

transmission losses, which represent the most
significant source of Scope 2 emissions. Although
emissions have generally tended to decrease, the
operation of synchronisation equipment is expected
to increase transmission losses and, consequently,
emissions.
In 2025, in accordance with the GHG Protocol
Corporate Value Chain (Scope 3) Standard, which
supplements the Greenhouse Gas Protocol (GHGP),
the Group initiated the calculation of Scope 3 GHG
emissions, including the following Scope 3 categories:
• Category 1 - Purchased goods and services;
• Category 2 - Capital goods (fixed assets);
• Category 3 - Activities related to fuel and energy use
(not included in scope 1 and 2 GHG emissions);
• Category 5 - Waste generated as a result of
operations;
• Category 6 - Business trips;
• Category 7 - Employee travel to/from work.
Data for emissions calculations in the categories above
are obtained from the procurement databases of both
Group companies, financial records, waste management
records, energy consumption data and human
resources management systems. For the calculation
of Category 7, an estimate of activity data was carried
out, namely an employee survey was conducted to
determine their daily mobility patterns for commuting

to and from work, the actual survey results obtained
from respondents were applied to the total employee
population.
Assessing the materiality of the categories, the potential
volume of emissions, and their relevance to the Group’s
areas of activity, the following Scope 3 categories
deemed immaterial by the Group or not applicable
to its operations have not been included in the GHG
emissions calculations:
• Category 4 – Upstream transportation and
distribution. The costs of delivering purchased goods
are included in Categories 1 and 2, as delivery costs
are incorporated into procurement prices; separate
contracts with logistics companies are not concluded;
• Category 8 – Leased assets. The volume is not
material, and the emissions are already accounted
for and included in the Scope 1 and Scope 2 GHG
emissions calculations (e.g. emissions resulting from
the use of leased office premises);
• Category 9 – Downstream transportation and
distribution. The AST Group is a service provider
that delivers services using infrastructure rather than
distributing physical products through traditional
logistics. According to the GHG Protocol, Category
9 relates to the transportation of physical products
to customers. In the AST Group’s business model,
electricity and natural gas are transmitted through
transmission networks, which are already reflected in
Scope 1 and Scope 2 emissions, and "downstream
logistics" outside the transmission infrastructure does
not exist. Considering the specifics of the sector,
95


=== pdf p.96 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

Scope 3 Category 9 is not applicable to the AST Group;
• Downstream value chain emissions related to product
processing, use and end-of-life treatment (Categories
10–12) are not accounted for and are not applicable
to the Group’s operations, because neither AST nor
Conexus produces or sells finished products;
• Category 13 (Leased assets) – Electricity consumption
related to assets leased by AST is included in Scope
2 emissions, whereas other leased assets are either
not associated with energy consumption or such
consumption is immaterial, taking into account the
nature of the leased assets;

on actual and estimated data, Scope 3 calculations
use emission factors obtained from reliable sources,
including emission factor databases such as DEFRA,
Open CEDA by Watershed or other data sources.
In calculating Scope 3 emissions, the Group applied
a significant assumption. Specifically, for AST purchases
of construction in progress—which represent 74% of
Scope 3, Category 2 emissions and 59% of total Scope
3 emissions—a single conservative emission factor of
0.32 kg CO2e per EUR 1 was applied. This approach
was used without conducting an individual assessment
of the underlying cost components of construction
in progress.

Given the current level of data quality and availability,
most of the AST Group’s Scope 3 calculations are based
on secondary data (i.e. data not obtained directly from
suppliers or partners, but estimated based on activity
volumes and/or costs and the corresponding emission
factors from internationally recognised databases applied
to those volumes). The transition to the primary data
method will be gradual, initially focusing on positions with
a significant volume of emissions (estimated based on the
secondary data method approach).

In accordance with the IPCC and GHG Protocol
guidelines, the analysis conducted by Climatiq, as
well as studies on the potential margin of error of
measurement instruments and other public sources,
the overall uncertainty of Scope 3 GHG emissions
calculations in the reporting year is estimated within the
range of ±30–35%, considering the use of secondary
emission factors and data limitations in certain
categories. In contrast, the uncertainty of Scope 1
and Scope 2 emissions is assessed at a lower level –
approximately ±10–15% – as the calculations are often
based on actual consumption data, while emission
factors are applied from sectoral or national databases
rather than supplier-specific factors.

A mixed methodology is applied for the calculation of
Scope 3 emissions, with an expenditure‑based approach
used for Categories 1 and 2, and an activity‑based
approach applied to Categories 3, 5, 6, and 7.
In order to ensure accurate emissions calculations based

The uncertainty assessment performed confirms that
the uncertainty identified does not significantly change
the overall emissions structure and is suitable for
representing emission volumes and setting
climate targets.

• Category 14 (Franchises), Category 15 (Investments) –
these categories are not applicable because the Group
does not carry out such activities.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT
ESRS E4

CARE FOR BIODIVERSITY
AND ECOSYSTEMS
Within the framework of the Environmental Protection
process, when assessing environmental aspects, the
Parent Company has identified several medium-level
risks affecting flora and fauna (within the meaning of the
ESRS standards, these risks are treated as the existing
or potential impact of AST on biodiversity). These
include:
• Restrictions on biodiversity during the clearing of
power transmission line corridors, including risks
to birds and their nests. The impact on biodiversity
is most often associated with emergency works,
the timing of which cannot be planned in advance.
In cases where works can be planned in advance,
natural processes are taken into account to as great
an extent as possible (e.g. avoiding intensive works
during the active bird nesting period
• The potential negative impact of electric and magnetic
fields on the surrounding environment, including
birds, bats and other living organisms. However,
this impact is largely theoretical, as electromagnetic
field measurements indicate that radiation levels
within power line protection zones are several
times lower than the permissible limits. Even in
the immediate vicinity of high-voltage power lines,
magnetic field radiation is usually within the range
of 25 to 30 microtesla (µT), while at the boundary

of the protection zone it approaches zero, whereas
the permitted level in Latvia and globally is 100
microtesla (µT), which is 3–4 times higher than the
levels typically measured on AST lines. Moreover, the
impact of electromagnetic fields on biodiversity has
not been sufficiently studied worldwide to determine
whether this factor affects flora and fauna, and if so,
to what extent.
The impact on biodiversity may also occur when birds
come into contact with high-voltage lines. In accordance
with the conditions of the permit issued by the authority,
AST reports annually to the Nature Conservation
Agency on cases where birds have died for this reason.
The Group’s operations do not contribute to land
degradation, desertification or soil compaction, given
that its activities mainly take place within the territories
of already existing infrastructure and do not alter the
condition or composition of the soil in a way that would
create an impact on biodiversity. Therefore, more
detailed information on this aspect is not included
in the report.
The Group has not developed a comprehensive policy
on biodiversity, as this area only affects the Group’s
operations to a limited extent. However, in 2025, AST
developed a procedure that sets out actions in cases
involving the removal of nests of specially protected bird
species or the disturbance of individuals. This procedure
has been coordinated with the Nature Conservation
Agency and defines permissible actions in the vicinity of
nests of the white stork, osprey and other bird species,
including restrictions on planned repair works (not
applicable to emergency repairs that cannot be planned
in advance, such as those following storms or other

emergency situations). It also establishes procedures
in cases where birds have built nests on 330 kV or
110 kV power transmission lines or electrical
installations, where a hazardous situation has arisen
that poses a risk to the bird’s life.
In addition, in 2025, AST approved amendments to the
Standard Instruction for the Operation of 110–330 kV
Overhead Power Lines, stipulating that when planning
and carrying out line maintenance works, the company
and its subcontractors must assess the potential impact
on the environment and the local community and, where
possible, plan works so as to avoid or minimise such
impacts. This includes taking natural processes into
account (e.g. nesting periods), as well as considering
the possibility of carrying out works using specialised
equipment that causes less disturbance or using
manual tools.
During the planning of capital investment projects, the
Parent Company carries out an environmental impact
assessment or an initial environmental screening
(hereinafter – EIA) in accordance with regulatory
requirements. If the initial screening identifies a
potentially significant environmental impact, a full EIA is
carried out. For example, the initial public consultation
for the project "Construction of the 330 kV power
transmission line Ventspils – Brocēni – Varduva in
the territory of Latvia" took place in November 2024,
while repeated meetings were held in March 2025 in
the municipalities of Saldus, Kuldīga and Ventspils. In
addition, any member of the local community may ask
questions, express concerns and request information
outside the EIA process.

The Subsidiary’s infrastructure, which has been in
operation since the 1960s, including the Inčukalns UGS
facility and gas pipelines with a total length of 1,190 km,
crosses both Natura 2000 sites and areas sensitive
in terms of biodiversity. As the Subsidiary does not
construct or expand new infrastructure in such areas,
no negative impact has been identified. Operational
protection zones are established for infrastructure
facilities, which border these territories, but no physical
impact on these areas has been identified. In practice,
all maintenance and servicing activities take place only
within the boundaries of the protection zones. However,
the Subsidiary has not yet compiled detailed information
on the exact locations where the protection zones
of the Inčukalns UGS infrastructure border protected
natural areas.
Since 2024, the Subsidiary has also informed the Nature
Conservation Agency about planned works in the
protection zones of its infrastructure that border areas of
particular importance for biodiversity and ecosystems.
The recommendations of the Nature Conservation
Agency are taken into account whenever possible. For
example, maintenance works are not carried out during
the bird nesting period in order to avoid exposing birds
to the negative effects of noise. In 2025, one letter
was received from the Nature Conservation Agency
granting permission to carry out the planned works
and specifying conditions and recommendations on
how they should be performed to minimise impacts on
natural values such as: "To reduce the impact on birds
nesting in forests, the Agency invites compliance with
a quiet period from 1 April to 30 June throughout the
planned activity area with respect to works associated
with increased noise levels."

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

Despite the impacts described above, in some cases
the Subsidiary’s infrastructure contributes to the
preservation of biodiversity. The results of the Nature
Census project (2017–2019) indicate that infrastructure
facilities are often located in areas that have been
designated as biodiversity-sensitive territories. This can
be explained by the fact that activities around critical
infrastructure are restricted or even prohibited if they
could disturb the development of biodiversity. As a
result, a favourable environment is created for various
species to live and reproduce. For example, according
to the information available in the OZOLS database, in
2018 an EU-protected grassland habitat – Floodplain
meadows (habitat code 6450) – was recorded in a
territory managed by Conexus.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

MEASURES IMPLEMENTED
IN 2025
AST has long implemented measures to reduce
negative impacts, particularly with regard to bird
species. In previous years, using drones 580 bird
diverters have been installed on 330 kV power
transmission lines. These devices improve the visibility
of the lines and help birds to notice them in time.
In 2025, a price inquiry was conducted with the aim
of procuring special "propeller" structures designed
to prevent birds from landing on power line elements.
This was done in order to reduce the likelihood of
disturbances and, at the same time, the need to disturb
birds during their natural lifecycle if they choose these
locations to settle and start building nests. A similar
price inquiry was also conducted for the installation of
metal caps on overhead line towers. Such measures are
implemented to induce birds to choose safer locations
for themselves and their offspring in advance, where
they are not exposed to electric shocks or the need to
relocate nests due to repair works or other technical
requirements.
In 2025, the environmental impact assessment
continued for the 330 kV overhead power transmission
line from Ventspils–Brocēni–Varduva.
Meanwhile, pilot projects launched in 2024 aimed
at developing natural meadows at Bišuciems and
Stīpnieki substations also continued. The objective

of these projects is to promote the conservation and
development of biodiversity across an area of 1,800
square metres in Riga and Mārupe.
The Subsidiary has not implemented specific measures
in this area, as its operations are mainly related to
underground gas pipelines and the extent of activities
above ground that could disturb bird, animal or plant
species is limited.

OTHER INFORMATION
Currently, the Group has not defined specific targets
related to biodiversity. After the development of the
climate transition plan by the end of 2026, it will be
clarified whether such targets will be established.
The financial impact that may arise from biodiversityrelated risks and opportunities has not been assessed
as material in the double materiality assessment.
Therefore, this information is not included in the
annual report.
In accordance with amendments to European Union
legislation adopted in 2025, the Group may also present
the information required in this section in a simplified
form (phase-in approach) for 2025 and 2026, and this
report applies such an approach.

CIRCULAR ECONOMY
ESRS E5
According to the double materiality assessment, the
Group’s material subtopic in the Circular Economy
section is incoming resources rather than waste.
The Parent Company focuses on the efficient use
of resources, particularly in the maintenance and
development of electricity transmission network
infrastructure. The main resources used are electricity,
heat energy, fuel, and natural gas (energy resource
consumption is discussed in more detail in the section
on climate change), as well as electrical equipment,
operational materials and IT equipment.
The Parent Company integrates circular economy
principles into its quality management systems,
complying with the ISO 9001, ISO 14001 and ISO
50001 standards. These standards define the
company’s approach to efficient resource use and
waste management. Information is compiled annually
in the Integrated Environmental and Energy Efficiency
Report, which reflects the measures implemented
by AST in managing resource consumption and
generated waste. The Parent Company also applies
green procurement principles that promote sustainable
resource consumption. More information on the
procurement policy can be found in the table included
in the section of the report focusing on "Business
conduct policy and corporate culture", while information
on procurement criteria and green procurement can be
found in the section on "Management of relationships

with suppliers". External stakeholders are not involved
in the development of these systems and documents,
as the focus is primarily on AST’s own operational
opportunities and directions in the context of the circular
economy. Parallel to this, information that may be
required by external stakeholders, such as suppliers, is
published on the AST website.
The Group currently has no defined targets regarding
waste management and the volume of recoverable
resources. However, the Parent company has
established procedures for the management and
reuse of frequently recoverable materials. In particular,
a procedure for the acquisition and sale of ferrous
and non-ferrous metal scrap and residues has been
approved. This procedure stipulates that all metal
scrap and residues generated in AST’s operations (e.g.
during the dismantling of equipment, facilities, etc.) are
handed over to an appropriate buyer who manages and
sells these materials further. According to international
studies, metal waste can be used practically indefinitely;
however, AST does not monitor whether and where
the metal scrap handed over by the company is
subsequently used.
In order to reduce the consumption of its own resources,
the company also ensures the treatment and reuse
of insulation oils used in various equipment, thereby
reducing the volume that must be disposed of as final
waste, as well as the volume that would otherwise
need to be newly procured or replaced with alternative
insulating materials. In accordance with the AST Oil
Management Procedure, all oils used in electrical
equipment are recorded and, after the end of their useful
lifecycle, assessed and, depending on the possibilities
100


=== pdf p.101 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

for reuse, treated and reused for AST’s needs,
transferred for recycling to a qualified third party, or sent
for final disposal.
Other incoming or reusable resources are not
specifically recorded or purposefully managed in the
context of the circular economy within AST.
The volumes of recoverable resources (metal scrap and
residues, waste electrical and electronic equipment,
insulation oils) are recorded based on delivery notes and
invoices from contractual partners. In accordance with
the regulatory framework of Latvia, a waste transport
registration card – delivery note (hereinafter also
referred to as a delivery note) – is additionally prepared.
Depending on their assessment, insulation oils are
regenerated (oil quality indicators are restored) in
the oil management facility and reused in electrical
equipment or transferred to a contractual partner for
the production of other products and materials. The
volumes of oil circulation vary from year to year, which
is explained by differences in the amount of oil obtained
from dismantled equipment, the demand for operational
needs in a particular year, and oil surpluses that are
sold to a contractual partner.

8

SOCIAL
INFORMATION
ESRS IRO-2

GROUP EMPLOYEES
ESRS 2 SBM-2, SBM-3, S1-1,
S1-2, S1-3, S1-4
Considering the dynamic development of the Group,
the processes related to integration into the EU
internal electricity market, synchronisation with
European electricity transmission networks and
digital transformation, the achievement of the Group’s
sustainability and strategic objectives would not be
possible without engaged and professional employees.
Employees are the Group’s most important
strategic resource.

Employee composition and specifics of
operations
All Group employees are permanently or temporarily
employed staff. In 2025, the Group does not
permanently employ self-employed persons or
freelancers.
AST employs 554 employees, who are mainly highly
qualified engineering and technical staff with long-term
practical experience in the energy sector. Employees
are characterised by a high level of professionalism,
precision and responsibility for critical infrastructure.
Specialists and managers continually improve their
knowledge and competencies in order to ensure
the safe, stable and efficient operation of the
electricity system.

The Group promote a safe and non-discriminatory
working environment, with equal employment conditions
and treatment for all employees. Employees form
a team that ensures motivated, flexible, loyal and
professional work in the interests of AST. They are
professionals in their respective fields who continuously
develop their professional skills and competencies
and help new colleagues integrate into the team by
sharing their professional experience and practice.
Employees are open to change and take responsibility
for the high-quality performance of delegated duties in
order to ensure the achievement of AST’s objectives
and maintain a positive corporate reputation and image
in interactions with representatives of stakeholders.
Mutual relations are based on general ethical principles,
honesty and mutual respect, and conflicts of interest
are not tolerated.
Conexus employs 363 employees, who are mainly
highly qualified engineering and technical staff with
long-term practical experience in the gas transmission
and storage sector. Managers of operational areas
and specialists possess in-depth knowledge in the
design, operation and safety management of gas
infrastructure, enabling the company to implement
complex technological processes efficiently and safely
in accordance with international standards and industry
best practices.

102


=== pdf p.103 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT
Respect for human rights
The Group needs to ensure that employees are
provided with a working environment in which they feel
free to communicate, express their opinions, ideas and
proposals, and provide feedback. The Group supports
the protection of internationally recognised human
rights principles, including the Universal Declaration of
Human Rights, the International Labour Organisation
Declaration on Fundamental Principles and Rights
at Work, the United Nations Convention against
Corruption, and the Ten Principles of the United Nations
Global Compact.
The activities of the Group’s companies, including
their status as state-owned or partially state-controlled
enterprises, as well as the labour legislation in force in
the country, do not allow the use of human trafficking,
forced labour or child labour, nor the possibility of such
practices in any area of the Group’s operations.
During the reporting period, no cases of human rights
violations related to labour were identified in the Group’s
operations.

Employee engagement and satisfaction
Employees and managers of the Group are
professionals in their respective fields who build both
internal relationships and relationships with stakeholders
involved in the core operations with fidelity to the values
of each company.
The Group’s management recognises that employees
with diverse competences and backgrounds represent
a value that enables the company to develop and

achieve new objectives. For a long time, initiatives
reflecting the Group’s core values and the principle of
equal treatment have been implemented and supported,
fostering a diverse and inclusive working environment
for all employees. These activities are carried out
within the existing budget and work plan. Each year,
AST organises various training programmes to
strengthen the development and growth of employees’
competencies, as well as informal events such as sports
games for employees with the participation of their
family members.
An employee engagement and satisfaction survey
is also organised every year, within which the
management of both Group companies identifies both
positively assessed and less favourable aspects of the
working environment and implements improvements in
company operations accordingly.
In AST’s annual employee engagement survey in 2025,
a record-high response rate of 90% was achieved,
indicating that employees feel safe to express their
opinions and be involved.

Safety in the working environment and
business continuity
Group employees are affected by both positive and
negative factors. Positive influences include ergonomic
working conditions, training opportunities, possibilities
for professional development, and various benefits
provided under the collective agreement. In the case
of the Parent Company, these include, for example, the
review of the monthly base salary, which is calculated
according to a defined formula that takes into account

the average value between the previous year’s inflation
rate and the percentage increase in the average
monthly salary in the energy sector.
By offering open-ended employment contracts, benefits
for various life situations, and by not planning reductions
in the number of employees, the Group not only ensures
the protection of employee rights, but also enhances
employees’ long-term sense of security.
In the Conexus annual employee engagement survey,
a question on employees’ sense of safety while
performing their duties is also included. In this survey,
92% of respondents indicated that they feel safe.
Given that the Group provides strategically important
services, ensuring their continuity is essential.
Therefore, Group employees—especially those
responsible for the operation of energy supply
equipment and service continuity (such as electrical
technicians, electricians, and similar roles)—are often
involved in tackling the consequences of various
weather-related events. The Group must also ensure
a high level of employee availability and readiness to
maintain service continuity.
The Group complies with the requirements of the
Labour Law, ensuring appropriate working and rest
periods, paying overtime, and providing additional
benefits in accordance with the Collective Agreement.
To support employee well-being, the Group provides
accommodation close to work sites for employees
whose job duties involve travelling to nearby or distant
operational locations. However, due to the nature of
such work, employees’ interests may be negatively
affected, because flexible working arrangements are

not available to many employees, which may influence
work–life balance.
The Group has also developed and approved a list of
critical personnel—both to ensure operational continuity
in the event of a potential national threat (critical
personnel are not subject to mobilisation, continue
working, and are prohibited from terminating their
employment contracts during such periods), as well as
in order to ensure succession planning.
During regular evaluation processes, critical positions
are identified, the absence of which could significantly
affect the continuity of the company’s operations. At
AST, in the evaluation questionnaire the employee’s
manager marks whether a successor has been
designated for the position. Succession planning is also
included in the evaluation questionnaire for colleagues
who have indicated plans to retire, which helps to
ensure operational continuity and preserve critical
competencies.
Conexus has also developed and approved a
succession plan for employees. Succession planning
not only covers the development of department
managers to build the next generation of leaders, but
also the succession of other positions that have a
significant impact on ensuring uninterrupted operations.
The assessment of whether and what kind of impact
on employees may arise from transition plans aimed at
reducing negative environmental impacts and ensuring
greener, climate-neutral operations is linked to the
further development of the Group’s climate transition
plan project prepared in 2025 and the final decision on
the measures to be implemented. Overall, the developed
103


=== pdf p.104 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

draft and the potential emission-reduction measures
included in it do not indicate a significant impact on
employees or their working environment. Detailed
information on whether the approval and implementation
of the climate transition plan will have any impact
on employees will be disclosed in the Group’s 2026
sustainability report.

PERSONNEL-RELATED POLICIES
The Group’s policies regarding employees are based on
the following core values:

The Codes stipulate that the Group’s companies ensure
equal treatment of all employees, without promoting
discrimination regardless of the employee’s age, gender,
race, ethnic origin, religious beliefs, sexual orientation,
political views, family status or other circumstances, as
well as that employees treat one another equally and
with respect.
At the same time, the Codes of Ethics also stipulate
that employees should maintain a positive company
reputation and brand, which is important both in relation
to cooperation partners and clients and to society as
a whole. Mutual relations are built on general ethical
principles, honesty and mutual respect, and situations
involving conflicts of interest are not permitted.

• fair, just and equal treatment of all employees;
• a diversity-inclusive work environment, ensuring equal
opportunities;
• sustainability – developing employee competencies,
transferring knowledge, sharing experience and
ensuring the continuity of relevant activities;
• social dialogue with employee representative
organisations.
The Group’s companies have developed Codes
of Ethics, the purpose of which is to create a unified
set of norms for ethical conduct and to strengthen the
internal culture, business practice and reputation of the
Group’s companies by establishing ethical principles
and internal organisational measures reflecting the
Group’s core values.

The Codes of Ethics of the Parent Company are publicly
available on the websites of the companies.
Violations of the Codes of Ethics or other violations
are identified by Employees by informing their direct
manager, a member of the management board or
by submitting a whistleblower report. The identifying
information of an employee who has reported a
violation of the Code of Ethics or another breach must
not be disclosed and is protected as restricted-access
information, available only to persons involved in the
evaluation of the violation of the Code of Ethics or
another breach.
The Group does not apply any sanctions or other
adverse consequences against an employee who has
reported a violation of the Code of Ethics or another
violation, except in cases where false information has
been knowingly provided.

Recognising the importance of raising issues
related to unethical conduct, the Group and the
Parent Company have established a whistleblowing
system, an ethics committee and a labour dispute
commission. In the Parent Company, a Human
resources management strategy for 2025–2027
was drawn up and approved in 2025. It has been
developed based on the objectives and tasks defined
in the Group’s medium-term operational strategy for
2021–2025 and will be implemented during the new
medium-term strategy period. The strategy ensures
the development, attraction and retention of employees
in order to achieve the company’s overall objectives,
including ensuring the continuity, quality and safety
of the energy supply transmission service, which is
strategically important for national security. It provides
a structured approach to the development of employee
competences, the creation of an effective working
environment and the strengthening of the company’s
internal culture. The strategic areas and objectives form
the basis for sustainable and effective human resources
management, contributing to the positioning of AST as
an attractive and competitive employer, the attraction of
personnel and the long-term growth of the organisation.

The AST strategy includes the following areas of
human resources management: workforce planning,
recruitment and onboarding, performance management
and development, the remuneration system, employer
branding and internal communication.

The strategy includes the most important human
resources management processes and defines
the main tasks and target indicators for each year,
taking into account the assessment of AST’s current
situation, the sustainability material aspects in the field
of human resources management approved by the
AST management board, as well as the results of the
employee engagement survey and the AST employee
development discussion process.

• Development and maintenance of AST traditions;

The main basic principles are based on
the following values:
• Compliance with laws and regulations;
• Acceptance and observance of AST’s mission,
values and ethical principles;
• AST priorities and objectives;
• Honest, fair and equal treatment of all employees;
• An inclusive work environment embracing diversity,
ensuring equal opportunities;
• Sustainability – developing employee competences,
transferring knowledge, sharing experience and
ensuring appropriate succession of activities;

• Social dialogue with employee representative
organisations.
Detailed information on AST’s policies is available in
the section on "Business conduct policy and corporate
culture", which provides information about policies and
other internal regulatory documents.

The aim of the Conexus Personnel and
Remuneration Policy is to ensure effective practices
for addressing human resources management issues
within the Company, in order to implement sustainable
management of internal resource operations, ensuring
competitive and motivating remuneration, as well as
employee succession and competence development.
The main guidelines for human resources management
at Conexus are as follows:
• Professionalism – employees are competent in their
respective fields and are oriented towards acquiring
new skills and abilities, as well as exchanging
knowledge and experience at both national and
international level;
• Quality – employees ensure the performance of
assigned tasks at a high level of quality through
precise, prompt and professional execution of their
duties and a conscientious attitude;
• Development – Conexus supports employees’
professional growth and purposefulness, providing
them, where possible, with appropriate training and
career development opportunities both horizontally
and vertically;
• Stability – Conexus’s operations are stable and
oriented towards long-term development. Conexus
cares for its employees and strengthens their sense
of security regarding stable employment, appropriate
remuneration and social guarantees;
• Prohibition of discrimination – Conexus recognises
the equal role of every person in the development

of the Company regardless of race, skin colour,
gender, religious, political or other beliefs, national
or social origin, property or family status, or other
circumstances. Equal rights are ensured for all
employees.
Selection and evaluation criteria in the recruitment
process in both Group companies are applied equally,
regardless of the candidates’ gender. Candidates
are assessed according to their experience and
skills, and identical selection test tasks are provided
to all candidates. When organising various events,
employees’ different interests, age groups and
family status are taken into account – the programme
of the event is adapted for both children and for older
people. Events are organised from the perspective of
an inclusive working environment, ensuring accessibility
for employees both in Riga and in the regions. In 2025,
various events involving employees were organised,
including sports games, a Midsummer celebration
event, New Employee Day, participation in the Riga Rimi
Marathon, and a fishing event.
To ensure the representation of employees’
interests, the Latvian trade union Enerģija operates in
the Group’s companies. Collective labour agreements
have been concluded, prepared in accordance with the
requirements of the Labour Law and other regulatory
enactments, which include the duties, rights and
guarantees of the Parent Company and the Subsidiary
and their employees. In cases of remediation, various
support measures are provided in accordance with the
provisions of the collective agreement and the available
health insurance and accident insurance policies.
Additional information is provided in the "Stakeholder

engagement" section.
Internal documents for occupational safety management
have also been developed in the Group’s companies in
order to preventively identify and manage potential risks
that could affect the health and safety of employees of
the Group’s companies.

• Determination of effective operational objectives
and measures;
• Maintaining management competences at a high
level;
• High readiness to respond to emergency situations;

The Group pays continual attention to employees’
opinions both regarding workplace safety (work
environment risk assessments involving employees;
employee surveys on related issues) and other
important aspects related to the working environment
that promote employee engagement with the company
and contribute to their dedication. More detailed
information on the formats of employee engagement
and the main issues discussed can be found in the

The Subsidiary has developed an occupational health,

of which is to establish an effective framework for the
preventive identification and management of potential
risks that may affect employee health and safety, the

and society. For its implementation, the following

• Continuous compliance and suitability of

EMPLOYEE ENGAGEMENT
An internal culture and direct communication with
all employees are fostered in the Group’s companies,
providing employees with an understanding of shared
values, objectives and team unity. This section reviews
ongoing activities that the Group’s companies have
introduced to promote employee engagement.
The management boards of the Group’s companies
since 2025 regularly organise online meetings with all
employees on current issues, where employees have
105


=== pdf p.106 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

the opportunity to participate in open discussions and
ask questions. Employees of the Group’s companies
also have access to internal websites where information
relevant to employees is published on a regular basis.
In 2025, AST approved a regulation that provides for
the annual recognition of employees and teams who
make a professional contribution to the company’s
development and act in accordance with the company’s
values. In the Employee of the Year award nominations,
the employee’s performance is evaluated in accordance
with AST’s values – together, responsibly, intelligently
and honestly. For the Team of the Year awards, a
description of the team’s performance is assessed
according to defined criteria – project or team
effectiveness and results, innovations or improvements
in processes, and the impact on company culture and
cooperation. It is planned to present such awards on an
annual basis.
In order to promote understanding and a sense of
belonging to the company, the management of the
Parent Company organises regional meetings with
employees during which employees are informed
about the company’s strategy, objectives and planned
activities, and their opinions are heard and questions
answered, thereby fostering mutual dialogue. In 2025,
in addition to meetings with the Management Board,
the Head of the Human Resources Department
held meetings with each regional structural unit to
discuss current issues related both to remuneration
and the working environment and to hear employees’
suggestions. In addition, meetings of the Human
Resources Department with AST structural units were
organised during the year, where the results of the

engagement survey and its data in each structural
unit were discussed, including both aspects evaluated
positively and areas with room for improvement. It is
planned to continue organising such meetings in the
future.
In order to promote open communication and strengthen
employee engagement, based on the results of the
engagement survey, a new initiative – "Coffee with
added value" – was launched in 2025. This initiative
has been created with the aim of strengthening internal
communication, promoting employee engagement
and a sense of belonging to the company, creating an
opportunity in a direct and open format for employees
to learn about current developments, ask questions
and share ideas, build closer connections between
colleagues and management, and ensure transparent
and two-way information flow. These meetings take
place at least once per quarter and are attended by both
the company’s management and employees.
With a view to facilitating everyday communication,
ensure a unified approach to information, including
regulatory enactments and company updates, learn
employees’ opinions and receive feedback, the Parent
Company uses the internal communication channel
ASTe. In addition, the most important updates related
to employees’ daily work, well-being or company
procedures are communicated via company email or by
organising joint Microsoft Teams online meetings.
At the end of 2024, AST began, continued in 2025, and
plans to continue implementing in the future as a highly
valuable engagement activity, conducting probation
period interviews with new employees – one conducted

in the middle of the probation period followed by another
shortly before the end of the probation period – as well
as surveys to evaluate the adaptation process. Survey
data show that AST’s new colleagues are most satisfied
with the support from their manager, the team and the
information provided (average survey rating 4.9 out of
5). In questions related to the working environment and
culture, the opportunity to learn and receive help when
necessary, as well as the direct manager’s support,
information and availability, received the highest
evaluation (average score 4.8 out of 5 for both factors).
The results of the probation period surveys indicate a
high level of satisfaction among new employees with
the working environment and the integration process.
Average ratings in all main categories exceed 4.5 points
on a five-point scale. The survey data confirm that
the company has a cooperation-based and respectful
culture, with new employees feeling welcome, supported
and already involved in their first weeks of work.
Almost all respondents indicated open communication,
responsive colleagues and a safe environment for
asking questions, which are important prerequisites for
an inclusive working environment. Within the framework
of the new employee integration programme, a similar
approach is also implemented at Conexus.
Conexus employee meetings with the management
board regarding current issues take place once every
three weeks. During these meetings, the management
board informs employees about the most important
aspects of Conexus operations and current events.
The meeting content is supplemented with information
relevant to the workforce about internal processes,
technological development issues, presentations or
discussions. In 2025, particular attention was paid to

security issues in the areas of civil protection, public
information and disinformation analysis, as well as threat
risks in the virtual environment.
In the Group’s companies, an employee satisfaction
survey is conducted once a year, providing all
employees with the opportunity to express their
evaluation and opinion. Accordingly, employees’ views
are taken into account both in the implementation of
further improvements to the working environment and
other aspects of company development, while valuable
information on the effectiveness and value of previous
improvements or changes is also provided from the
employees’ perspective. The survey is conducted
annually, and it is planned to continue its implementation
in the coming years.
The Parent Company organises an annual employee
engagement survey using the TRI*M Employee
Commitment methodology tool. The purpose of
the survey is to obtain an overall overview of the
employee engagement indicator as well as changes
in the evaluation of organisational performance,
identifying strengths and areas for improvement and
determining the main priorities for the company’s further
development and management. In 2025, 90% of all AST
employees contacted participated in the engagement
survey (84% in 2024). The TRIM index remained at
the level of the previous year. In the evaluation of
additional aspects, compared with the previous period,
positive dynamics can be observed in relation to
employee engagement, loyalty, a sense of belonging
to the company and the improvements implemented by
the company. Over the past three years, ratings have
slightly increased in all aspects related to the circulation
106


=== pdf p.107 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

of internal information and communication (timeliness,
availability of information necessary for work, distribution
of responsibilities within the company, etc.). Ratings
are also gradually improving in areas where a need
to improve company practices in engagement and
communication had previously been identified (including
ensuring understanding of the company’s strategy).
The next employee engagement survey is planned for
February 2026.
It is important that after the completion of the annual
employee survey, its results are presented to the
company’s Management Board, the Supervisory Board
and all employees. Subsequently, discussions are
organised with the heads of structural units to review
the obtained data and employee comments and, based
on them, agree on the necessary improvements, which
are then presented to the management board. For
example, in 2025, one significant comment from an
employee concerned the need to improve feedback
and communication between direct managers and
employees. Therefore, in 2025, special training was
organised for managers to develop these skills. As a
result of the employee survey, remuneration has also
been reviewed, the provision of work clothing has been
expanded, new internal communication solutions have
been introduced (e.g. see above regarding "Coffee with
added value"), and remote meetings on various current
topics have been organised, etc. When starting a new
survey cycle, employees are provided with summarised
information about the improvements and innovations
introduced in the previous period based on their
evaluations and comments.
In the Subsidiary’s 2025 employee satisfaction and

engagement survey, 310 employees participated,
representing 87% of all Conexus employees contacted
(65% in 2024). Compared with previous years, the
survey methodology was changed – in 2025 the
survey was conducted similarly to that of the Parent
Company, using the internationally recognised and
widely applied TRIM™ Employee Commitment method.
The survey used a quantitative approach complemented
by qualitative analysis in order to obtain a deeper
understanding of employees’ opinions and experiences.
The survey results continue to demonstrate high
employee engagement, loyalty and motivation, and the
Subsidiary maintains a stable situation with consistently
high employee satisfaction indicators. As in the previous
year, employees highly evaluate the organisation of
work and internal communication, while further positive
growth in ratings has been observed in the areas of
leadership and planning. The culture of the Subsidiary
is also rated slightly higher than before. All suggestions
expressed in the survey are evaluated and taken into
account in planning further improvements to processes
and the working environment.

EMPLOYEE DEVELOPMENT
The Group is proud of its professional, highly qualified,
competent and motivated team of employees, which
contributes to the company’s development and the
achievement of strategic objectives. At the same time,
the Group’s companies also foster the development of
their employees by providing diverse opportunities for
growth.

The following section reviews ongoing activities
introduced by the Group’s companies to promote
employee development.
One of the important instruments used by the Parent
Company to promote employees’ career development
and sustainability is internal competitions. In 2025,
several structural changes took place in the company,
as a result of which both closed and open internal
competitions were organised. Closed competitions
were held in newly established structural units that
required very specific knowledge and experience –
competencies possessed only by a particular team
(e.g. in the Electricity Accounting Group and the Market
Development Group). Closed competitions were
organised four times, resulting in career advancement
for three men who had started working in the company
between 2000 and 2018, and one woman who has
been working at AST since 2022. In the open internal
competitions, four men (according to Horizon data –
they started working in the company between 2012
and 2018) and three women (according to Horizon
data – they started working in the company between
1994 and 2001) participated and were selected for the
respective positions. This distribution demonstrates that
career development opportunities through participation
in internal competitions are not limited to experienced
or long-serving employees – opportunities are
available both to long-standing and new employees.
This highlights AST’s inclusive approach and its
interest in developing employees at all levels of
experience. Internal competitions not only provide
employees with vertical but also horizontal professional
growth opportunities, allowing them to move to other
structural units. Growth opportunities are provided at

various levels, and in 2025, four of the eleven internal
competitions were for management-level positions.
Employees have also increasingly chosen to participate
in AST’s external competitions, winning four of them.
Vacant position competitions at Conexus are initially
available only to employees, thereby motivating their
development. The average length of service in the
company is 14 years.
In 2025, AST’s position map was approved. At the
beginning of 2026, it is planned that AST’s remuneration
policy will be approved, establishing salary ranges for
each job group. This process ensures a unified and
clear approach to the remuneration system. Bonuses
are granted based on the completion of work tasks in
accordance with the specified deadlines, quality and
quantity. In addition, communication, cooperation skills,
initiative, responsiveness, development of knowledge
and skills, as well as compliance with ethical standards
and ensuring the confidentiality of information is
evaluated.
In 2025, AST participated in the employer image survey
in the Baltic States organised by Kantar, where AST
was recognised by students as one of the TOP 20
employers. It also ranked among the TOP 50 employers
in the TOP Employer survey organised by Alma Career
CV-Online. In addition, in 2025, the employer branding
agency Instar, a member of the European Association
of Employer Branding Agencies (EAEBA), conducted
a Baltic-wide survey on employee expectations and
employer reputation, collecting nearly 9,000 responses.
In Latvia, where the survey was conducted for the first
time in 2025, 783 employees and students participated,
107

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

whose evaluation ranks AST among the TOP 10 most
attractive employers in the private sector (according to
the employer classification used by the study’s authors).
Conexus implements a New Employee Integration
Programme. The integration programme is comprised of
a set of activities aimed at supporting a new employee
in order to enable them to integrate into the team as
quickly as possible and acquire the knowledge and
skills necessary for the effective performance of work
duties. During the programme, under the supervision of
a mentor appointed by the manager, the new employee
becomes acquainted with the Company’s culture,
values and objectives, job responsibilities, colleagues
and the working environment, as well as the tools
and information necessary for work. In 2025, through
the implementation of the New Employee Integration
Programme, 25 new employees were successfully
integrated into work.
At AST, every new employee is provided with a first
working day programme and a New Employee Day,
where new colleagues are introduced to the work of the
Management Board, areas of responsibility, structural
units and the specifics of the company’s operations.
This activity was launched in 2025, and it is planned to
be continued in the future.
In order to attract and retain employees, employees
of the Group’s companies are provided with a benefits
package, regular salary reviews, performance
evaluations resulting in quarterly, semi-annual or annual
bonuses (premiums), and employee development is
promoted through training and succession planning.
As in the previous year, in 2025 the Group continued

its development direction regarding the development of
employee competences and the availability of training,
in order to ensure both the future transfer of knowledge
to new specialists and succession processes, taking
into account the objective of supporting education and
science and promoting innovation-oriented employee
development.
In implementing the annual employee evaluation for
2024, during the reporting year 99% of the Parent
Company’s employees received career development
feedback (96% in 2023). Each year the evaluation
process becomes more significant and is strengthened
by introducing regular discussions between colleagues
and managers. The evaluation for 2025 will begin at the
beginning of 2026.
Based on the results of the previous year’s development
discussions and achievement of objectives, the Parent
Company developed a personnel training plan for
2025, which included training focused on improving
individual competences. This training mainly focused
on the development of digital skills, improvement of
general skills and competences, and the provision
of professional training for employees. The Parent
Company carefully monitors and ensures that the
skills of technical personnel comply with the required
qualification requirements, as well as the integration of
new working methods and the acquisition of innovative
technologies in modern, competitive and high-quality
adult professional training. Professional education
improvement programmes were tailored taking into
account the Parent Company’s strategic objectives and
the individual objectives and tasks of structural units and
employees. The training and qualification improvement

system is oriented towards the application of safe
working methods in everyday work while maintaining
the highest standards of professionalism. Competence
development was carried out with the involvement of
internal and external trainers.
Taking the specifics of their work and the company’s
field of activity into account, AST personnel are subject
to defined criteria regarding employee education,
qualifications and competences. Electrical engineers
and AST electrical system engineers equivalent to them
are included in the list of regulated professions in the
energy sector, which establishes special requirements
regarding the education of the professional practitioner,
documents confirming professional qualifications or
the use of the professional title. The education and
qualifications of personnel working in these professions
must comply with the education and qualification
requirements stipulated in the Law "On Regulated
Professions and the Recognition of Professional
Qualifications". In total, AST employs 77 representatives
of regulated professions.
In the Parent Company, one of the priorities is digital
transformation and the improvement of employees’
digital skills, as well as the development of management
competencies. In 2025, AST continued training aimed
at improving digital skills, expanding the range of
topics and providing employees with the opportunity to
learn new tools and solutions for more efficient daily
work. At the same time, the AST Viszinis platform was
augmented with training materials that can be learned
through self-study, providing each colleague with the
opportunity to independently improve their knowledge at
a convenient time. Conexus, in turn, has established the

Conexus Academy platform, which is used for similar
purposes.
At the same time, systematic training for managers
continued, strengthening their competences in setting
objectives, providing feedback, team management and
personnel development.
Such training forms a unified approach to management
culture within the company, promoting both employee
development and the overall development of the
organisation.
In order to expand professional development
opportunities for all employees, AST has purchased
subscriptions to online learning platforms that provide
access to lectures, courses and video content in
various fields. These subscriptions allow employees to
choose topics of interest and learn at a time convenient
for them, thereby promoting a broader outlook and
individual development.
The company is goal-oriented in taking care of the
professional development and long-term employment
of its employees by actively following the cofinancing opportunities for education and professional
development offered by state institutions. AST not only
informs employees about these opportunities but also
motivates and supports them in obtaining higher and
professional education using the state co-financing
available. In order to expand employees’ opportunities to
learn and develop, the Parent Company has concluded
an agreement on receiving co-financing to support
employee education. This will ensure broader and more
accessible learning opportunities, providing employees
108


=== pdf p.109 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

with the possibility to acquire new knowledge, improve
professional skills and develop labour market-relevant
competences with partial coverage of costs from
external financial sources.
Based on the results of employee evaluations and
observations, training in defining objectives and
providing feedback was implemented. This training will
help managers and employees develop a common
understanding of the principles of setting objectives,
effective communication and the importance of regular
feedback in daily work. As a result of this training, the
next evaluation phase will ensure a more qualitative
and meaningful dialogue between managers and
employees, clearer and measurable objectives, as well
as greater engagement and responsibility for achieving
individual and team results.
In 2025, cooperation with a certified supervisor was
ensured – supervision consultations help employees
and managers to improve cooperation, communication
and work quality. This provides emotional support,
promotes professional development and helps to
prevent burnout. For managers, supervision enables
more effective team management and the maintenance
of a favourable working environment. Cooperation with
a supervisor will also be ensured in 2026.
Reflecting the company’s commitment to promoting
awareness of a healthy lifestyle, lectures on maintaining
balance between work and private life have been
organised. Such initiatives help to reduce stress,
improve well-being and promote productivity in
everyday work.

According to the Parent Company’s estimates, the
average number of training hours per employee in the
Parent Company in 2025 was 50.5 hours, while in the
Subsidiary it was 30.8 hours.

HEALTH, SAFETY AND WELL-BEING
OF EMPLOYEES
In the Group, special attention is paid to creating a safe
working environment. Accordingly, the Group provides
employees with workplaces suited to their needs,
personal protective equipment and technical resources,
and also provides employee training on occupational
safety issues and safe working methods. The Group’s
activities in the field of safety have been highly
appreciated by third parties.
This section outlines ongoing activities that the Group
companies have implemented to promote occupational
health and safety.
An occupational health and safety management system
has been implemented within the Group that complies
with ISO 45001 requirements and enables the company
to systematically reduce occupational health and safety
risks.
The occupational health and safety system applies to
all areas of the Group’s activities and all employees.
The system ensures internal monitoring of the working
environment, employee awareness of hazards and
risks in daily work, and prompt response in emergency
situations. An employee or witnesses to an accident

immediately report the incident to their direct manager,
and the injured person is provided with immediate
first aid and medical assistance. It is the manager’s
responsibility to inform the company’s management
and the occupational safety specialist. Once a quarter,
the management board is informed about accidents,
incidents and near-miss events. After an accident,
workplace risks are reassessed taking into account the
causes of the accident.
Workplace accidents and occupational diseases
can only be completely prevented if a safe working
environment is ensured; therefore every employee
must comply with all occupational safety requirements.
In 2025, there were two work‑related accidents
without serious consequences within the Group and
no fatalities. In the Parent Company, accidents are
recorded and investigated in accordance with the
regulatory enactments of the Republic of Latvia and
the company’s internal procedure "Procedure for the
Investigation, Recording and Reporting of Accidents and
Workplace Incidents".
The frequency of recordable work-related injuries in the
Parent Company in 2025 was 1.85 (per 1 million working
hours), compared to 1.08 (per 1 million working hours) in
2024; at the Group level, the corresponding figures were
1.17 and 2.68, respectively. To calculate the frequency
of accidents per 1 million working hours, the Parent
Company and the Group uses the following calculation
methodology:
1. Number of accidents: how many accidents occurred
during a given period of time (e.g. a year).

2. Number of hours worked: how many hours were
worked in total during that period.
3. The number of accidents is divided by the total
number of hours worked, yielding the accident rate
per hour worked.
4. The result is multiplied by 1,000,000. This allows us to
calculate the accident rate per million hours worked.
The Group regularly assesses work environment
risks in accordance with the methodology, also taking
into account the accidents that have occurred, and
measures are taken to reduce work environment risks,
continuously improving employee safety at work as well
as the work environment.
In the Group, work environment assessment is carried
out by labour protection specialists, involving heads
of structural units and responsible employees, trusted
persons, as well as those employees who work in
specific premises/workplaces or perform relevant work.
If necessary, competent institutions are outsourced.
In the Subsidiary, a competent institution is involved
in the assessment of work environment risks and
explosion risks.
In the Group, work environment assessments are
carried out by occupational safety specialists, involving
heads of structural units and responsible employees,
trusted representatives, as well as those employees who
work in the specific premises/workplaces or perform the
relevant work. Where necessary, competent institutions
are engaged through outsourcing. In the Subsidiary, a
competent institution has been engaged to assess work
109


=== pdf p.110 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

environment risks and explosion risks.
In the context of remote work, the assessment of work
environment risks is carried out in cooperation with
remote employees. In the process of work environment
risk assessment, all workplaces and all types of work
are identified.
In both Group companies, the results of the work
environment risk assessment are compiled once a year
and an occupational health and safety measures plan is
prepared to eliminate or reduce work environment risks.
In accordance with the scope provided for in the work
environment risk assessment, employees are regularly
trained, briefings are held, and regular knowledge tests
are conducted for employees who perform work in
electrical installations or technical maintenance work
on the gas storage and transmission system, as well as
work with hazardous equipment. The need for training,
the scope of briefings and the need for knowledge
testing are determined by assessing the work
environment risks of the specific employee. Training is
mostly provided in cooperation with training institutions
that comply with the requirements of regulatory
enactments, is free of charge, and mainly takes place
during working hours.

required collective agreement costs (inter alia, various

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT
ESRS S1-10, S1-11

ADEQUATE WAGES AND
SOCIAL PROTECTION
Every year, the Group participates in a general
remuneration study provided by Figure Baltic Advisory
and compares its remuneration data with market data.
All positions in the Group are assessed and divided
into job groups. When reviewing remuneration, both
the general remuneration study data compared to the
current remuneration amount, as well as the position
assessment groups and individual performance, in light
of the results of the annual assessment, are taken into
account. The amount of remuneration is determined
according to the duties of the position to be performed,
and not by the employee’s gender.
In addition, it should be noted that all Group employees
are protected from loss of income in the event of
parental leave, illness, unemployment, work-related
injuries, disability or retirement in accordance with the
labour law regulations in force in the Republic of Latvia.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT
S1-16
REMUNERATION INDICATORS
The wage gap and total remuneration gap Indicators
are calculated in accordance with the formulas specified
by ESRS. The calculation of the wage gap between
men and women uses gross hourly remuneration. The
total remuneration gap is determined by comparing the
total annual remuneration of the highest paid employee
in the Parent Company or the Group (including
bonuses, allowances, etc.) with the average median of
total remuneration. The total remuneration gap does
not include the remuneration of the members of the
Management Board, Supervisory Board and Audit
Committee of the Group’s companies, because these
officials are not employees of the company within
the meaning of the Labour Law, but act on the basis
of authorisation, and the remuneration Indicators of
the said officials are already publicly available in the
declarations of officials and in the documents published
by the companies. Comparable data for 2024 are not
provided, as the indicator in question was not calculated
within the Group in 2024.

including base salary, bonuses, supplements for
overtime and night work, as well as other statutory
supplements in accordance with the Labour Law (such
as paid leave, sickness benefits, etc.) and paid days off
in accordance with the Collective Agreement.
The calculation does not include employer contributions
to pension funds, health insurance, or additional leave
allowances provided under the Collective Agreement.
The Group considers the impact of excluding these cost
components from the calculation to be immaterial, as
such costs are proportionate to employee remuneration
and/or (in the case of health insurance) uniform for all
employees. Consequently, these exclusions do not
materially affect median remuneration indicators or the
results of the pay gap (ratio) calculations.
In the calculation of medians at Conexus, the
remuneration of 22 employees who were not employed
for the full year 2025 was excluded. Management
considers the impact of excluding these employees on
the pay gap indicators to be immaterial.

AST has an equal approach to employees with
equivalent duties, competencies and levels of
responsibility, observing a non-discriminatory approach.
The monthly basic salary for employees is determined
in accordance with the assessment of the specific
position, labour market assessment, the amount of
work to be performed and the employee’s competence.
The variable part of the salary is directly related to the
employee’s individual work results, competencies and
achieved individual and/or company goals.
Different job groups have different salaries, which result
from the aforementioned principles that determine
employee remuneration. Job groups are not evenly
represented between genders. Based on the ESRS
methodology, the salary gap is calculated only by
comparing genders, without taking into account
differences between job functions and levels.

The methodology for calculating the pay gap indicator
will be further aligned with ESRS requirements
in 2026, by incorporating the currently omitted
remuneration‑related elements.

The calculation of the gender pay gap and total
remuneration gap indicators includes remuneration paid
to employees engaged under employment contracts,
