SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
ESRS S3
As the operator of the electricity and natural gas
transmission system, the Augstsprieguma tīkls Group
has infrastructure that covers the entire territory
of Latvia. In the implementation of the Group’s
strategic development projects and in the process of
infrastructure maintenance, interaction occurs with
residents whose properties are crossed by, or located
near, the Group’s infrastructure. The Group takes public
interests into account and, where possible, plans works
in a way that minimises its negative impact, for example
by aligning works with the agricultural work calendar,
seasonality and weather conditions so that the use of
machinery is considerate of local roads, and by carrying
out planned works in compliance with environmental
noise limits.

communities, including the observance of human rights.
The Group’s strategy and operating model are designed
to take the needs of society into account, and to find a
balance between economic development, environmental
protection and social responsibility.

impact on the living conditions of local residents. During
the reporting period, no cases of human rights violations
related to affected communities were identified, neither
in connection with the Group’s own activities nor within
its value chain.

As transmission system operators, AST and Conexus
are responsible for the security and continuity of
energy supply throughout the country; however, their
infrastructure projects often affect local communities,
their quality of life and the environment. Therefore, the
Group’s strategy places great importance on open and
inclusive communication with all stakeholders. To this
end, the Group engages in dialogue with residents,
businesses and non-governmental organisations to
ensure that their views are heard and taken into account
in the decision-making process. Additional information is
available in the section on "Stakeholder engagement".

Augstsprieguma tīkls adheres to the principles of fair
compensation when projects affect private property
and seeks to offer solutions that reduce potential
inconvenience and long-term consequences for
the local community. The company also carefully
evaluates environmental impacts in order to ensure
that its activities promote sustainable development
and minimise negative impacts on biodiversity and the
landscape.

When assessing the types of communities affected, it
should be taken into account that these communities
may be located directly next to transmission lines
or gas pipelines, but the impact may also extend to
communities that are economically connected to
these projects. This includes communities that provide
materials, services or other resources, such as guest
houses and catering services, as well as communities
whose business activities may be disrupted, such as
farmers or tourism operators.

Public participation is an important element in the
Group’s activities. Before the construction of new
transmission lines (e.g. the construction of the new
Ventspils–Brocēni–Varduva line) or the modernisation
of existing infrastructure, public consultations and
discussions are organised. In this process, the
objectives of the project, possible benefits and impacts
are explained, along with solutions that best correspond
to the interests of the community are sought. In recent
years, public interest in participating in consultations has
increased. The Augstsprieguma tīkls Group relies on
transparency and trust, ensuring that all actions taken
are justified and comply with legal regulations.

The Group’s operations are significantly influenced
by the interests, opinions and rights of affected

Particular attention is paid to the observance of human
rights, especially with regard to property rights and the

As AST considers that an informed society is better able
to adapt to changes in the energy sector and actively
participate in overall energy development, the company
regularly informs the public about transmission system
development plans and electricity market trends.
Overall, the interests and rights of affected communities
are an integral part of the activities of Augstsprieguma
tīkls. The company is committed to operating
responsibly, observing the highest standards of ethics
and social responsibility in order to ensure long-term
benefits for both society and for the energy sector
as a whole.

LINKING TO THE STRATEGY AND
BUSINESS MODEL
Both the strategy and business model reflect the need
to minimise negative impacts on communities and
to address potential issues in a timely manner. For
example, based on studies conducted on the need
to strengthen the transmission network in Kurzeme,
in 2025 AST organised public consultations in the
Ventspils, Kuldīga and Saldus municipalities regarding
the planned project "Construction of the 330 kV power
transmission line from Ventspils–Brocēni–Varduva" (see
additional information in the section on "Implemented
and planned activities").
Information about the public consultations is also
published by the municipalities involved in order to
reach a wide range of interested parties. The owners
of real estate properties whose properties border the
territory of the planned activity are informed individually.
Subsequently, an environmental impact assessment
is carried out, and repeated public consultations are
organised in order to present the environmental impact
assessment report.
This approach is important to promote public support
and ensure the successful implementation of projects.
In addition, the necessary funding is planned for
compensation and support measures for affected
communities.
In accordance with Section 24 of the Energy Law
and points 1 and 6 of Cabinet Regulation No. 603 of
25 July 2006 "Procedure for Calculating and Paying
Compensation for the Restriction of the Right to
119


=== pdf p.120 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

Use Land Property Necessary for the Installation
or Reconstruction of an Energy Supply Facility", the
procedure is stipulated as to how an energy supply
merchant compensates a real estate owner for losses
directly related to the installation of new facilities or to
ensuring the operation and repair of existing facilities.
When assessing the impact on local communities, the
company recognises the need to adapt its operating
model in order to reduce negative impacts. These
changes may include modifications to reconstruction
plans, the use of new technologies that reduce
environmental impact factors (e.g. noise and vibrations),
and additional support mechanisms for society. For
example, noise barriers have been installed in the Tume
and Rēzekne battery projects to improve the wellbeing of nearby residents, while in areas with historical
heritage (e.g. in Kuldīga and within the boundaries of
the historic centre of Riga, among others) solutions are
sought to successfully integrate infrastructure structures
into the overall landscape.

SIGNIFICANT IMPACT ON COMMUNITIES
The activities of the Group’s companies have
the potential to create adverse impacts on local
communities. This impact may be related both to actual
and potential negative effects on the environment, the
economy and society, particularly in relation to the
construction of infrastructure, its maintenance and
possible losses that may arise in connection with land
use and the living conditions of residents.

Both companies implement projects that require the
construction or reconstruction of new transmission
lines, gas pipelines, substations or overhead lines,
which may affect landowners. In some cases, this may
create a need to use agricultural, forestry or residential
land plots, which may conflict with the interests of
the respective landowners. At the same time, it is
conceivable that residents who live or work near
electricity or gas lines may also face issues related to
noise, visual aesthetics or environmental pollution.
In some cases, this impact may be widespread, for
example, if a project requires large areas of land or if the
affected areas include several communities. However,
the specific impacts may depend on the local situation
and the public response to the particular project.
Negative impacts may also be associated with individual
events in the energy system, such as temporary
disruptions to the operation of local infrastructure or
during the construction of transmission lines.
AS Augstsprieguma tīkls services all 330 kV and 110
kV electricity transmission lines located in the territory
of Latvia in order to ensure their efficient operation,
maintenance and repair. In accordance with the
Protection Zones Law, special protection zones have
been established, the main purpose of which is to
ensure the safe and efficient operation of electricity
infrastructure. As a result, landowners must be prepared
for regular inspections of power lines and planned repair
works, about which they are informed in advance, as
well as emergency repair works of which owners may
not be informed in advance. After the works have been
completed, AS Augstsprieguma tīkls is responsible
for ensuring that the land areas are restored and can

again be used for their original purposes. In addition,
appropriate compensation must be provided to
landowners or users if damage has been caused during
the course of the works, and such compensation is
provided in accordance with the procedures established
by law or by mutual agreement.
AST is also responsible for felling hazardous trees if
they threaten the safety of power transmission lines,
as well as for clearing vegetation from line corridors
that may interfere with their operation. Farmers are
prohibited from growing trees and shrubs within
the protection zones of power transmission lines,
particularly on forest land, while outside forest land it is
possible to grow plants only with prior written agreement
with AST, the owner of the transmission network. Within
the protection zones of overhead power lines that cross
forest areas, regular forest management is carried
out — including clearing and tree trimming — in order
to prevent potential threats to the power lines. These
measures are particularly important to ensure the safety
of the network, prevent accidents and maintain the
operational quality of transmission lines.
All necessary information on these matters is provided
on the AST website, where landowners and other
interested parties can familiarise themselves with all
rules and procedures applicable to their properties
and rights.
At both national and European Union level, maximum
permissible limits for electromagnetic fields (EMF) have
been established in order to ensure the protection of
public health. In Latvia, the electricity network operates
at a frequency of 50 Hz, and the permissible EMF limits

for electric field intensity (maximum
5000 V/m) and magnetic flux density (maximum
100 µT) at this frequency are determined by the
European Union recommendation for the protection
of the public from exposure to electromagnetic fields
— Council Recommendation of 12 July 1999 on
the limitation of exposure of the general public to
electromagnetic fields (0 Hz to 300 GHz) (1999/519/EC).
These requirements are also incorporated into Latvian
regulatory enactments, i.e. Cabinet Regulation No. 637
(16 October 2018).
The intensity of the magnetic field in the immediate
vicinity of high-voltage power transmission lines is
usually within the range of 25 to 30 µT, while at the
boundary of the protection zone it practically reaches
zero. These values are 3–4 times lower than the
maximum limit permitted in Latvia and internationally —
100 µT — thus ensuring the safety of residents.
When designing and reconstructing power transmission
lines and substations, AST complies with all relevant
regulatory requirements related to environmental
protection and health safety, strictly observing the
established EMF limit values.
AST Technical Expertise Service (hereinafter also
referred to as AST TED) is accredited to carry out
electromagnetic field measurements (LATAK registration
No. LATAK–I–166). However, AST TED does not assess
the impact of EMF on human health and does not
provide recommendations regarding the construction
of houses near power transmission lines.

120


=== pdf p.121 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

AST carries out noise measurements near substations
and, based on received complaints, assesses the
noise level in accordance with the applicable Cabinet
regulations.
For safety purposes, protection zones where Conexus
infrastructure is located are subject to restrictions on
use by other parties, as set out in Sections 56 and 58.2
of the Protection Zones Law. These restrictions include
prohibitions on carrying out construction works, growing
trees and shrubs, placing highly flammable objects,
conducting earthworks, land improvement or geodetic
works, organising public events, and other restrictive
activities.
The positive impact created by projects implemented by
transmission system operators improves the quality and
availability of electricity supply in regions that previously
depended on unstable energy supply systems.
Moreover, for residents who live or work near electricity
or gas transmission networks, such systems provide
long-term stability and development. New projects can
also create jobs and promote regional development
opportunities, thus conferring positive impacts to
communities. The Sustainability Report describes in
detail the positive impact of synchronisation and other
projects on the Latvian public.

RISKS AND
OPPORTUNITIES

• The European Community 10-year development
plan, prepared by ENTSO-E and including regional
projects, ensuring a coordinated approach to the
development of electricity transmission systems
across Europe,

system operators. The implementation of the plans is
the responsibility of the highest management level –
the AST Management Board – which ensures that all
aspects are integrated into the organisation’s strategic
priorities and operational processes.

Residents’ objections to projects represent one of the
main risks, particularly in relation to compensation
for damages and possible objections. This risk is
associated with legal and financial issues, as well as
he company’s reputation. The cost risk is related to
the need to compensate for losses of property rights
or temporary impacts on the living conditions
of communities. Therefore, it is important to continuously
monitor public reaction and adjust activities in order to
minimise this risk to as great an extent as possible.
On the other hand, successful community engagement
and the effective implementation of public dialogue can
reveal opportunities for building long-term relationships
with local communities, which may also be beneficial
for future projects. Reducing environmental impacts
and promoting public well-being can improve the
company’s image.

• The Latvian National 10-year development plan,
which, in addition to the regional projects included
in the ENTSO-E plan, allows for the planning and
implementation of projects of national importance,
effectively developing the Latvian electricity
transmission infrastructure.

The Group recognises its responsibility for respecting
human rights, and its policy with regard to affected
communities is aligned with the UN Guiding Principles
on Business and Human Rights and the ILO Declaration
on Fundamental Principles and Rights at Work.

The plans cover all areas of AST’s operations, including

are integrated into the organisation’s strategic priorities
and operational processes.
A similar 10-year development plan is also in place
for Conexus, which determines the development of
the natural gas transmission system, future functional
development directions and specific capital investment
projects for their implementation.
The plans cover all areas of AST’s activities, including
the maintenance, development and modernisation of
the electricity transmission network, ensuring safe
and uninterrupted electricity supply to all direct users,
i.e. electricity consumers, producers and distribution

These commitments are included in the AST Code of
Ethics, which is publicly available on the AST website.
With regard to affected communities, AST strives to:
• respect the right of communities to self-determination,
culture and traditions;
• ensure that AST activities do not restrict communities’
access to resources and services essential to their
survival and well-being;
• promote open dialogue and engagement with
affected communities in order to understand their
needs and concerns, and involve them in decisionmaking processes.
In addition, AST carries out impact assessments
regularly and proactively, including impacts on
communities, in order to identify potential risks and
implement preventive measures in a timely manner.
In 2025, AST approved amendments to the Standard
Instruction for the operation of 110–330 kV overhead

defines the scope and procedures according to which
protection zones are cleared of trees and shrubs.
Conexus sends informational warning letters by post or
electronically regarding protection zone clearing works
from trees and shrubs or the performance of repair
works. The requirement to send such warnings derives
from Section 35, Paragraph 2 of the Protection Zones
Law, which stipulates that at facilities where a protection
zone has been established, the owner or possessor
of the facility is permitted to carry out works within the
protection zone that are necessary for the operation,
repair, restoration or reconstruction of the respective
facility. The landowner or lawful possessor must be
notified in writing at least two weeks before the start of
the works, except in the case of emergency repairs or
the elimination of their consequences, which may be
carried out at any time without prior notice.

In order to improve cooperation with affected
communities, in 2025, the Group is reviewing the
Corporate Social Responsibility policy, which includes
identifying the views of local communities and,
once approved, will serve as the basis for improving
cooperation and awareness in the coming years.
The Subsidiary is engaged in the maintenance of
existing infrastructure, which requires communication
with landowners whose territories contain protection
zones. Communication with landowners takes place
in cases where protection zone clearing from trees
and shrubs is carried out or when works on Conexus
infrastructure are performed. Cabinet Regulation No.

S3-3

PROCESSES FOR
REMEDIALISATION OF
NEGATIVE IMPACTS AND
CHANNELS FOR AFFECTED
COMMUNITIES TO
EXPRESS CONCERNS
Several communication channels have been
established within the Group’s companies through
which representatives of affected communities can
express their concerns or ask questions, as well as
report potential violations, including in the field of human
rights. Complaints can also be submitted through the
whistleblowing systems of the Group’s companies (see
the sections on "Channels for raising concerns" and
"Whistleblower protection").
Whistleblower reports are examined in accordance with
the established Whistleblowing Procedure, whereas in
the case of other complaints, the information is received
by the Document Management Department, which
forwards the specific issue to the structural unit whose
activities are most closely related to it. Complaints
are processed in accordance with the requirements
of external regulatory enactments and, if necessary,
corrective measures are taken to eliminate violations
and compensate affected persons.

AST’s communication approach provides for informing
affected communities about the available communication
channels and complaint-handling processes. This
approach includes informational materials, public
meetings and regular communication with community
representatives.
Signs displaying the contact telephone number of the
Group’s companies are placed in the immediate vicinity
of the Group’s infrastructure facilities. Informational
materials sent to residents contain contact information
for obtaining additional information and for reporting
emergency situations.
In the event that complaints are received from real
estate owners regarding potential damage to their
property caused by the Group’s companies during
the performance of works (repair, restoration or
reconstruction of infrastructure facilities), the respective
real estate is inspected after the completion of works,
and the Group’s companies agree with the property
owner on the elimination of the damage caused.
AST has established an internal monitoring system
to track and supervise issues that have been raised
and need to be addressed in relation to affected
communities. This system includes regular meetings
with responsible employees, as well as data analysis
and record-keeping regarding the progress and the
results of complaint handling. Information on each
recorded case is compiled in a table, creating a unified
register and archive of complaints. In 2025, 22 claims
were received; after reviewing and assessing the
situation, eight claims were found to be unfounded,
ten were recognised as justified due to the fault of
contractors, and four submissions or calls were found

to be requests for information that were not related to
claims or complaints. An assessment of the submissions
received shows that during the last three years, no
justified claims have been received that were related to
the actions of AST personnel.
Regular reviews and meetings with stakeholders are
developed in order to monitor progress in resolving
complaints and ensure continuous improvement.
In order to assess whether communities are aware
of and trust these structures and processes, AST
regularly conducts surveys and interviews, as well as
analyses the number and nature of complaints received.
Where it is found that communities are not sufficiently
informed or do not trust the available mechanisms, AST
takes additional measures to improve communication
and foster trust, but this improvement process is not
formalised. However, AST’s main goal is to ensure the
best possible awareness of communities. Accordingly,
when necessary, not only digital channels and media are
used, but also letters are sent, individual calls are made
or face-to-face meetings are organised. A structured
approach to ascertaining the views of local communities
is envisaged in the Corporate Social Responsibility
Project, which is planned to be submitted for approval
in 2026.
If the Group’s companies determine that their activities
have caused or contributed to a significant negative
impact on the affected communities, they shall ensure
or facilitate appropriate legal protection. This includes
the payment of compensation, rehabilitation measures
or other support that is proportionate to the specific
situation.

Conexus pays for damage caused to agricultural
crops during repair work, in the event that the repair
work cannot be postponed to the period when the
crop has been harvested. The consideration of the
issue of compensation for losses is carried out on the
basis of the application received. In the application,
the landowner is required to indicate the name of the
damaged crop (wheat, barley, rapeseed or other), the
volume (area) and attach an estimate of the loss. The
estimate shall be checked and compared with the
current grain market prices. In case of doubt, Conexus
cooperates with the Latvian Rural Consulting and
Education Centre. In order to ensure fair assessment
of the damage caused and compensation in relation to
damaged crops, Conexus has concluded a cooperation
agreement with the Latvian Rural Consulting and
Education Centre (LLKIC). The amount of damage
caused by damaged crops is calculated in accordance
with the calculation made by LLKIC. To determine the
amount of other types of damage caused, Conexus and
the owner of the real estate reach a mutually agreement,
taking into account the nature, amount of damage,
damage caused and the market value of the relevant
thing, if such can be determined.
Conexus compensates losses if the land property is
encumbered with new engineering communications,
which have protective zones, thereby imposing new
encumbrances on the land owner. Compensation is
carried out in accordance with the procedure specified
in Section 23 of the Energy Law.
AST has incorporated procedures in relation to possible
damage, including representatives of surrounding
communities, into contracts with contractors who
123

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

perform work in power line separation zones or
other work on behalf of AST. At the end of the work,
contractors are obliged to submit a certificate confirming
the absence of third-party claims. In turn, if community
representatives address complaints to AST and they are
related to the activities of contractors, AST directs them
to the relevant contractor, who is obliged to resolve the
situation and, if necessary, compensate the victim
for losses.
In the case of new projects, if they are related to the
repurchase of real estate or the determination of
encumbrances, the external legal framework is observed
and the applicable amount of compensation is decided
by a decision of the company's Management Board.
AST actively engages in dialogue with various
stakeholders, including state institutions, municipalities
and non-governmental organisations, in order to
promote and support the availability of effective
communication channels and adapt them to the
audience to be reached. The Parent Company has
established cooperation with municipalities and, where
necessary, engages consultants (e.g. to organize public
consultations), which helps to ensure that affected
communities have access to reliable and effective ways
to express their concerns and needs. Regular reports
are prepared and meetings with stakeholders are
organised in order to monitor the progress of complaint
resolution and ensure continuous improvements.

S3-4

ACTION IN RELATION TO
SIGNIFICANT IMPACTS ON
AFFECTED COMMUNITIES
In order to prevent, mitigate or correct significant
negative impacts on affected communities, the Group
implements measures such as environmental impact
assessments, public consultations and cooperation with
local communities. For example, during construction
projects, AST makes every effort to minimise noise and
dust pollution, as well as providing compensation for
reasonable losses.
In order to make a positive impact on affected
communities, the Group implements various additional
initiatives, in particular focusing on public education
activities – for example, organising educational classes
in schools and field trips to universities (which also
serve the purpose of recruiting new employees), as well
as for several years discussing energy security topics
at the LAMPA Conversation Festival.
The Group regularly conducts internal audits and
external assessments, including in community-related
areas, in order to track and evaluate the effectiveness
of its activities and initiatives. The company uses various
indicators to measure its progress and ensure that its
operations meet its sustainability goals. For example,
the dynamics of the number of complaints, the results
of surveys, the amount of compensation paid, the

number of participants in public consultations and other
activities, and the level of satisfaction with cooperation,
etc.
AST’s approach is based on proactive engagement,
regular communication and transparent accountability.
The company strives to identify potential risks in
advance and take preventive measures to prevent
or mitigate negative impacts. Existing procedures
are regularly reviewed and improved to ensure their
effectiveness.
AST takes preventive actions to identify possible
dependencies on the external manifestations of affected
communities by conducting surveys, monitoring
media and social networks, and, if necessary,
engages in resolving the situation so that the identified
dependencies on the external manifestations of affected
communities do not become risks.
Conexus, in implementing the requirements of
the Cabinet Regulation No. 982 "Methodology for
Determining Protective Zones of Energy Infrastructure
Facilities" of 5 December, provide information on actions
to be taken in the event of a possible transmission gas
pipeline accident and protective measures to be taken.
Since the impact on communities may also be related
to safety aspects, the Subsidiary maintains a Safety
Report. The new version includes information on
modernisation projects carried out and initiated in recent
years, as well as a reassessment of the risk of industrial
accidents at the Inčukalns UGS facility using a new
version of the quantitative risk assessment programme,
which, among other things, serves to protect the
surrounding community from unwanted impacts.

Conexus, in accordance with the Cabinet Regulation
No. 131 "Procedure for Risk Assessment of Industrial
Accidents and Risk Reduction Measures" of 1 March
2016, regularly informs residents living in the vicinity
of the Inčukalns UGS Facility and companies located
nearby about the possible threat and necessary actions
to be taken in the event of accidents at the Inčukalns
UGS Facility, as well as about planned protective
measures.
In accordance with Cabinet Regulation No. 563,
emergency drills are conducted every three years at the
Inčukalns UGS with the aim of testing the effectiveness
of response and consequence elimination measures in
emergency situations in cooperation with the State Fire
and Rescue Service (SFS), as well as other institutions
in disaster management cases. Residents are informed
about the drills, possible inconveniences and the
movement of emergency service vehicles. The drills
provide an opportunity to practically test the cooperation
between the Inčukalns UGS, SFS, the municipality and
residents. Such drills were most recently conducted on
6 November 2025.
All costs associated with the above‑mentioned activities
and processes related to affected communities are
planned and ensured within the annual budget.

ESRS S3-5

TARGET INDICATORS
The Group does not formally set quantitative targets
for affected communities. Activities are carried out
in accordance with legal requirements and internal
procedures that ensure that the rights and interests of
affected communities are taken into account.

account the opinions of the communities when making
decisions and planning its activities. Accordingly,
affected communities indirectly participate in setting
goals, tracking performance, and identifying lessons and
improvements, because their opinions are heard and
taken into account in the company’s decision-making
process.

Qualitative targets for affected communities are included
in the Group’s sustainability initiatives and internal
procedures. These include:
• Mitigating adverse impacts: The Group strives to
minimise its impact on the environment and society by
adhering to the highest standards and using the best
available technologies;
• Promoting positive impacts: The Group promotes
knowledge and understanding of local communities
about energy supply and energy security by engaging
in public education activities;
• Risk and opportunity management: The Group
identifies and manages risks and opportunities
related to affected communities to ensure sustainable
development.
The Group regularly engages in dialogue with affected
communities to understand their needs and concerns.
This dialogue takes place in various formats, such
as public consultations, meetings with local residents
and cooperation with NGOs. The company takes into

The Group’s management promotes business
operations based on fair and honest principles,
compliance with ethical norms, and takes the necessary
actions to prevent fraud and corruption risks and ensure
the improvement of the control environment. The Group
has established absolute zero tolerance for any kind
of abusive, improper or illegal behaviour of employees
and officials for personal gain and has established zero
tolerance for the risk of corruption and fraud.
The Group sets corporate values, as well as high
standards of professional conduct and ethics, ensuring
that they are directed against corruption, preventing
conflicts of interest in everyday operations, unlawful use
of inside information and any other illegal and unethical
behaviour.
AST’s corporate values ​​are defined in the Corporate
Social Responsibility Policy:
1. With integrity - Independent, ethical and open
action towards everyone and everyone;
2. Smartly – effectively. With an eye on the future.
Long-term thinking;

corruption, preventing conflicts of interest in everyday
operations, unlawful use of inside information and
any other illegal and unethical behaviour. The
Company promotes corporate social activities that are
consistent with the Company’s strategic goals (safety,
professionalism, sustainability, openness) and involve
the Company’s employees and the public in their
implementation.
Both the Parent Company and the Subsidiary have
developed and apply Codes of Ethics. The purpose of
each Code is to create a single set of ethical norms of
behaviour in the respective company and to strengthen
the company’s internal culture, business practices,
and reputation by defining basic ethical principles
and internal organisational measures based on the
company’s core values, as well as promoting a safe and
inclusive work environment.
The Code is binding on all employees and elected
officials, and it, among other things, provides for:
• prohibition of engaging in corrupt or fraudulent
activities;
• principles for managing conflicts of interest;

3. Responsibly - Thoughtful action. With a high level
of responsibility towards work, people and nature;

• ethical requirements in cooperation with employees,
customers, and partners;

4. Together - Joining forces to achieve more.
A strong team that encourages and challenges.

• rules for accepting and giving gifts.

In accordance with the AST Code, employees are
introduced to the aforementioned document by the

its amendments coming into force, which is ensured by
the Personnel Department or the head of the relevant

Upon commencement of employment at Conexus, the
new employee is introduced to the Code of Ethics and
undergoes an ethics training course at the Conexus
Academy, which is mandatory for all employees of the
company.
In accordance with the Conexus Code, employees
are encouraged to receive consultations on ethical
issues, as well as to be socially responsible and to
react if non-compliance with the Code or other violation
is ascertained, informing their immediate manager,
a member of the Management Board or submitting a
whistleblower report in accordance with the procedures
specified in the internal whistleblowing regulations.
Detailed information on the implemented whistleblowing
mechanism and communication channels is available
on the Conexus website. Employees are informed about
this possibility during mandatory ethics training, and
the whistleblowing rules are available to all employees
electronically, in addition to which information on
submitting reports in paper form was posted on
information boards when the system was implemented.
The Group’s basic ethical principles are observed in
all procurement procedures organised by the Group,
including when signing the Supplier Declaration, as well
as when concluding contracts with cooperation partners,
as well as throughout the entire period of cooperation.
In 2025, AST developed a draft anti-corruption and
bribery policy, which is aligned with the UN Convention

against Corruption, and in 2026, it is planned to continue
work on the development and improvement of the policy.
AST employees are informed of the requirements of
the AST fraud, corruption and conflict of interest risk
management framework. Suppliers and other interested
parties can access key information on AST’s policies
related to responsible business conduct through the
AST website. Here detailed information is available on
AST’s actions aimed at ensuring responsible business
conduct, which, in addition to the Code of Ethics and
information on whistleblowing, includes information
on fraud, corruption and conflict of interest risk
management, procurement policy, supplier declaration,
privacy policy, real estate leasing/leasing and valuation
of property investment. AST suppliers, through the

comply with the principles of fair competition, laws
and contractual provisions, confidentiality, preventing
conflicts of interest, fraud and corruption, preventing
discrimination, ensuring sustainable development,
observing social responsibility and keeping transaction
and financial records.
The policies and other internal regulations of AST
and Conexus specify those persons responsible for
implementing and monitoring policies, as well as a
deadline for updating policies. The Management Board
is generally responsible for implementing policies in
the relevant company, while the Supervisory Board
oversees the implementation of policies.
During the reporting period, stakeholders were not
directly involved in the development and/or updating
of the Group’s policies.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

INTEREST
REPRESENTATION
ESRS G1-5
At national level, the Group cooperates with the Ministry
of Climate and Energy as a policy maker in the climate
and energy sector, participating in the development of
regulatory frameworks that are relevant to the Group.
At European Union level, the Group's interests are
represented through involvement in non-governmental
and professional organisations. The Group's main
topics of interest are included in the agendas of such
organisations:
• ENTSO-E (European Network of Transmission
System Operators for Electricity) – registered in
the Transparency Register of the European Union
(25805148045-87), participates in the development of
current regulatory framework of the European Union
for the industry and the Parent Company;
• ENTSOG (European Network of Transmission
System Operators for Gas) – registered in
the European Union Transparency Register
(565032821273-72), where the Subsidiary participates
in working groups on market development,
investments, system transparency and coordinated
cooperation and long-term secure system operation
throughout the European Union;

• GIE (Gas Infrastructure Europe) – registered in the
European Union Transparency Register (761309920715), ensuring participation of the industry and the
Subsidiary in the development of current European
Union regulatory frameworks on issues such as
security of gas supply, internal gas market, network
codes, hydrogen issues.
In 2025, AST actively participated in the following
ENTSO-E working groups and committees: System
Development Committee, System Management
Committee, Market Committee, Research, Development
and Innovation Committee, Legal and Regulatory Group,
Digital Committee.

related to transmission and storage infrastructure,
interconnections, tariffs, gas quality and system
efficiency assessment.

and the achievement of its goals, no political cash or
in-kind investments are made, therefore the amount of
such investments in 2025 was EUR 0.

1. ENTSOG monitors and improves the gas network
codes in 2025 to ensure a unified and efficient operation
of the European transmission market. Conexus actively
follows the changes in these regulations that affect the
Latvian transmission and storage system.

The Group's participation in industry associations and
organisations ensures access to information about
current events in the energy and related sectors, as
well as representation of interests in the development of
national and international policy documents, laws and
regulations, and standards.

2. Work continues on the preparation of the ten-year
development plan, emphasizing the assessment of new
projects, including hydrogen infrastructure. Conexus
is involved in the development processes of regional
infrastructure and interconnections.

The most important processes in which AST is involved:
• Synchronisation of the Baltic States' electricity
systems with the Continental European
Synchronous Zone;
• 15-minute products in the day-ahead market;
• Work on the review of the draft amendments to the
CACM Regulation;
• Participation in the development of the European
Resource Adequacy Assessment for 2025.
In 2025, ENTSOG not only strengthened its regulatory
role, but also expanded its strategic function - integrating
the implementation of the Network Code, infrastructure
planning, security of supply and energy transition into a
single operation. Conexus actively follows the progress
of issues in a total of 15 working groups directly

3. ENTSOG performs EU-wide security of supply
analyses and coordinates emergency preparedness.
Conexus actively follows the security of gas supply
situation across Europe.
4. Conexus follows developments on the wider
integration of renewable gases (biomethane, hydrogen)
and the development of CO2 infrastructure.
5. ENTSOG promotes innovation, data exchange and
digitalisation in the transmission sector. Conexus uses
these initiatives and knowledge to improve the stable
and efficient operation of the system.
Reflecting the fact that the Group's interest
representation process is carried out in accordance with
the Interest Representation Law, as well as ENTSO-E
and ENTSOG, which were established to promote the
implementation of the European Union's energy policy

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

Membership in the Latvian Association of Electrical
Engineers and Energy Engineers
(LEEA) provides an opportunity to participate in the
evaluation and improvement of regulatory enactments,
policy documents and standards in the field of electric
power and power engineering, in the organisation of
personnel certification and training programmes, in the
conduct of scientific research related to electric power
and in the organisation of scientific and technical events,
as well as to cooperate with educational institutions in
the field of electric power. AST representatives regularly
participate in LEEA meetings to ensure an exchange of
views on current events in the energy sector, including
energy security.

AST is a member of the Association of Issuing Bodies
of European Guarantees of Origin. The circulation and
trade of guarantees of origin for electricity in Europe
is made possible thanks to the Association of Issuing
Bodies (AIB).

AST has been a member of the World Energy Council
Latvian National Committee (WEC LNC) since 2016.
Membership in WEC LNC provides information on
the exploration, extraction, transport, conversion and
efficient use of energy resources both nationally and
internationally.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

In 2021, AST joined the Business Sustainability Council
initiated by Swedbank to exchange knowledge, deepen
understanding of sustainability, and work with other
companies to create a more sustainable business
environment in Latvia.
Image: blog.swedbank.lv/biznesa-ilgtspejas-padome-bip

AST joined the Employers' Confederation of Latvia
in 2025, thus providing an opportunity to influence
important processes not only at the company level,
but also on a national and international scale – to
participate in social dialogue, influence policy planning
and document development processes, as well as to
achieve significant changes in regulatory enactments,
thus promoting the development of the company and the
represented industry, relations with the state, employees
and society as a whole.

AST has been a member of the association "Baltic Institute
of Corporate Governance" (BICG) since 2022.
Being a member of BICG means joining a progressive and
engaged community of active individuals and companies
that highly value good corporate governance practices and
implement them in their daily business activities. At the same
time, it is a contribution to the development of corporate
governance practices throughout the Baltics, an opportunity
for AST to be involved and actively participate in the latest
trends in the field of corporate governance by participating in
events organised by BICG.
AST and Conexus do not have procedures or defined
responsibilities for monitoring political influence and
lobbying activities.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

PREVENTION AND
DETECTION OF
CORRUPTION AND
BRIBERY
ESRS G1-3, G1-4
In the Parent Company, prevention and detection of
corruption and bribery is ensured through the Code of
Ethics and NOP-1-024 "Rules for the Management of
Risks of Fraud, Corruption and Conflicts of Interest".
Certain requirements that determine the application of
risk-mitigating measures such as the "4-eyes principle"
are incorporated into AST processes.
In the Parent Company, risks of fraud, corruption and
conflicts of interest are assessed for all structural units
of the company (100%), and for all positions. Risks are
grouped by position groups if the potential risks are
comparable.
In total, AST has identified 41 unique risks related to
fraud, corruption and conflicts of interest. Each position
is assessed separately with the risks associated with
the specific position. The most significant risks are
associated with procurement, contract monitoring,
electricity purchase on the exchange, control of the
accounting of electricity transmitted and cooperation
with third parties.

The scope of fraud, corruption and conflict of interest
risk monitoring measures includes AST employees’
conflict of interest declarations for the relevant
year, monitoring of AST employees’ data in Lursoft
databases, monitoring of internal and external
environment. All these measures together indicate
whether AST employees comply in practice with AST’s
requirements in the field of fraud, corruption and conflict
of interest risk management.
During the reporting period, no significant financial
investments were made in the field of prevention and
detection of corruption and bribery, as the necessary
mechanisms, including controls, have been established
in previous periods. Currently, these mechanisms are
being maintained and employee awareness is being
strengthened.

GROUP CORRUPTION OR BRIBERY INCIDENTS
Indicators

2024

2023

Total number of confirmed cases of corruption
or bribery

0

0

Number of confirmed incidents where the company’s
own employees were dismissed or disciplined

0

Number of confirmed incidents related to contracts with
business partners that were terminated or not renewed
due to corruption or bribery-related violations

0

0

0

Internal procedures provide for a procedure by which
the Management Board is informed at least once a
year about all fraud risk events and reports received
during the reporting period, as well as about decisions
taken and risk and/or impact mitigation measures. The
Supervisory Board is also immediately informed of fraud
risk events that may cause significant damage to the
Group’s reputation or cause losses. No significant risk
events or complaints were identified during the reporting
period that would require immediate notification to the
Supervisory Board.
Once a week, heads of structural units inform the
Management Board about the progress of achieving
goals and the most significant events, including if
problems are identified in the implementation of
internal regulatory enactments. Regular meetings of
managers with the Management Board are also held,

where significant issues and the progress of work are
discussed. Issues that apply to all employees are also
discussed in online meetings of the Management Board
with employees. In addition, in assessing employee
performance, one of the evaluation criteria is compliance
with internal regulatory documents at work.
AST’s additional corruption and conflict of interest risk
measures in the activities of the Management Board
and Supervisory Board are included in the bylaws
of the Management Board and Supervisory Board,
respectively, including the obligation for a Management
Board or Supervisory Board Member not to participate
in making a decision or to perform other actions related
to the performance of official duties that affect or may
affect the personal or material interests of the relevant
official, his or her family members or relatives, or
business partners.
141


=== pdf p.142 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

In accordance with the Conexus Code, the Supervisory
Board manages those conflict of interest situations
involving members of the Management Board and
Supervisory Board or persons related to them. The
Management Board manages those conflict of interest
situations involving company employees, as well as
monitors the company’s transactions with former board
and Supervisory Board Members, as well as persons
related to them. In the event that a conflict of interest
is discovered in relation to a Management Board or
Supervisory Board Member, this information is provided
to the Supervisory Board or board, respectively. If a
conflict of interest situation is discovered in relation to
an employee, the information is disclosed to the head of
the structural unit and/or the responsible Management
Board Member.
Potential cases of corruption and bribery are
investigated when specific reports of possible violations
are received or when information indicating potential
violations is identified during the checks carried out
by the internal control system. In cases of potential
violations, the investigation function is provided by
employees who are separated from the management
chain involved in the specific case, ensuring the
independence and objectivity of the investigators.
AST procedures stipulate that if suspicions of potential
violations are prima facie justified and can be treated
as an offence, the materials obtained during the
investigation are transferred to the Corruption Prevention
and Combating Bureau (KNAB) as the competent
investigative authority, ensuring full cooperation from
the AST. Penalties for detected violations are adopted in
accordance with applicable laws and regulations.

The Parent Company regularly provides training on
actions in conflict of interest situations for persons
subject to the obligation in order to prevent conflicts
of interest. Training material and a test for knowledge
testing are sent electronically once a year, but face-toface training with the involvement of KNAB specialists
is provided no less than once every three years. The
last such training, involving a KNAB specialist, was
organised on February 1, 2024. The next such training
is due to be organised in 2027.
In 2025, employee training with a knowledge test
on fraud, corruption and conflict of interest issues
was implemented in the AST internal training system
Viszinis, which was mandatory for all employees who
had at least one medium risk identified. The training
was implemented using IT solutions that allowed
employees to familiarize themselves with the training
materials remotely. The training materials are available
to all AST employees. All Conexus employees are
informed about the Conexus anti-corruption regulation
and procedures and have completed ethics training,
which also covered conflict of interest and corruption
prevention issues. All Conexus cooperation partners
are informed about the Conexus anti-corruption
regulation and procedures, because when participating
in procurements organised by Conexus, all suppliers are
obliged to certify compliance with the aforementioned
basic ethical principles, while other suppliers have the
opportunity to familiarize themselves with them on the
Conexus website. The Code of Ethics determines the
mechanism for preventing and controlling conflicts of
interest – the Management Board approves the list of
positions for which a declaration must be submitted in
which the employee confirms the absence of a conflict

of interest, as well as the content of the declaration
of conflict of interest. The list may include heads of
structural units, auditors, senior specialists, project
managers, procurement specialists and other employees
who participate in making or preparing decisions that
significantly affect the activities of Conexus or who are
constantly provided with restricted access information
or information containing commercial secrets for the

performance of their duties.
Both the Code and the regulatory enactments containing
regulations regarding the prevention of corruption and
conflicts of interest comply with the best anti-corruption
principles and regulatory enactments, including the
United Nations Convention against Corruption.

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

CHANNELS FOR RAISING
CONCERNS
ESRS G1-1
According to the provisions of the AST Code, every
employee must take action to prevent unethical
behaviour in the event that a violation of the Code
requirements is detected or should a suspicion of such
a violation arise. Every employee has the right to file
a complaint with the Human Resources Department
regarding violations of the Code. Questions and/or
information related to the Code or its violations can be
submitted via the AST website or e-mail: etika@ast.lv,
by contacting your direct manager, head of department
or Human Resources Department.
A whistleblowing system has been implemented in the
Group to detect and prevent fraud and corruption, as
well as other types of violations or dishonest actions.
The reporting or whistleblowing channels established
in the Group can also be used by customers and
other interested parties to file any type of complaint
regarding the Group’s activities or violated personal
interests. Received reports are directed within the Group
according to responsibility. Complaints regarding fraud,
corruption and conflict of interest cases are handled
in accordance with the procedure set out in the Fraud,
Corruption and Conflict of Interest Risk Management
Regulations.

Detailed information on the implemented whistleblowing
mechanism and communication channels is available on
the AST and Conexus websites.

representative (a representative of the trade union
organisation of the independent unit Augstsprieguma
tīkls of the Latvian Trade Union Enerģija).

Before submitting a whistleblowing report, if you have
any questions or doubts, it is possible to receive
advice (also anonymously) from AST, which is provided
by AST’s contact person for whistleblowing issues
– Head of Legal Affairs, via AST website specified
communication channels. Similar to AST, it is possible
to receive advice (also anonymously) from Conexus,
provided by the Head of the Internal Audit Department,
via Conexus website specified communication
channels. The information received by the contact
person during the consultation is confidential. The
personnel department informs each intern or employee
about the availability of the whistleblowing procedure
simultaneously upon the commencement of the
internship or employment relationship with AST.

After clarifying all the circumstances and evaluating
the employee’s explanation, the Ethics Commission
prepares a proposal for the AST Board to make a
decision. If the employee disagrees with the decision
made by the Management Board, he or she shall write a
submission addressed to the Chair of the Management
Board, indicating detailed objections.

Conexus employees are informed about the possibility
of whistleblowing during mandatory ethics training,
in addition to which the whistleblowing rules are
sent to all employees electronically, and information
about submitting reports in paper form was posted on
information boards when the system was implemented.
In the event of unethical behaviour, an employee may be
held disciplinarily liable in accordance with the Conexus
rules of procedure.
In order to ensure fair and objective consideration
of disputes arising from the Code, the Chair of the
Management Board of AST shall establish an Ethics
Commission consisting of five members by order to
resolve a specific dispute, one of whom is an employee

In the event that a complaint is filed regarding a possible
violation of the Code by a member of the Management
Board, the issue is considered by the AST Supervisory
Board. If the complaint is filed as a whistleblower
report, it shall be considered in accordance with the
AST’s effective whistleblower procedure, as well as in
accordance with the Conexus’ effective whistleblower
regulations.
In a Subsidiary, upon receipt of a whistleblower report or
other information about a corrupt act/attempt, it shall be
investigated by a commission consisting of employees
who have appropriate knowledge of the matter under
consideration and who do not have a conflict of interest,
informing the Management Board of the results, and
in the case of employee violations, a decision may be
made to impose disciplinary sanctions.

WHISTLEBLOWER
PROTECTION
ESRS G1-1
The Group provides whistleblower protection by
guaranteeing that reporting violations will not result
in adverse consequences for the whistleblower, their
relatives or other related persons.
According to the AST Whistleblower Procedure, the
whistleblower may not suffer any adverse consequences
in connection with the report submitted, including to:
• Discipline or other forms of punishment;
• Dismissal from work or position, including without
extending the employment contract or without
appointing to a position for an indefinite period, if
there was reason to believe that this would be done;
• Transfer to another job or position;
• Denial of promotion, professional training or
qualification improvement;
• Changes to job or positional duties, working hours
and place or remuneration;
• Provision of a negative assessment of the duties
of the job or position;

143


=== pdf p.144 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

• Offending honour, dignity and reputation;

• Unilateral withdrawal from the contract for the supply,
purchase or provision of a service or to terminate it;

MANAGEMENT OF
RELATIONSHIPS WITH
SUPPLIERS

• Request a medical report;

ESRS G1-2, G1-6

• Cancellation of licenses and permits;

• Otherwise directly or indirectly causing adverse
consequences, including by violating the principle
of equal treatment.
AST may only disclose the identity of the whistleblower
in accordance with the procedures specified in
regulatory enactments. The whistleblower shall be
informed of the disclosure of the identity, except in
cases where the information would prejudice the
consideration of the whistleblower’s report or the case of
a violation initiated on its basis.
In accordance with the provisions of the Conexus Code
and the Whistleblower Procedure, the Subsidiary shall
not apply any sanctions or other adverse consequences
to an employee who has informed about a provision
of the Code or another violation, except in cases
where false information has been knowingly provided.
In order to protect the whistleblower from negative
consequences (repression, retaliation and other possible
consequences), whistleblower applications are received
by the Head of the Internal Audit Department, who only
retains personal data in the original whistleblower report
and pseudonymises them.

The Group’s procurement objective is to ensure uniform,
transparent and responsible procurement principles that
promote efficient use of resources and fair competition.
Procurements conducted by the Group’s companies
are carried out in compliance with Public Service
Provider Procurement Law, requirements stipulated
in Cabinet regulations and other applicable regulatory
enactments, as well as the explanations of the European
Commission and guidelines of competent institutions,
including the Procurement Monitoring Bureau
"Procurement guidelines for public service providers".
The Parent Company purchases goods and services
through centralised purchasing, for example, using the
Electronic procurement system’s e-order sub-system,
where the procurement procedures have been carried
out by the State Digital Development Agency. These
procurements are mainly related to the maintenance,
development and security of information technology
infrastructure: maintenance and development of licenses
and software; data security and cybersecurity solutions;
maintenance and modernisation of server and data
centre infrastructure; computer equipment, printing
equipment, etc. The aforementioned procurements
contribute to digital security, system stability and

efficient use of resources, ensuring a reliable and
sustainable IT environment for business continuity.

• open tenders, where all interested parties can
submit offers;

In certain cases, the Group implements centralised
procurements, in which the Parent Company cooperates
with several public service providers from other
European Union Member States, jointly awarding the
contract. For example, the procurement "Technical
and economic study service for the fourth electricity
interconnection between Estonia and Latvia" (ID. No.
AST2025/75) was also carried out for the needs of the
Estonian transmission system operator AS Elering. In
turn, the procurement "Maintenance and development
of the Baltic Transparency Information System" (ID.
No. AST2025/84) was implemented for the joint needs
of AS Elering and AB Litgrid (Republic of Lithuania).
Such an approach promotes regional cooperation, more
efficient use of resources, and a unified approach to the
development of electricity transmission networks in the
Baltic region.

• open negotiated procedures, which allow for the
specification of offers and contract terms through
negotiation;

The Parent Company’s low-threshold procurement
procedures are carried out in accordance with a specific
procedure that ensures transparency, competition and
efficient use of resources. Depending on the estimated
value of the contract, different procedures are applied:
• price survey (EUR 4,000–10,000);
• simplified procurement (EUR 10,000–42,000);
• procurement procedures above EUR 42,000 –
organised by the procurement commission, applying
the appropriate type of procedure:

• restricted tenders or restricted negotiated
procedures, where selected suppliers are invited.
The Parent Company’s 2025 procurement plan provides
for procurement procedures, including procurement
procedures applying the Law on Procurement of Public
Service Providers, as well as purchases made in the
e-orders section of the Electronic Procurement System.
In 2025, 167 procurement procedures were actually
carried out. At the end of 2025, the Parent Company
approved the 2026 procurement plan, which provides
for 221 procurement procedures.
Conexus sub-threshold procurement procedures are
conducted in accordance with established procedures
that ensure transparency, fair competition, and the
efficient use of resources.
Depending on the estimated contract value, different
procedures are applied: price survey (EUR 3,000–
50,000); procurement procedures above EUR 50,000
up to the applicable threshold – organised by the
Procurement Commission, applying the appropriate
procedure type: open negotiated procedure, which
allows for the clarification of tenders and contractual
terms through negotiations; restricted negotiated
procedure, in which selected suppliers are invited; price
survey, if the Head of the Procurement Department
144


=== pdf p.145 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

determines that its application is the most efficient
approach.
The Conexus procurement plan for 2025 envisaged
procurement procedures, including those conducted in
accordance with the Public Utilities Procurement Law.
The procurement framework provides for the possibility
of carrying out purchases via the e-ordering module
of the Electronic Procurement System. In 2025, a total
of 259 procurement procedures were carried out. The
Company’s procurement plan for 2026 provides for 186
procurement procedures.
In order to ensure free and direct access to procurement
documents and prevent the risk of corruption, the
Group’s announced procurements are published in
the Electronic Procurement System (www.eis.gov.lv)
in the e-tendering subsystem, Procurement Monitoring
Bureau Publication Management System, in the Official
Journal of the European Union (where applicable), and
on the relevant websites of the Parent Company and the
Subsidiary.
Public procurement is carried out to purchase services,
goods and works, but it is also a tool for achieving
strategic objectives. Strategic public procurement is
understood as public procurement that can achieve
one of the objectives in the social, environmental
or innovation fields. The Group also implements
sustainable procurement, as a result of which the
purchased services, goods and works have a positive
impact on the environment, society and the economy
throughout their lifecycle, while reducing potential
negative impacts.

GREEN PUBLIC
PROCUREMENT
Green Public Procurement (GPP) promotes
environmentally friendly solutions by including
requirements that reduce negative environmental
impacts, such as the selection of energy-efficient
equipment and vehicles. In accordance with the Cabinet
regulations, the Group applies GPP requirements in
its procurement. Such an approach helps to reduce
emissions, promotes the efficient use of resources and
supports sustainable procurement practices.
For example, in 2025, AST procurement includes the
provision of an environmental impact assessment
service for a new power transmission line (ID. No.
AST2024/146-ES), the purchase of hybrid and
electric vehicles (ID. No. AST2025/123), the use of
environmentally friendly cleaning products and cleaning
services (ID. No. AST2025/47), hazardous waste
management, maintenance and repair of a sandoil separator, maintenance and repair of biological
wastewater treatment plants (ID. No. AST2025/33).
Similarly, the AST qualification requirements for energy
supply facility construction contractors stipulate that
the construction company must have implemented
and maintained a quality management system that
meets the requirements of the international standard
ISO 9001 or equivalent, as well as an environmental
management system that meets the criteria of ISO
14001, EMAS or equivalent. Such requirements ensure

that high standards of quality, safety and environmental
management are adhered to in the execution of
construction works, promoting the implementation
of sustainable and responsible projects in
the energy sector.

SOCIALLY RESPONSIBLE
PUBLIC PROCUREMENT
The AST qualification requirements for energy
supply facility construction employees provide that
the construction company’s employees are provided
with fair and honest remuneration that corresponds
to the industry average. The average hourly rate of
the applicant’s employees in all occupational groups
in the first three quarters of the year and in the last
four quarters up to the date of submission of the offer
must not be less than 80% of the national average
hourly rate in the relevant occupational group or at
least reach the national minimum hourly rate. This
requirement promotes social responsibility, labour
market sustainability and fair competition, while at the
same time mitigating the risks associated with unfair
employment practices and the shadow economy in the
construction industry.

INNOVATION
PROCUREMENT
The Parent Company’s innovation procurements
are implemented with the aim of developing and
implementing new or significantly improved solutions,
especially in the field of information and communication
technologies, where there are no ready-made products
or services on the market that meet the needs of the
Parent Company.
The innovative procurements implemented in
2025 contributed to the implementation of digital
transformation and security solutions in the electricity
transmission and storage sector. They included the
development and implementation of new IT and data
management systems, data security and market
modelling solutions, as well as modern video wall
and network protection infrastructure for dispatcher
efficiency and system stability. This approach enables
the Parent Company to improve operational processes,
strengthen network and data security, improve digital
infrastructure and automate decision-making and
management systems. Innovation procurements
promote cooperation with research institutions and
technology companies, thus facilitating knowledge
transfer and commercialisation of new technologies.
These procurements ensure more efficient use of
resources and strengthen the sustainability and
competitiveness of the Latvian energy sector in
the long term.

CONDITIONS FOR
EXCLUSION OF
CANDIDATES
Group procurement excludes candidates and tenderers
if the grounds for exclusion set out in Article 48 of
the Law on Procurement of Public Service Providers
apply, including criminal offences, non-fulfilment of
tax obligations, violations of environmental, social or
labour law, competition offences, insolvency, as well
as provision of misleading or false information. The
contracting authority also assesses conflicts of interest,
undue influence on decisions and lack of professional
integrity of tenderers, ensuring transparency and equal
treatment. Checks are carried out using the registers
of competent authorities and requesting evidence of
compliance. The law also provides for the possibility
of "re-establishing credibility" if the tenderer
demonstrates prevention of damage and implementation
of internal control measures, and in certain cases
applies time limits (e.g. three years). This approach
strengthens a sustainable and ethical supply chain,
thereby reducing the risks of corruption, fraud and
human rights violations.
In low-threshold procurement procedures, simplified
exclusion rules are applied to ensure a legal, transparent
and secure procurement environment. The procurer
checks whether the tenderer and the persons on whose
capabilities it relies are not in the State Revenue Service
debtors’ register with a tax debt of over EUR 150, as

well as whether insolvency proceedings have been
declared, economic activities have been suspended
or liquidation has been initiated in the Enterprise
Register or the Lursoft system. This approach ensures
that the contract is awarded only to tenderers who do
not have legal, financial or security restrictions that
could jeopardise the performance of the contract or
public safety.

• fair competition,
• compliance with laws and contracts,
• prevention of conflict of interest,
• prohibition of fraud and corruption,
• prohibition of discrimination,
• sustainable development,
• social responsibility.

If the procurement is related to critical infrastructure,
additional security and cybersecurity requirements are
applied, as well as an assessment of the reliability of
the persons by the state security authorities.
In accordance with the requirements of the Law on
International and National Sanctions of the Republic
of Latvia, the Group excludes a bidder from the
procurement if international or national sanctions have
been imposed on the bidder itself or its Management
Board/Supervisory Board Members, beneficial owners,
authorised representatives, procurators or branch
representatives, as well as sanctions of an EU or NATO
member state affecting significant financial market
interests, which impede the execution of the contract.
Checks are carried out using data from competent
authorities and public registers. The Group has the
right to unilaterally withdraw from the execution of
the contract if the execution of the contract cannot be
objectively ensured due to the application of sanctions.
Each AST supplier, when submitting an application for
participation in the AST procurement, signs the Supplier
Declaration (available at: https://www.ast.lv/lv/content/
atbildiga-uznemejdarbiba), Committed to the following
principles:

PAYMENT PRACTICE

Arbitration, more detailed information is currently
considered confidential. The Subsidiary has no legal
proceedings related to late payments.

The Parent Company’s standard payment terms are
set out in the internal regulations "Basic Rules for
Procurement Procedures", which provide for payment
within 30 days after receipt of the goods or services,
performance of construction works and receipt of the
invoice. In individual cases, other payment terms are
also possible if they are justified by the specifics of
the procurement. The Parent Company applies equal
payment procedures to all cooperation partners,
regardless of their scale.

In 2025, the Subsidiary paid 96% of all received invoices
within 30 days of receipt of the invoice, 4% of invoices
are paid within more than 30 days. In 2025, the average
invoice payment period was 14 days. The information
provided is based on the actual invoice payment data
in the Subsidiary’s financial accounting system.

In 2025, the Parent Company paid 96% (in 2024 - 96%)
of all received invoices within 30 days after receipt
of the invoice, 4% (in 2024 - 4%) of invoices are paid
within longer than 30 days. In 2025, the average invoice
payment time was 21 days (in 2024 - 22 days). The
information provided is based on the actual invoice
payment data in the Parent Company’s financial
accounting system.
As a result of the implementation of the international
sanctions regulation, the Parent Company has 1 (one)
pending legal proceedings related to late payment.
Legal proceedings were initiated in the Stockholm
International Arbitration against the Baltic Transmission
System Operators, including AS Augstsprieguma tīkls,
the consideration of which is currently temporarily
suspended. The legal proceedings are related to the
fact that, in compliance with the international sanctions
regulation, the operators have not made settlements. In
accordance with the rules of the Stockholm International

The Subsidiary does not have a separate policy to
prevent late payments and target Indicators, but in
practice a standard payment term is used, which is
1-3 days before the due date specified in the invoice or
contract. The same payment terms are applied to
all suppliers and service providers.
According to the Subsidiary’s internal procedure
"Order of circulation of received invoices", if an invoice
does not specify a payment term and no contract has
been concluded with the partner, the invoice payment
term is set within 30 days from the later date – either
from the date of issuing the invoice or its receipt. The
order of circulation of received invoices sets out the
principles of invoice processing in cases where the
payment term specified in the received invoice is
shorter than seven days.

10

INFORMATION
ABOUT GREEN
PROJECTS

The Parent Company is the first electricity transmission
system operator in the Baltics to issue green bonds.
The first green bond issuance in 2021 was a significant
step in the development of AST, implementing ambitious
capital investment projects and ensuring progress
towards the synchronisation of the Latvian and Baltic
electricity transmission networks with European
networks.
The bond issuance programme was launched in
October 2021 with the issuance of green bonds in
the amount of EUR 100 million. The bonds of AS
Augstsprieguma tīkls are included in the Baltic debt
securities list and are listed on the AS Nasdaq Riga
exchange. No further bond issuances have been made
within the framework of the programme. At the end of
the reporting period, the amount of outstanding bonds is
EUR 100 million, which constitutes 100% of the Parent
Company’s total borrowings.
In accordance with the Parent Company’s goal – to
ensure continuous, safe and sustainably efficient
electricity transmission throughout Latvia – AST
developed a green bond framework, which aims to
ensure the use of raised funds in environmentally
friendly projects, as well as their management
procedures and monitoring.
The green bond framework sets guidelines for the
use of proceeds from bond issuance for sustainable
development, the selection and selection process of
green projects, and the management and reporting of
proceeds from bond issuance.
When evaluating the AST green bond framework, an

opinion was received from an independent expert,
Standard & Poor’s Financial Services LLC, on its
compliance with the 2021 Green Bond Principles of
the International Capital Markets Association.
In accordance with the Green Bond Framework,
projects are divided into three groups:
• renewable energy;
• energy efficiency;
• quality, security and resilience of electricity
transmission infrastructure.
The funds raised within the framework of the bond issue
in the amount of EUR 100 million were fully utilised
by 31 December 2022 (the funds were utilised, while
project implementation continued in 2025), detailed
information is provided in the AST 2022 Sustainability
Report (see www.ast.lv).
When determining the eligible projects, the Parent
Company was guided by the Green Bond Framework.
Transformer Replacement projects are implemented in
compliance with the eco-design requirements for power
transformers (in accordance with Directive 2009/125/
EC of the European Parliament and of the Council) and
lifecycle costing. Accordingly, a balance is achieved
between the price of the transformer, the level of losses
in the lifecycle and the costs of losses (transformers are
efficient and are not overcapitalised).
Within the framework of the substation reconstruction
projects implemented by AST, the equipment installed in
it that has served its time is completely replaced - circuit
148


=== pdf p.149 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

breakers, disconnectors, transformers, etc., as well
as the protection equipment of network elements are
replaced, replacing the existing electromechanical relays
with digital and high-speed equipment with much wider
functionality. In addition, by modernising communication
solutions, it is ensured that the reconstructed substation
can be controlled not only from the workplace in the
substation itself, but also remotely from the central
dispatch centre, with the dispatcher directly receiving
all the necessary information for decision-making and
action both in the normal operating mode of the system
and in various emergency situations.
In addition to the aforementioned activities, as a result
of the substation reconstruction, a new energy-efficient
control building is being constructed, which houses the
new relay protection and dispatcher control systems,
as well as updated facility security and fire protection
solutions.
In addition to the above, it can be concluded that the
reconstruction and renovation of substations reduces
CO2 emissions during the lifecycle. By replacing
outdated circuit breakers, disconnectors, relay protection
and automation, and dispatcher control equipment,
maintenance and renewal repairs of these equipment
need to be carried out less frequently, i.e. the CO2 eq. t
emissions released during repairs are lower.
