11

COMPLIANCE
WITH EU TAXONOMY
REQUIREMENTS

Disclosures on the proportion of capital
investment, operating expenses and
turnover in the 2025 reporting year that
meet the requirements of the EU Taxonomy
Regulation
The European Green Deal is Europe’s new strategy
for sustainable and efficient growth. To achieve the
EU’s climate and energy targets, it is essential that
investments are directed towards sustainable projects
and activities.
The EU Taxonomy is one of the cornerstones of the
European Green Deal, which aims to facilitate the
redirection of capital flows towards more sustainable
activities. The taxonomy is a unified system for
classifying sustainable economic activities, which for
each sector defines the set of economic activities that
are considered sustainable and make a significant
contribution to one or more of six environmental
protection objectives – climate change mitigation,
climate change adaptation, sustainable use and
protection of water and marine resources, transition to
a circular economy, pollution prevention and control,
and restoration and protection of biodiversity and
ecosystems.
In accordance with Article 10(2) of the EU Delegated
Regulation 2021/2178, the Group discloses quantitative
information on the proportion of Taxonomy aligned
and non-aligned economic activities for each of the
three identified Indicators (KPIs) — turnover, capital
expenditure (CapEx), operating expenses (OpEx).
In order to identify which activities of the Group are
eligible to and aligned with the Taxonomy, the Group’s

methodological guidelines were developed in 2025. The
proportion of taxonomy‑eligible and taxonomy‑aligned
activities is determined based on the breakdown of
operating segments (see detailed information in Annex 3
to the financial statements). Based on these guidelines,
two eligible economic activities have been identified
within the Group:
• 4.9. Transmission and distribution of electricity (covers
the majority of AST’s activities);
• 4.14. Transmission and distribution networks for
renewable and low-carbon gases (applicable to a
relatively small part of the current Conexus activity
related to the injection of biomethane into the
common gas transmission system; in the future,
as biomethane injection and/or hydrogen transmission
capabilities are developed, a larger part could
be eligible).
The Group has assessed alignment of the above
activities with the criteria set out in the Taxonomy,
including both the technical screening criteriaas well
as "Do No Significant Harm" and minimum social
safeguards criteria. The parent company of the
Augstsprieguma tīkls Group, JSC "Augstsprieguma
tīkls", operates within a single primary operating
segment—electricity transmission. In accordance
with the criteria set out in Section 4.9 of Commission
Delegated Regulation (EU) 2021/2178, and considering
that the parent company’s economic activity—electricity
transmission—is carried out through a single electricity
transmission network forming part of the interconnected
European power system, the Group’s management
has assessed all economic activities and reported the
153


=== pdf p.154 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

corresponding key performance indicators (KPIs)—
turnover, capital expenditure (CapEx), and operating
expenditure (OpEx)—as taxonomy‑eligible and
taxonomy‑aligned activities within a single economic
activity category (code 4.9). This assessment and
conclusion by the Group’s management constitute a
significant judgment in the evaluation of EU Taxonomy
eligibility and, consequently, alignment. This judgment
will be applied consistently in the reporting period and in
subsequent periods.
In the case of Conexus, only those activities related
to the injection of biomethane into the common
gas transmission system and its possible storage
(code 4.14) can be considered taxonomy‑eligible
and taxonomy‑aligned. Conexus has made capital
investments in the creation of new biomethane entry
points or in the acquisition of already established
injection points from private operators, and according to
the assessment carried out, these capital investments
deemed to be aligned with the EU Taxonomy.
Accordingly, in the case of revenues, only those
revenues generated from the injection of biomethane
can be considered to be aligned with the Taxonomy,
indicating the revenues from specific biomethane entry
points in absolute figures, but in 2025, such revenues
have not yet been generated. In the case of operating
expenses, only expenses related to the maintenance
of the biomethane entry point and related infrastructure
can be considered as Taxonomy-aligned, and the
company calculates them proportionally by determining
the maintenance costs per gas station, which also
corresponds to the functionality of the biomethane
entry point. All other capital investments, revenues
and expenses related to Conexus’s operations in the

natural gas segment are considered non-eligible to the
Taxonomy.
For all of the abovementioned activities, the Group has
carried out a detailed assessment, primarily evaluating
compliance with the technical screening criteria
(significant contribution criteria), including the following:
• With regard to activity code 4.9 – as noted
above, the infrastructure operated by AST forms
part of the interconnected European electricity
transmission system and comprises 110 kV and
330 kV transmission lines and related infrastructure,
including interconnections with the Estonian and
Lithuanian transmission systems, which are further
interconnected with the transmission systems of
other European countries. Accordingly, alignment
with the relevant technical screening criteria is
ensured at the system level. The parent company
does not conduct assessment of the technical
screening criteria (including "Do No Significant
Harm" criteria) individually for each CapEx project,
all new projects correspond to activities considered
taxonomyaligned, including, inter alia, the construction
of new interconnections or the expansion of existing
interconnections, the installation of transmission
transformers, and the deployment of equipment
and infrastructure aimed at increasing the use of
renewable electricity, enhancing system controllability,
and achieving other related objectives;
• In addition, it is taken into account that transmission
infrastructure developed to establish or expand
connections between substations or with power
generation facilities whose greenhouse gas (GHG)

emission intensity exceeds 100 g CO₂e/kWh cannot
be considered taxonomy‑aligned. Given that in recent
years AST has established all direct connections
solely for the purpose of connecting new renewable
energy capacities to the grid, this criterion is met;
namely, no new connections or expansions of existing
connections are developed in areas where renewable
energy sources are not used;
• Furthermore, compliance with the criterion concerning
smart metering systems in accordance with the
requirements of Directive (EU) 2019/944 is ensured—
all smart electricity metering solutions installed by
AST within the transmission network comply with the
provisions of this Directive;
• With regard to activity code 4.14 – activities related
to the establishment, maintenance, and operation of
biomethane injection points are aligned with the EU
Taxonomy criterion "Retrofitting of gas transmission
and distribution networks to enable the integration
of hydrogen and other low‑carbon gases into the
network," which includes, inter alia, any activities
that enable an increase in the blending of such
gases—in this case, biomethane, which qualifies
as a low‑carbon gas—into the overall gas system;
• In addition, the company ensures that biomethane
injection point construction projects include
requirements for the implementation of gas leak
detection and control mechanisms in order to
prevent or reduce potential methane leaks. These
include, inter alia, the installation of chromatographs,
automated flow control systems, backflow safety
valves, leak detectors, and other relevant equipment,

thereby ensuring alignment with the EU Taxonomy
technical screening criterion on the provision of leak
control measures.
The Group has also carried out an assessment of
compliance with the "Do No Significant Harm" (DNSH)
criteria, including the following:
• With regard to activity codes 4.9 and 4.14, the
Group conducted a climate risk assessment of its
operations in 2025, thereby meeting the criterion
related to climate change adaptation (for more
detailed information on the climate risk assessment,
please refer to the Climate Change section of the
sustainability report);
• With regard to activity code 4.9, AST meets
the transition to a circular economy criteria by
implementing measures defined within the company’s
environmental protection processes for material
recovery, the return of resources to the economic
cycle, and the management of waste (including
hazardous waste), as well as by incorporating waste
management‑related requirements into contracts with
construction project contractors;
• In the area of pollution prevention and control,
AST has not implemented a dedicated process
specifically for the application of the IFC (International
Finance Corporation) General Environmental,
Health and Safety Guidelines in the implementation
of construction projects, as required by the EU
Taxonomy. However, AST carries out its operations
in accordance with ISO 9001, ISO 14001, ISO 50001,
and ISO 45001 standards, which, inter alia, ensure
154


=== pdf p.155 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

compliance with the requirements set out in the IFC
guidelines in the fields of environmental protection,
occupational health and safety, and other related
areas. In the autumn of 2025, a detailed assessment/
mapping of the IFC guidelines against AST’s existing
processes was conducted, and no compliance gaps
were identified;

in compliance with all applicable recommendations,
including those related to biodiversity. In 2025, the
only AST development project subject to an EIA
under the Law on Environmental Impact Assessment
is the construction of the Ventspils–Brocēni–Vurdava
(Lithuania) transmission line. As of early 2026, the
EIA process has not yet been completed.

• In the course of its day‑to‑day operations, the
company implements measures, in line with the
requirements of the EU Taxonomy, to conduct
electromagnetic field measurements and to ensure
appropriate planning and control of work execution,
so as to prevent the company’s own employees
and subcontractors from being exposed to
electromagnetic fields beyond permissible levels. In
this context, aspects related to electromagnetic fields
and the relevant safety requirements are incorporated
into instructions applicable both to the company’s
employees and to contractors performing work at the
company’s facilities;

• For the purposes of day‑to‑day operational planning,
the company has carried out mapping of protected
areas and identified crossing points where interfaces
occur between AST‑operated infrastructure and
protected areas, and takes these into account
when planning its activities. The company has also
established procedures defining actions to be taken
in cases involving the removal of nests of specially
protected bird species or disturbance of individual
birds. Furthermore, AST has obtained permits
from the Nature Conservation Agency for activities
affecting two bird species—the white stork and the
lesser spotted eagle. In order to mitigate the impact
of its activities on bird species, AST has entered into
a cooperation agreement with the Ornithological
Society and jointly implements the necessary
measures;

• The EU Taxonomy provides that polychlorinated
biphenyls (PCBs) are not to be used in electricity
transmission activities. AST discontinued the use of
such substances in its operations as early as 2005,
when—while still operating as part of the Latvenergo
Group—it participated in a project aimed at removing
from circulation equipment and assets containing
such substances;
• With regard to the protection of biodiversity – in
cases where an environmental impact assessment
(EIA) is required for the implementation of new
projects by AST, such assessments are carried out

• With regard to activity code 4.14 – measures
for the protection of water resources in day‑to‑day
operations are ensured within the framework of an
ISO 14001‑certified environmental management
system. The majority of Conexus’s activities
are carried out within the boundaries of existing
infrastructure; therefore, no new environmental impact
assessments (EIAs) are required. At one site—the
Inčukalns Underground Gas Storage facility—the

company holds a Category A polluting activity permit,
which, inter alia, establishes specific obligations in
relation to water resources, including monitoring, data
reporting, limitations, and other requirements;
• In order to mitigate and prevent potential pollution
caused by equipment (such as fans, compressors,
pumps, etc.) in accordance with the EU Taxonomy
criteria, the company requires CE energy labelling for
purchased equipment;
• With regard to biodiversity conservation, all
development projects are implemented within the
scope of Conexus’s existing infrastructure and
therefore do not require an Environmental Impact
Assessment (EIA). For the planning of day‑to‑day
operations, the company has carried out mapping
of protected areas and identified crossings where
points of contact arise between the infrastructure
managed by Conexus and protected areas, and this
is taken into account when planning its activities. The
company’s operations in these areas are accordingly
supervised by the Nature Conservation Agency.
The Group has also assessed the implementation of
minimum social safeguards in accordance with the
requirements of the EU Taxonomy, which it ensures
through the following measures:
• AST and Conexus Codes of Ethics (sets out the
basic principles of conduct for employees, including
regarding a safe and inclusive work environment,
non-discrimination in the field of direct or indirect
discrimination, etc.);

• Whistleblowing procedures developed by AST and
Conexus (availability of mechanisms for reporting
violations of labour law and other issues, which
provide the necessary protection for the whistleblower
and a clear process for reviewing the report);
• AST Fraud, corruption and conflict of interest risk
management rules (sets out the framework and
measures for managing the aforementioned risks both
in the company’s own operations and in relations with
external partners, ensuring active risk identification,
monitoring and prevention at all stages and areas
of operation) and Conexus internal regulatory
documents governing specific measures and the
company’s conduct in the relevant areas (including
the Code of Ethics, the Corporate Governance Policy,
and others);
• AST Remuneration Policy and Conexus Personnel
Policy (sets out fair principles for determining
and ensuring employee remuneration, including
preventing discrimination in the field of remuneration).
The AST Personnel Strategy defines measures
aimed at promoting a diverse and inclusive working
environment. The Conexus Code of Ethics sets out
the fundamental principles for a non‑discriminatory
and inclusive working environment;
• AST and Conexus Occupational Health and
Safety Policy (sets out measures for ensuring and
implementing a safe and healthy work environment,
as well as ensuring continuous monitoring);
• Supplier Declaration – a statement included in
AST contracts with suppliers, in which they confirm
155


=== pdf p.156 ===
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT

responsible behaviour in the areas of fair competition,
conflict of interest management, prohibition of
fraud and corruption, prohibition of discrimination,
sustainable development, social responsibility
(including fair remuneration) and other areas.
Similarly, Conexus has integrated the Supplier Code
of Ethics into its Procurement Policy and requires
adherence to these principles both in its own activities
and in its cooperation with suppliers;
• Work instructions for contractors hired by
AS Augstsprieguma tīkls and AS Conexus Baltic Grid,
introductory labour protection training instructions for
contractor personnel (especially for those companies
performing work at AST/Conexus facilities), which set
out requirements in the areas of labour protection, fire
safety, environment and energy management, as well
as conditions for action in emergency situations. AST
contracts with contractors provide for sanctions in
case of violations of the requirements specified in
the instructions.
The abovementioned assessment of the alignment
(including "Do No Significant Harm" assessment) in
the Group has been conducted and documented in
2025. According to the management of the Group,
the assessment is also applicable to the information
disclosed in this report for 2024, thus providing the
reasoning behind alignment of the AST economic
activities with the EU Taxonomy criteria.

The following considerations have been considered
when reporting financial data related to Taxonomy:
• Turnover. Includes all AST and Conexus revenues
from external customers, generated in accordance
with the electricity or natural gas transmission or
natural gas storage tariff, and allocated according to
the Taxonomy eligibility and alignment requirements.
Detailed information on the accounting policy for
revenue recognition in accordance with International
Financial Reporting Standards is provided in the
financial statements, Note 2.9;

changes in leased assets recorded in the Group’s
financial accounts in 2025 were solely attributable to
amendments to lease agreements; accordingly, these
financial indicators are not included in the capital
expenditure table presented below.

• Operating costs. Includes all direct costs related to
the maintenance of AST and Conexus infrastructure
and the provision of services, and allocated
according to the Taxonomy eligibility and alignment
requirements. Does not include costs related to
depreciation and amortisation and financial expenses
(e.g., interest payments). Detailed information on
the accounting policy for revenue recognition in
accordance with International Financial Reporting
Standards is provided in the financial statements,
Note 2.;
• Capital expenditures. All capital investments made
in 2025 by the Group companies to renew, modernise
or build new infrastructure or necessary for its
operation are included, and are classified according to
the Taxonomy eligibility and alignment requirements.
Detailed information on the investments made by the
Group is provided in the financial statements, Notes
2. and 10. Pursuant to the EU Taxonomy Regulation,
additions to leased assets are required to be included
in capital expenditure accounting. However, the

INDEPENDENT
AUDITOR'S LIMITED
ASSURANCE REPORT
ON THE SUSTAINABILITY
REPORT

LIMITED ASSURANCE REPORT ON
AS "AUGSTSPRIEGUMA TĪKLS" CONSOLIDATED
SUSTAINABILITY STATEMENT
TO THE SHAREHOLDER OF AS "AUGSTSPRIEGUMA TĪKLS"

LIMITED ASSURANCE
CONCLUSION

Group as of and for the year ended 31 December 2025
is not prepared, in all material respects, in accordance
with the Article 7 of the Sustainability Disclosure Law of
the Republic of Latvia implementing Article 29(a) of EU
Directive 2013/34/EU, including:

We have performed a limited assurance engagement
on whether the consolidated Sustainability Statement,
of AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls" (the "Company") and

OUR RESPONSIBILITIES
Our objectives are to plan and perform the assurance
engagement to obtain limited assurance about
whether the Sustainability Statement is free from
material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error,

and reporting our limited assurance conclusion
to the shareholders of AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls".
Misstatements can arise from fraud or error and
are considered material if, individually or in the
aggregate, they could reasonably be expected to
influence decisions of users taken on the basis of the
Sustainability Statement as a whole.
Our responsibilities in relation to the Process for
reporting the Sustainability Statement, include:
• Obtaining an understanding of the Process but not
for the purpose of providing a conclusion on the
effectiveness of the Process, including the outcome of
the Process; and
• Designing and performing procedures to evaluate
whether the Process is consistent with the Group’s

relevant to the preparation of the Sustainability
Statement but not evaluating the design of
particular control activities, obtaining evidence
about their implementation or testing their operating
effectiveness;

are likely to arise, whether due to fraud or error; and
• Designing and performing procedures focused on
disclosures in the Sustainability Statement where
material misstatements are likely to arise. The risk
of not detecting a material misstatement resulting
from fraud is higher than for one resulting from error,
as fraud may involve collusion, forgery, intentional
omissions, misrepresentations, or the override of
internal control.
Summary of the work we performed as the basis
for our conclusion
A limited assurance engagement involves performing
procedures to obtain evidence about the Sustainability
Statement. We designed and performed our procedures
to obtain evidence about the Sustainability Statement
that is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis
for our conclusion. The nature, timing and extent of
our procedures depended on our understanding of
the Sustainability Statement and other engagement
circumstances, including the identification of disclosures
where material misstatements are likely to arise,
whether due to fraud or error, in the Sustainability
Statement. We exercised professional judgment
and maintained professional scepticism throughout
the engagement.

=== pdf p.174 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

2

MANAGEMENT
REPORT

SIGNIFICANT FACTS
AND EVENTS
The Baltic States successfully joined
the European energy network
Estonia, Latvia, and Lithuania successfully synchronised
their electricity systems with continental Europe on
9 February 2025. This was a significant historic step for
the Baltic States and Europe as a whole, strengthening
the energy resilience and independence of the region.
Synchronisation allows the Baltic States to manage
their electricity networks in close cooperation with other
continental European countries, ensuring stable and
reliable frequency control, thereby significantly improving
regional energy security.
This process was the result of several years of careful
preparation and cooperation between the Baltic
transmission system operators and their partners in the
European Synchronous Area.
To achieve this result, significant investments have
been made in the development and strengthening of
infrastructure in the Baltic States and Poland. In 2025,
with the financial support of the European Commission,
AST, in addition to the previously made investments,
continued and completed the delivery and installation of
synchronous compensators at the substations "Grobina",
"Ventspils", and "Liksna", as well as the implementation
of high-capacity electricity storage facilities at the
substations "Rezekne" and "Tume".

As the final step towards establishing independent
synchronous operation with the Continental European
electricity transmission system, at the end of 2025,
the Synchronous Area Framework Agreement for
Regional Group Continental Europe was concluded with
the transmission system operators of the Continental
European Regional Group and the European Network
of Transmission System Operators for Electricity
(ENTSO-E).

The Baltic balancing capacity market
has launched successfully
In February 2025, the Baltic balancing capacity market,
formed by the Estonian, Latvian, and Lithuanian
transmission system operators AS "Elering",
AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls", and AS "Litgrid", began
operating. The new market ensures the necessary
capacity reserve for system balancing and frequency
control after the disconnection of the Baltic power
systems from the Russian-controlled IPS/UPS system
and synchronisation with continental Europe. In the
future, as the share of renewable energy in the common
market increases, the capacity market will also be an
important tool for market participants, allowing them to
obtain additional revenues.

174


=== pdf p.175 ===
ANNUAL REPORT
AST joined the European balancing energy
exchange platform PICASSO
On 10 April 2025, Latvian electricity transmission
system operator AST successfully joined the
European frequency restoration reserves with
automatic activation (aFRR) energy exchange
platform PICASSO. This platform not only provides
market functions and access to the single regional
market, but also allows for the optimisation of
the quantity of reserves used, thereby reducing
balancing costs.

The electricity market in Latvia and
across Europe switched to a 15-minute
trading interval
In order to ensure the broad development of renewable
energy production and introduce wider competition,
both by expanding the market geographically and
by introducing new types of services, European
transmission system operators, including AS
"Augstsprieguma tīkls", and electricity exchanges have
been working on improving the electricity market model
for several years. As a result of the aforementioned
work, from 1 October 2025, the day-ahead trading
interval on electricity exchanges across Europe was
changed from 60 minutes previously to 15 minutes.
Along with the changes in the day-ahead electricity
market, a 15-minute trading period has also been
introduced in the Baltic balancing capacity market. This
change will allow Baltic transmission system operators to
more accurately plan and purchase balancing capacity
reserves, as well as to book the inter-system capacities
necessary for balancing the electricity system. This

will provide balancing capacity market participants,
especially those whose portfolio includes renewable
energy resources or resources with high flexibility, with
the opportunity to offer capacity maintenance for shorter
periods and plan capacity provision periods more
accurately.

The electricity transmission service tariff
has been approved, ensuring its stability
for the next three years
The Public Utilities Commission (hereinafter referred to
as "PUC") approved the electricity transmission tariffs
of the Latvian transmission system operator AST for
the next three years, setting them to come into force on
1 January 2026. AST ensured the competitiveness of
tariffs in the Baltics and long-term stability.
AST managed to maintain the stability of electricity
transmission tariffs despite the challenges within the
regulatory period, as well as the ambitious development
plans for the security of the energy system and the
strengthening of the network in the coming years.

A unique system for efficient battery
management has been developed
In order to ensure the efficient operation of battery
storage systems (hereinafter referred to as BESS) and
to fully utilise their capacity, AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls"
together with scientists from Riga Technical University
(RTU) developed a unique battery management system.
The IT solution developed in cooperation between AST
and RTU will allow batteries to be managed according
to a certain algorithm and the situation in the network,

charging or discharging electricity into the network.
The modern battery system will provide the necessary
high-speed and automatically activated frequency
regulation reserves for the synchronisation mode, in
which the electricity transmission systems of the Baltic
States operate synchronously with the power grids of
continental Europe. The battery system will be able to
provide the necessary reserves more economically than
most conventional stations.

will benefit market participants by providing wider choice
and better services.
In November 2025, a new operator – EPEX SPOT –
started operating in the intraday market in Estonia,
Latvia, and Lithuania. Until then, the only nominated
electricity market operator in the Baltic electricity market
was Nord Pool.

Innovative solutions to increase throughput
are being tested in Latvia’s electricity
transmission network
In order to increase the capacity of the existing electricity
transmission network and reduce the possibility of
network overload and the need to limit renewable
energy generation in the future, AST implemented a pilot
project, within which dynamic capacity determination
solutions (DLR "Dynamic Line Rating") are being tested
on the 110 kV high-voltage overhead line between
Iecava and Jelgava. The aforementioned solution will
increase the maximum use of the existing electricity
network and promote greater integration of renewable
energy into the transmission network, using the existing
transmission network without immediate significant
network reconstruction.

The second electricity exchange in the
Baltic States has launched operations
The Baltic electricity transmission system operators,
together with their partners, have implemented a solution
that allows multiple electricity exchanges to operate in
the market. Competition between electricity exchanges
175

ANNUAL REPORT

BUSINESS MODEL
DESCRIPTION
Our mission is to ensure a continuous, secure, and
sustainably efficient energy supply throughout Latvia.
Detailed information on the Group’s business model,
overall strategic objectives, and medium-term operating
strategy is provided in the Sustainability Report section
"Business Model and Strategy". Information on the
Group’s ownership interests in its Subsidiary and the

associate, Baltic RCC, and their location is disclosed in
Note 11 to the financial statements.
The Group’s structure is organised into three operating
segments: electricity transmission, natural gas
transmission, and natural gas storage. This division is
based on the Group’s internal organisational structure,
which serves as the basis for monitoring and controlling
segment performance. Further information on the
operating segments and the Parent Company, the
characteristics of the operating environment, and the
development of the operating segments is provided in
the Sustainability Report section "Operating Segments".

FINANCIAL
PERFORMANCE
During the reporting period, the Group’s net turnover
reached EUR 355,824 thousand (in 2024: EUR 258,607
thousand), net profit – EUR 29,252 thousand (in 2024:
EUR 22,672 thousand). The increase in the Group’s net
turnover by 38% compared to 2024 is mainly related to
the revenues received for balancing capacity, as well as
the allocation of received congestion fee to cover the
costs of electricity transmission services.

When evaluating the segment’s financial performance
indicators and economic performance, it should be taken
into account that, under Section 5 of the Energy Law,
electricity transmission is a regulated industry, the PUC
determines the allowed profit by determining the rate
of return on capital, approving the tariffs for electricity
transmission system services.
The segment’s net turnover in the reporting period
was EUR 266,008 thousand, including revenue from
electricity transmission network services of EUR 94,194
thousand. The significant increase in revenue by EUR

111,997 thousand or 73%, compared to 2024, is related
to the increase in congestion revenue allocated to cover
the costs of electricity transmission services, as well as
the increase in revenue from balancing electricity.
In 2025, the Parent company's profit was EUR 19,496
thousand, including profit from electricity transmission
services EUR 6,150 thousand and dividends from the
Subsidiary of EUR 13,346 thousand. The increase
in profit by EUR 4,732 thousand, compared to 2024,
is related to both the increase in dividends received
from the Subsidiary and the congestion fee revenues
allocated to cover costs. By the decision of the PUC
Council of 22 May 2023, new electricity transmission
system service tariffs were approved, setting their
entry into force from 1 July 2023. At the same time, the
aforementioned decision provides permission to use
accumulated congestion management revenues to cover
transmission system service costs until the end of 2025
in the total amount of up to EUR 62.1 million, of which
EUR 45,450 thousand were used to cover costs in 2025
and recognised in revenue (see Note 4 for details).

NATURAL GAS
TRANSMISSION SEGMENT
The revenue of the natural gas transmission segment
in 2025 was EUR 48,246 thousand, and profit before
corporate income tax reached EUR 6,622 thousand,
which is 66% more than in the previous year. The
176


=== pdf p.177 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

increase in profit in 2025, compared to the previous year,
was mainly due to the revaluation of assets carried out
in 2024, as a result of which an impairment of the value
of property, plant and equipment was recognised in the
income statement position "Depreciation, amortisation"
in 2024.
At the same time, natural gas transmission service
revenue in 2025 decreased by 6% compared to 2024,
which reflects a decrease in the volume of incoming and
outgoing transmission capacity reservations.
Conexus’s operations are regulated, and the regulatory
periods differ from the financial reporting year. According
to the methodology for calculating tariffs for the Natural
Gas Transmission System service, deviations in revenue
and costs from the permitted volumes may occur during
the tariff period, which will affect the tariff values in the
following tariff cycles. Such deviations may occur in
the transmission segment, since the actual natural gas
consumption may differ from the planned one in the
tariffs, creating a revenue adjustment.

NATURAL GAS STORAGE
SEGMENT
The revenue of the natural gas storage segment in
the reporting period was EUR 41,570 thousand, and
profit before corporate income tax reached EUR 21,761
thousand, which is 42% less than in the previous year.

The decrease in profit is mainly due to a 31% decrease
in revenue from natural gas storage services compared
to the previous year. The decrease in revenue from
storage services is due to a decrease in storage
capacity reservations, associated with a reduction
in auction premiums and a decrease in demand for
storage.
The natural gas storage segment generates revenue
from storage capacity reservations, which are allocated
to system users in storage capacity auctions within
the storage cycle. The storage cycle lasts from May
1 to April 30 of the following year. According to the
methodology for calculating the tariffs for the natural gas
storage system service, deviations in revenue and costs
from the permitted volumes may occur during the tariff
period, which will affect the permitted revenues for the
storage system service in the next tariff cycle.

INVESTMENTS
ELECTRICITY
TRANSMISSION SEGMENT
Capital investments in the electricity transmission
system are made in accordance with the Electricity
Transmission System Development Plan approved
by the PUC, implementing projects necessary for
the provision of a safe and high-quality electricity
transmission system service. In order to minimise the

impact of the planned investments on the electricity
transmission tariff, the Parent Company has actively
raised EU co-financing, raised EU co-financing in the
amount of more than 400 million EUR over the past
12 years. Similarly, accumulated congestion fee is
directed to financing capital investments, by the end of
2025, congestion fee in the amount of EUR 92 million
have been directed to financing capital investments. The
part of the asset value financed from EU co-financing or
congestion fees, as well as depreciation, is not included
in the calculation of transmission system service tariffs.
The assets of the electricity transmission segment at
the end of the reporting period were EUR 1,131 million.
In 2025, investments in electricity transmission assets
amounted to EUR 182,848 thousand, including:
• Synchronisation with the European electricity
transmission network (phases 1 and 2) EUR 90,838
thousand invested – the project’s goal is to strengthen
Latvia’s energy security by synchronising the Latvian
electricity transmission network with the continental
European network, taking into account the principles
of safety and cost-effectiveness.
• EUR 16,702 thousand invested in the reconstruction
and renovation of substations and power transmission
lines – within the framework of the reconstruction and
renovation projects of the substation, the equipment
installed in it that has served its time is completely
replaced, as well as the protection equipment of
network elements is replaced, installing digital and
high-speed equipment with wide functionality. In
addition, by modernizing communication solutions,
the total amount ensures that the reconstructed

substation can be controlled not only from the
workplace in the substation, but also remotely from
the central dispatch centre, in addition, the dispatcher
directly receives all the necessary information for
decision-making and action both in the normal
operating mode of the system and in various
emergency situations. The aforementioned projects
increase the controllability and observability of the
electricity system, as well as open up opportunities to
develop and integrate renewable energy resources.
• EUR 51,066 thousand has been invested in
ensuring connections for producers of renewable
energy resources. The costs of the aforementioned
connections are covered by the connection applicants
and do not have a negative impact on the electricity
transmission system service tariffs.

NATURAL GAS
TRANSMISSION SEGMENT
The assets of the natural gas transmission segment
at the end of the reporting period were EUR 223
million, which constituted 48% of the total assets of
the Subsidiary. Investments made during the reporting
period amounted to EUR 11,829 thousand. The largest
of them:
• Renovation of transmission gas pipelines –
EUR 6.1 million;
177


=== pdf p.178 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

• Biomethane input point "Džūkste" – EUR 1.5 million.
On 28 July 2025, a biomethane input point was
opened in Džūkste parish, and its total value reached
EUR 1.6 million, of which EUR 1.5 million was
provided by the European Union Recovery
and Resilience Mechanism.

NATURAL GAS STORAGE
SEGMENT
The segment’s assets at the end of the reporting period
totalled EUR 245 million, which constituted 52% of the
total assets of the Subsidiary. Investments made during
the reporting period amounted to EUR 14,611 thousand,
including the most significant:
• reconstruction of wells, installation of a new gas
pumping unit, and reconstruction of gas collection
point No. 3 – EUR 10.5 million, implemented within
the framework of the ambitious European project
of common interest PCI 8.2.4 "Improvement of
the operation of the Inčukalns underground gas
storage facility";

FUTURE DEVELOPMENT
OF THE GROUP
Electricity transmission
In order to ensure efficient development of the
transmission system and reliable electricity transmission
system service, AST annually develops an electricity
transmission system development plan for the next
ten years.
The Development Plan for 2026-2035 approved by
the AST Management Board envisages investing EUR
908 million in the transmission system over the next
10 years, within the framework of which investments
are foreseen in the development of interconnections,
as well as in the protection of critical synchronisation
infrastructure and in the maintenance and development
of the operational capacity of the existing infrastructure,
ensuring efficient and high-quality electricity
transmission system service at the lowest possible
service tariffs.

• renovation of the interconnection between
compressor station I and compressor station II –
EUR 705 thousand;

In addition, in the coming years, AST will also prioritise
the connection of large-scale renewable energy power
plants to the transmission network. The development
of renewable energy power plants makes a significant
contribution to achieving Latvia’s energy and
climate goals.

creation of a SCADA storage control system –
EUR 639 thousand.

In order to ensure the smallest possible impact of the
planned capital investments on electricity transmission

tariffs, AST plans to attract EU co-financing for
projects of common European interest included in
the Development Plan. Detailed information on the
further development of the electricity transmission
system, as well as system management and market
development, innovations, and research is provided in
the Sustainability Report section "Operating segments".

Natural gas transmission and storage
The Subsidiary’s further development is focused on
the implementation of climate-friendly and sustainable
energy solutions. In order to promote biomethane
production in Latvia, Conexus is developing a solution
that will offer biomethane injection into the natural gas
transmission system to those producers whose existing
or planned future biomethane stations are located
relatively far from the existing natural gas transmission
infrastructure. The Natural Gas Transmission System
Development Plan for 2026–2035 envisages continuing
to develop the construction of biomethane injection
points in the territory of Latvia in order to ensure
access to the transmission system in various regions of
Latvia and expand access opportunities for small and
medium-sized biomethane producers. The Subsidiary
is also participating in an international project on the
creation of the Nordic-Baltic Hydrogen Corridor. The
aforementioned project is an important component of
Europe’s transition to a sustainable and decarbonised
energy system. Detailed information on the further
development of the natural gas transmission and storage
segments is provided in the Sustainability Report section
"Operating segments".

FINANCING AND
LIQUIDITY
The Group finances capital investment projects
from its own funds and external long-term borrowed
financing, which is regularly and timely raised in the
financial and capital markets. Timely planned raising
of borrowed financing is essential for ensuring optimal
risk management of loan refinancing, as well as for
repaying loan principal amounts within the binding
term. As of 31 December 2025, the total amount of
borrowings of the Parent Company was EUR 120,435
thousand (31 December 2024: EUR 100,420 thousand),
which consisted of borrowings from green bonds issued
in 2021 in the amount of EUR 100,446 thousand, as
well as a loan for financing capital investment projects
from AS "Luminor Bank" in the amount of EUR 19,989
thousand (see also Note 20). In order to ensure available
resources for financing current assets, the Parent
Company has concluded two overdraft agreements with
AS "SEB banka" for an amount of up to EUR 10,000
thousand each and maturing on 10 January 2027 and
10 December 2027. In addition to the liquidity reserve,
taking into account the green bond repayment date of
20 January 2027, the Parent Company has concluded
an overdraft agreement with AS "SEB banka" for an
amount of up to EUR 90,000 thousand and maturing
on 10 March 2027. During the reporting period, AS
"Augstsprieguma tīkls" did not receive any loans under
overdraft agreements. At the end of the reporting
period, 83% of the Parent Company’s total long-term
loans had a fixed loan interest rate (31 December 2024:
178


=== pdf p.179 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

100%). At the end of the reporting period, the weighted
average interest rate of the Parent Company’s long-term
borrowings was 0.9% (31 December 2024: 0.5%).
The Group’s total borrowings as of 31 December 2025
were EUR 186,895 thousand, including bonds issued by
the Parent Company of EUR 100,446 thousand, loans
from credit institutions of EUR 86,449 thousand. The
natural gas operator AS "Conexus Baltic Grid" attracts
external financing with its own resources. At the end of
the reporting period, 67% of the Group’s total long-term
borrowings had a fixed loan interest rate (31 December
2024: 76%), thus the increase in interest rates in the
financial market does not have a significant impact on
the Group. The Group’s weighted average interest rate
of long-term borrowings at the end of the reporting
period was 1.34% (31 December 2024: 1.50%).
All of the Group’s borrowings are denominated in
euros and unsecured.

FINANCIAL RISK
MANAGEMENT
The financial risk management of the High Voltage
Network Group is implemented in accordance with the
Financial Risk Management Policy and its subordinate
Financial Risk Management Regulations.
The Group’s Subsidiary develops and approves its
own Financial Risk Management Policies, which are
consistent with the basic principles included in the
Group’s policy.
Financial resource management is aimed at ensuring the
financing of economic activities and financial stability by
implementing conservative financial risk management.

Within the framework of financial risk management,
the Group and the Parent Company use financial risk
controls and take risk mitigation measures, reducing the
risk in open positions.

LIQUIDITY RISK
Liquidity risk is related to the ability of the Group and
the Parent Company to meet its obligations within the
specified deadlines. In order to mitigate short-term
liquidity risk and to mitigate unpredictable cash flow
fluctuations caused by operational risk, the Group and
the Parent Company ensure a reserve in the form of
cash or signed and irrevocably available credit facilities
for the next 24 months.
The Group and the Parent Company observe prudent
liquidity risk management, ensuring that it has access
to adequate financial resources to settle its obligations
within the specified deadlines. By providing the
necessary financial resources to cover its obligations,
the Parent Company had an overdraft of EUR 110,000
thousand available as of 31 December 2025. In turn, the
Subsidiary had available long-term loan agreements of
EUR 36,000 thousand at the end of the reporting period.
The management believes that it will not have any
liquidity problems and the Augstsprieguma tīkla Group
will be able to settle its creditors within the specified
deadlines.

179


=== pdf p.180 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

The management believes that the Augstsprieguma
tīkla Group will have sufficient cash resources so that its
liquidity is not threatened.

INTEREST RATE RISK
Interest rate risk arises mainly from borrowings with a
variable interest rate, creating the risk that financial costs
will increase significantly due to an increase in interest
rates. To mitigate this risk, the Group and the Parent
Company’s Financial Risk Management Regulations
stipulate that the proportion of fixed rates or rates with
limited increases in the borrowing portfolio may not be
less than 35%. At the end of the reporting period, 83%
of the Parent Company’s and 67% of the Group’s longterm borrowings had a fixed interest rate.

CREDIT RISK
Financial assets that potentially expose the Group
and the Parent Company to a certain degree of risk
concentration are mainly cash and receivables from
contracts with customers. Credit risk may be related to
financial counterparty risk and counterparty risk.
In carrying out economic activities, the Group and
the Parent Company cooperate with local and

foreign financial institutions. Consequently, financial
counterparty risk arises - in the event of insolvency or
suspension of operations of cooperation partners, the
Group and the Parent Company may suffer losses.
In the case of external financing, the risk exists until
the loan is drawn down and transferred to one of the
Group’s or the Parent Company’s partner banks.
Credit risk arising from the Group’s and the Parent
Company’s cash funds in current accounts is managed
in accordance with the Group’s Financial Risk
Management Policy and Financial Risk Management
Regulations, balancing the allocation of financial assets.
In accordance with the Financial Risk Management
Policy, in cooperation with banks and financial
institutions, business partners with a minimum credit
rating of at least investment grade, set by the parent
bank or an international credit rating agency,
are accepted.
When carrying out economic activities, the Group and
the Parent Company cooperate with local and foreign
merchants. Consequently, there is a risk of business
partners or debtors - in the event of insolvency or
suspension of operations of cooperation partners,
the Group or the Parent Company may suffer losses.
The Law on International and National Sanctions of
the Republic of Latvia establishes financial and civil
law restrictions for entities included in the sanctions
list, including the freezing of financial assets.
Taking into account the above, in addition to legal
and reputational risks, cooperation with an entity
included in the sanctions list creates a risk of nonfulfillment of contractual obligations for the Group

or the Parent Company.
The Parent Company has developed internal procedures
for managing the aforementioned risks, including
ensuring monitoring of cooperation partners, as well
as providing in procurement contracts the right to
unilaterally withdraw from the contract performance if
the contract cannot be performed due to the fact that
international or national sanctions have been applied
to the counterparty or sanctions imposed by a member
state of the European Union or the North Atlantic
Treaty Organisation affecting significant financial
market interests.
Although the Group and the Parent Company have a
significant concentration of debtor risk in relation to
one counterparty or a group of similar counterparties,
this risk is assessed as limited, taking into account
that the most significant counterparty is a stateowned commercial company - joint-stock company
"Latvenergo", as well as its group companies, with a
high credit rating of Baa2 (investment grade rating) and
a stable future outlook, which Moody’s has assigned to
the AS "Latvenergo" group. The credit risk associated
with receivables is managed in accordance with the risk
management measures set out in the Financial Risk
Management Regulations, monthly, but not less than
quarterly, by performing an analysis of receivables.

CAPITAL RISK
MANAGEMENT
The shareholder of the Parent Company is the
Republic of Latvia (100%). The objective of capital risk
management is to ensure the sustainable operation and
development of the Group and the Parent Company,
the financing necessary for the implementation of the
development plan in transmission assets, and the
fulfilment of the restrictive conditions set out in the loan
agreements. The restrictive conditions set out in the loan
agreements have not been violated. In order to ensure
the fulfilment of the restrictive conditions set out in the
loan agreements, a regular analysis of the equity ratio is
performed.

ANNUAL REPORT

5

NOTES TO THE
CONSOLIDATED
AND SEPARATE
FINANCIAL
STATEMENTS

1. GENERAL
INFORMATION ABOUT
THE GROUP
The main activity of the AS "Augstsprieguma
tīkls" Group is ensuring the functions of electricity
transmission system operator, efficient management
of energy supply system assets, transmission and
storage of natural gas.

The Group includes the Parent Company
AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls", the Subsidiary AS "Conexus
Baltic Grid", and the associated company
"Baltic RCC" OÜ.
All shares of AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls" are owned by
the State, and their holder is the Ministry of Climate and
Energy of the Republic of Latvia. The registered office
of the parent company is Dārzciema street 86, Riga,
LV-1073, Latvia.

THE GROUP’S MANAGEMENT BOARD AND COUNCIL
Management Board members
and their positions

Rolands Irklis – Chairman of the Management Board
Imants Zviedris – a member of the Management Board
Gatis Junghāns – a member of the Management Board
Arnis Daugulis – a member of the Management Board
Ilze Znotiņa – a member of the Management Board (until 31.12.2025)

Council members
and their positions

Olga Bogdanova – Chairwoman of the Council
Līga Rozentāle – deputy Chairwoman of the Council (until 31.12.2025)
Juris Zīvarts – a member of the Council (from 01.01.2026)

194


=== pdf p.195 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls" is a transmission system
operator, which, operates in accordance with the license
No. E13001 issued by the Public Utilities Commission,
ensures the reliability of the transmission network
operations and the electricity supply of the Latvian
electricity system, provides transmission services based
on published transmission service tariffs, and constantly
ensures the availability of transmission system services.
AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls" performs operational
management of the transmission system and ensures
safe, stable electricity transmission. The Group’s
subsidiary AS "Conexus Baltic Grid" is an independent
unified natural gas transmission and storage system
operator in Latvia, which manages one of the most
modern natural gas storage facilities in Europe - the
Inčukalns underground gas storage facility - and the
main natural gas transmission system that connects
the Latvian natural gas market with the transmission
systems of other countries. Conexus takes care of the
sustainability and safety of the infrastructure, high-quality
of services, which promotes market development and
provides economic value for customers and the entire
society. Conexus natural gas transmission and storage
services are regulated by the Public Utilities Regulatory
Commission. The Group’s associated company
"Baltic RCC" OÜ is the Baltic Regional Coordination
Centre in Tallinn, whose main task is to coordinate the
development planning of electricity systems, as well as
to coordinate the daily activities of individual operators to
guarantee the security of electricity supply.

The financial statements were approved on 24 April
2026 by the Parent Company’s Management Board
consisting of the following members: Rolands Irklis
(Chairman of the Management Board), Imants Zviedris
(a member of the Management Board), Gatis Junghāns
(a member of the Management Board), Arnis Daugulis
(a member of the Management Board). The decision on
the approval of the financial statements will be made by
the shareholders’ meeting.
The auditor of the Parent Company and the Group is the
auditing company SIA "KPMG Baltics", and the auditor
in charge is Armine Movsisjana.

2. 2. MATERIAL
ACCOUNTING POLICIES
This section sets out material accounting policies
applied in the preparation of the financial statements.
These principles have been applied consistently
throughout the period presented. The accounting and
measurement principles set out in this section have been
applied consistently throughout the period presented,
unless otherwise stated.
The consolidated and separate financial statements
have been prepared in accordance with IFRS
accounting standards as adopted by the European
Union (hereinafter referred to as "IFRS Accountant
Standards").

The consolidated and separate financial statements
have been prepared under the historical cost bases,
with exceptiont for certain groups of property, plant and
equipment that are carried at revalued amounts (see
Notes 2.3 and 10.2). The income statement is classified
by type of expense. Cash flow from operating activities
has been prepared using the indirect method. Cash
flows from investing activities and cash flows from
financing activities have been prepared using the direct
method.
The non-IFRS indicator EBITDA is presented in the
income statement. Such a presentation is typical of
the industry practice and provides better comparability
with other companies operating in this sector. EBITDA
within the framework of these financial statements is
calculated as profit before depreciation and amortisation,
dividends received, financial income, financial expenses
and corporate income tax. EBITDA maybe calculated
differently in the financial statements of
other companies.

Financial indicators in the financial statements are
presented in the official currency of the Republic of
Latvia, the euro (hereinafter "EUR").
The financial statements observe the comparability of
indicators. In cases where the presentation of financial
statement information is changed during the reporting
year, comparative indicators are reclassified to ensure
their comparability.
The financial statements cover the period from
1 January to 31 December 2025.
The consolidated financial statements of the Group
include the financial results of the subsidiary
AS "Conexus Baltic Grid" from the date of acquisition
of the Subsidiary.
As of 31 December 2025 and 31 December 2024,
the Parent Company of the Group had investments
in the following subsidiaries:

ANNUAL REPORT

IFRS 18, Presentation and Disclosure in Financial
Statements, endorsed by the EU in February 2026,
replaces International Financial Reporting Standard
(IFRS) 1 from 1 January 2027 and will result in
significant changes in IFRS accounting standards,
including IAS 8, Basis of Preparation of Financial
Statements (renamed from Accounting Policies,
Changes in Accounting Estimates and Errors), as well
as specific amendments to IAS 7, Cash Flow Statement.
Although IFRS 18 will not affect the recognition and
measurement of items in the parent company’s separate
or group consolidated financial statements, it is expected
to have a significant impact on the presentation of
certain items and disclosures in the notes. These
changes primarily affect classification and subtotals in
the income statement, the aggregation, disaggregation
and naming of items, and the disclosure of performance
measures defined by management in the notes to the
financial statements. The parent company and the group
are working on an IFRS 18 implementation plan.
The Parent Company and the Group expect that,
except IFRS 18, the adoption of these standards
and amendments to existing standards will not have
a material impact on the financial statements in the
period of initial application. Although early adoption
of certain standards is permitted, the Parent Company
and the Group have not early adopted the new or
amended standards in preparing these consolidated
financial statements.

CONSOLIDATION
A) Subsidiariess
A Subsidiary is an entity controlled by a Parent
Company. The Parent Company controls another entity
if it receives, or has rights to, variable returns from its
involvement with the entity and has the ability to affect
those returns through its power to govern the activities
of the subsidiary (IFRS 10 Consolidated Financial
Statements).
The financial statements of a Subsidiary are
consolidated from the date on which the Parent
Company obtains control of the Subsidiary and are
discontinued when that control ceases.
All intercompany transactions , balances and unrealised
profits on intercompany transactions are eliminated.
Unrealised losses are also eliminated but are considered
an indicator of impairment of the transferred asset.
Where necessary, the accounting and valuation
methods of the Group’s subsidiaries have been
changed to ensure consistency with the accounting
and measurement methods used within the Group.
Investments in equity of Subsidiaries are stated in the
Parent Company’s separate financial statements at
historical cost, less any impairment losses. Impairment
losses are recognised as the amount by which the
carrying amount of an investment in a Subsidiary
exceeds its recoverable amount. The recoverable
amount is the higher of the asset’s fair value less costs
to sell or value in use. Previous impairment losses of
Subsidiaries (if any) are reviewed to determine whether
there is any possibility of reversal at each reporting date.

B) Transactions with non-controlling
interests and shareholders
The Group’s transactions with non-controlling interests
are treated as transactions with the shareholders of
the entity. Changes in the Parent Company’s interest
in a Subsidiary that are not reflected in the Parent
Company’s share of the Subsidiary’s share when it
loses control of the Subsidiary are equity transactions
(i.e., transactions that are treated as transactions with
shareholders). Gains or losses arising from transactions
with non-controlling interests are recognised in the
Group’s equity.

C) Associates
Investments in associates are investments in an entity
in which the Group has significant influence (directly or
indirectly), which usually means ownership rights that
guarantee 20 to 50 per cent of the voting rights.
Investments in associates are accounted for in the
Group’s financial statements using the equity method.
The Group ceases to use the equity method from the
date when the investment ceases to be an associate.
The Group/Parent Company loses significant influence
when it loses the right to participate in the financial or
operating affairs of the entity. The loss of significant
influence may occur simultaneously with or without a
change in the absolute or relative shareholding.
Investments in associates are carried in the Parent
Company’s separate financial statements at cost, less
impairment losses, if any.

2.1. FINANCIAL
INSTRUMENTS
Financial instruments consist of financial assets at
amortised cost (trade receivables and deposits), and
financial liabilities at amortised cost (trade payables
and loans).
Financial instruments are initially recognised at their
fair value, plus transaction costs for all financial
instruments not designated at fair value through profit or
loss. Purchases or sales of financial assets that require
delivery of assets within a specified period of time,
determined by specific market conditions or agreements
(regular trading transactions), are recognised at the time
of purchase/sale, i.e., on the date when the Group
and the Parent Company are committed to purchase
or sell the asset.

FINANCIAL ASSETS
The Group and the Parent Company classify their
financial assets in accordance with IFRS 9 into the
following measurement categories:
• Those that are subsequently measured at fair value
(through other comprehensive income or profit or
loss), and
• Those that are to be measured at amortised cost.
197


=== pdf p.198 ===
ANNUAL REPORT
Classification of financial assets
The classification of debt instruments depends on the
business model applied by the Group and the Parent
Company to manage financial assets, as well as on
whether the contractual cash flows meet the SPPI
(solely payments of principal and interest) criterion.
If a debt instrument is held to collect cash flows, it may
be carried at amortised cost, if it meets the requirements
of SPPI. Financial assets whose cash flows do not
meet the requirements of SPPI should be measured at
fair value through profit or loss (FVTPL), for example,
derivative financial instruments. Embedded derivatives
are not separated from financial assets, but are
assessed for SPPI requirements when they are included
un financial assets.
Equity instruments are always measured at fair value.
However, management has the irrevocable election to
present changes in fair value in other comprehensive
income if the instrument is not held for trading. If
an equity instrument is held for trading, changes in
fair value should be recognised in the profit or loss
statement.

Debt instruments
The subsequent measurement of debt instruments
depends on the asset management and cash flow
characteristics of the Group’s and the Parent Company’s
business model. The Group and the Parent Company
classify all of their debt instruments (including deposits
with credit institutions) at amortised cost: assets held
to collect contractual cash flows, if those cash flows
are solely payments of principal and interest, are

measured at amortised cost. Any gain or loss arising
from derecognition is recognised directly in profit or loss.
Impairment is presented as a separate line item in the
profit or loss statement.

Cash
Cash and cash equivalents consist of cash balances in
bank current accounts and demand deposits with initial
maturity of up to three months.

Bank deposits
Mank deposits are financial assets with an original
maturity of more than three months.

Allowances for credit losses
of financial assets
Allowances for credit losses of financial assets is
recognised in accordance with the expected credit loss
(ECL) model. The model has a three-step approach
based on changes in the credit quality of the financial
asset compared to initial recognition. The model includes
operational simplifications for debts from contracts
with customers. At the time of initial recognition of the
financial asset, the Group and the Parent company
recognise immediate loss equal to the 12-month ECL,
even if the financial asset has no impairment indicators
(debts from contracts with customers are recognised by
their lifetime ECL). In the event of a significant increase
in credit risk, ECL is measured using the lifetime ECL of
the asset rather than a 12-month ECL.
For individually rated financial assets, a material

increase in credit risk is defined as a downgrade below
investment grade or a change in the composition of the
participants that may adversely affect the external credit
rating. For financial assets rated at the portfolio level,
material increases in credit risk is defined as default and
insolvency of the debtor.

ECL is recognition, the amount of loss decreases and
this decrease may be objectively related to an event
after recognition of impairment (for example, the credit
rating of the debtor improves), reversal of previously
recognised impairment loss is recognised in the profit
or loss statement.

The Group and Parent Company have applied
operational simplification granted by IFRS 9 in respect
of the valuation of debts from contracts with customers
— receivables shall be grouped according to their credit
quality and the days past due, applying the expected
credit loss interest to each Group Grouped. ECL rates
have been estimated considering the last three years of
payment history and adjusted to consider information on
the present and future projections.

Derecognition of financial assets
A financial asset (or, where applicable, a part of a
financial asset or a group of similar financial assets)
is derecognised when:
• The contractual rights to the cash flows from the
financial asset expire,
• The Group and the Parent Company transfer the
contractual rights to the cash flows from the financial
asset or undertake to transfer the cash received
in full to third parties without significant delay in
accordance with a pass-through arrangement; or if (a)
the Group and the Parent Company have transferred
substantially all the risks and rewards of the financial
asset and if (b) the Group and the Parent Company
have neither transferred nor retained substantially all
the risks and rewards of the financial asset, but have
transferred control of the financial asset.

Related party debts, as well as loans issued to related
parties, are allocated to a separate group, which ECL is
calculated taking into account not only past experience,
but also the credit rating and future development
forecasts of their ultimate owner – the Republic of Latvia.
Although cash and deposits with an original maturity
less than three months are also subject to the
requirements for calculating ECL under IFRS 9. For the
purposes of IFRS, the estimated value of expected credit
losses is immaterial given that, in general, all cash and
deposits with an initial maturity of less than three months
are held in financial institutions that themselves or their
parent banks hold an investment grade credit rating
(predominantly A level credit rating).

FINANCIAL LIABILITIES

Expected credit losses are included in a separate credit
loss account, and losses are recognised in the profit
or loss statement. If, in the subsequent period after

Except for derivative financial instruments, financial
liabilities are measured at amortised cost or fair value
through profit or loss. All financial liabilities of the Group
198


=== pdf p.199 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

and the Parent Company are financial liabilities at
amortised cost.

Financial liabilities at amortised cost
Financial liabilities at amortised cost are initially
recognised at fair value. In subsequent periods,
financial liabilities at amortised cost are recognised
at amortised cost using the effective interest rate.
Financial liabilities at amortised cost are classified
as current liabilities if their maturity is one year or less.
If their maturity exceeds one year, they are presented
as non-current liabilities.

Derecognition of financial liabilities
The Group and the Parent Company derecognise
financial liabilities when their contractual obligations
are fulfilled, cancelled or expired. The Group and the
Parent Company also derecognise financial liabilities
when the terms are modified and the cash flows of
the modified liability are materially different. In such
cases, a new financial liability based on new contract
terms is recognised at fair value. If the modification
is not material, the difference in the amortised cost
of the financial liability is recognised in profit or loss.
Transaction costs related to the modification are
included in the calculation of the effective interest rate
of the modified financial liability. On derecognition of a
financial liability, the difference between the carrying
amount extinguished and the consideration paid
(including any non-cash assets transferred or liabilities
assumed) is recognised in profit or loss.

2.2. TRANSACTIONS IN
FOREIGN CURRENCIES
FUNCTIONAL AND PRESENTATION
CURRENCY
The Group’s financial statements are measured in the
currency of the economic environment in which the
Group operates (the functional currency). The financial
statements are prepared, and all items are presented
in euro (EUR), which is the functional and presentation
currency of the Group and the Parent Company.
All transactions in foreign currencies are translated into
the functional currency at the exchange rates prevailing
at the dates of the transactions. Monetary assets
and liabilities denominated in foreign currencies are
translated into the functional currency at the exchange
rates prevailing at the last day of the reporting period.
The resulting gains or losses are included in the profit
or loss statement for the period. Non-monetary items
measured at historical cost in a foreign currency are
translated at the exchange rates ruling at the dates of
the original transactions.

2.3. INTANGIBLE ASSETS
AND PROPERTY, PLANT
AND EQUIPMENT
Intangible assets are initially measured at cost. After
initial recognition, intangible assets are measured at
cost less accumulated amortisation and accumulated
impairment losses. Computer software licenses,
computer software and costs related to their
implementation are included in intangible assets, and
their amortisation is calculated using the straight-line
method over the useful life of these assets. The useful
life of intangible assets is 5 to 20 years.
The property, plant and equipment (PPE) of the Parent
Company and the Group are initially recognised at
cost. After initial recognition, PPE are measured at
acquisition cost or the revaluation model applied to
certain PPE classes, less accumulated depreciation
and impairment losses. The acquisition cost includes
expenses that are directly related to the acquisition of
PPE. Land is not depreciated. Depreciation of PPE is
calculated using the straight-line method to allocate the
value of PPE to their estimated useful life (taking into
account the residual value of the PPE).
If the components (components) of the PPE item have
different useful lives and the acquisition values of these
components (components) are significant in relation to
the value of the PPE, then they are accounted for by the
components (components) of the PPE.

Subsequent costs are included in the carrying amount

specific property, plant and equipment is transferred to
retained earnings.

is higher than its recoverable amount, the relevant
intangible asset or PPE is written down to its recoverable
amount. The recoverable amount is the higher of the

fair value less costs of sell and the value in use of the
intangible asset or PPE (see also Note 10).

PROPERTY, PLANT AND EQUIPMENT

Revaluations are carried out with sufficient regularity to
ensure that the carrying amount of these assets is not
materially different from that which would be determined
using fair value at the end of the reporting period.
The following classes of PPE are revalued regularly, but
not less frequently than once every five (5) years:
For the Parent Company:
• Electricity transmission structures,
• Transmission lines and technological equipment,
• Other electricity transmission equipment.
For the Group:
• Natural gas transmission buildings and structures,
• Wells,
• Transmission lines and technological equipment,
• Natural gas pumping and automatic control
equipment,
• Electricity transmission structures,
• Other electricity transmission equipment.
The increase in value resulting from the revaluation is
recognised in the Statement of comprehensive income
as a change under "Long-term asset revaluation

=== pdf p.201 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

reserve". If an impairment loss has been incurred as
a result of revaluation, it is first written off against the
increase in value recognised in comprehensive income
in previous years; if the impairment loss exceeds the
revaluation reserve, the excess is recognised in the
Statement of profit or loss for the year.
At the date of revaluation, the initial carrying amount and
accumulated depreciation are increased or decreased
in proportion to the change in the carrying amount
of the asset so that the carrying amount of the asset

The revaluation reserve may not be distributed as

losses, transferred to other reserves, or used for other
purposes. See also Notes 2.12 and 10.

2.4. LEASES
At inception, the Group and the Parent Company assess
whether a contract is or contains a lease. A contract is
or contains a lease if the contract conveys the right to
control the use of an identifiable asset for a fixed period
in exchange for consideration.

THE GROUP AND THE PARENT
COMPANY AS A LESSEE
At inception, the Group and the Parent Company assess
whether a contract is, or contains, a lease. The Group
and the Parent Company recognise a right-of-use asset
and a corresponding lease liability for all leases in which
they are the lessee, except for short-term leases (with
a lease term of 12 months or less) and leases of lowvalue assets (such as tablets, personal computers, office
equipment, small items of equipment and telephones).
At initial recognition, the Group and the Parent Company
measure the lease liability at the present value of the
lease payments that are not paid at that date. The
lease payments are discounted using the interest
rate implicit in the lease. If that rate cannot be readily
determined, the Group and the Parent Company use
their comparablel borrowing rate.
Lease payments included in the lease liability include:
• fixed lease payments (including lease payments
that are in substance fixed), less lease incentive
payments receivable,
• variable lease payments that depend on an index
or rate and were initially measured using the index
or rate at the date of initial recognition,
• amounts that the lessee would have to pay as
NBV guarantees,
• the exercise price of a call option, if there are
reasonable grounds to believe that the lessee will

exercise that option,
• lease termination penalties if the lease term reflects
the lessee exercising an option to terminate the lease.
Lease liabilities are presented as a separate line item in
the statement of financial position.
Lease liabilities are subsequently measured at amortised
cost using the effective interest rate method, reducing
carrying amount to reflect lease payments made.
The Group and the Parent Company reassess lease
liability (and adjust the related right-of-use asset
accordingly) whenever:
• the lease term has changed or there is a significant
event or change in circumstances that results in
a change in the valuation of the purchase option,
in which case the lease liability is revalued by
discounting the revised lease payments using the
revised discount rate;
• the lease payments change due to a change in an
index or rate or the expected payments change due to
a guaranteed residual value; in such cases the lease
liability is remeasured by discounting the revised
lease payments using the discount rate originally
applied (unless the change in the lease payments
arises from a change in a variable interest rate, in
which case the revised discount rate is used), the
lease is modified and the lease modification is not
accounted for as a separate lease, in which case
the lease liability is remeasured based on the term
of the modified lease, discounting the revised lease

payments using the revised discount rate at the
effective date of the modification.
The right-of-use asset comprises the initial
measurement of the corresponding lease liability, lease
payments made on or before the commencement
date, net of lease discounts received and any initial
direct costs. It is subsequently measured at cost less
accumulated depreciation and impairment losses.
The right-of-use asset is amortised over the term of the
lease. The right-of-use asset is periodically reduced by
impairment losses, if any, and adjusted to reflect the
measurement of the lease liability. No impairment was
identified at the end of the reporting period for the Rightof-use assets.

THE GROUP AND THE PARENT COMPANY
AS LESSOR
The Group and the Parent Company have only operating
leases as lessors. Lease income is recognised based on
the amount of the lease payment, which is determined
in accordance with the fixed fee for each lease and is
recognised on a straight-line basis over the lease term.
