=== pdf p.202 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

2.5. INVENTORIES
Inventories are stated at the lower of cost or net
realisable value. Net realisable value is the estimated
selling price in the ordinary course of business, less
applicable variable selling expenses. Cost is calculated
using the weighted average method, except for natural
gas, which is accounted for using the FIFO method.
Inventories are recognised as an expense in the
statement of profit or loss in the reporting period when
they are consumed.
The cost of purchasing inventories includes the
purchase price, import duties and other taxes and
duties, transportation and related costs, as well as other
costs directly attributable to the delivery of materials and
goods. When determining the value of inventories, trade
discounts, rebates and similar discounts are deducted.

2.6. DEFERRED INCOME

GRANTS RELATED TO
EXPENDITURE ITEMS

Deferred income consists of grants, congestion fees and
transmission connection charges.

If a grant relates to an expense item and is subject to
a number of conditions, it is initially recognised at fair
value as deferred income. The conditions attached
to these grants are that the grant must be used only
to cover the related expenses, only for the intended
purpose, the amount used must be identifiable and
must be separately accounted for (by providing
separate accounting records). Grants are systematically
recognised in income in the periods in which the related
costs that are intended to be compensated are included
in expenses.

GRANTS
Grants are recognised when there is reasonable
assurance that the grant will be received and all related
conditions will be met. A description of the conditions
is provided in Note 19. Grants are recognised as income
in the period necessary to systematically match them
with the related costs for which they are intended to
compensate. Advance payments of grants are presented
in future periods’ income from the moment the advance
is received in the current account until the moment
the costs related to the use of the advances received
are approved.

The volume of inventories is verified at the end of the
year by taking an inventory.

ASSET-RELATED GRANTS
Grants related to the acquisition of PPE are recognised
as deferred income and credited to the profit or loss
using the straight-line method over the expected useful
life of the related assets.

A grant that is received as compensation for expenses
or losses that have already been incurred or with the
aim of providing immediate financial support to the
enterprise without further costs is recognised in profit or
loss in the period in which it becomes receivable.

CROSS-BORDER CONGESTION
MANAGEMENT (AUCTIONS) AND
ELIMINATION OF TRANSMISSION
CAPACITY OVERLOADS
Under Section 13, Paragraph 4 and Section 131,
Part 6 of the Electricity Market Law AS "Augstsprieguma
tīkls" implements congestion and overload management
of the transmission system, as well as receives a fee
for the auction of cross-sectional limited capacity in
accordance with the mutual compensation mechanism of
transmission system operators and concluded contracts.

In situations where market participants request more
cross-border electricity capacity than is technically
possible, cross-border electricity capacity rights are sold
to participants in special auctions. In accordance with
the principle used in these auctions and specified in
legislative acts, 50% of the auction income is allocated
to the transmission system operator of each participating
country. Types of auctions:
1. The revenues of the intraday market auction are
generated from the difference between the Latvian
and the neighbouring Nord Pool electricity exchange
price areas’ intraday exchange prices. The electricity
exchange receives the price difference and passes it
on to the TSOs Grouped.
2. Long-term transmission capacity auction, which
aims to reduce inter-regional price risk arising from
transmission capacity shortages. Latvian and Estonian
system operators AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls" and AS
"Elering" offer transmission rights auctions (FTR) on an
annual, quarterly and monthly basis. Market participants
who have purchased FTR capacity are entitled to the
same amount of day-ahead market auction revenue.
The auctions are organised, and the revenues are
distributed by the Single Allocation Platform (SAP)
of European transmission system operators (TSOs),
operated by the Joint Allocation Office (JAO), which
acts as an agent. The JAO concludes contracts between
the auction parties and collects the revenues, which
are paid by energy traders and distributed between
system operators based on flow volumes. Revenue is
recognised when the transmission service is actually
provided.

202


=== pdf p.203 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

Pursuant to Article 16 of Regulation (EC) No 714/2009
of the European Parliament and of the Council on
conditions for access to the network for cross-border
exchanges in electricity and repealing Regulation (EC)
No 1228/2003 (the "Regulation"), all revenues resulting
from the allocation of interconnection capacity shall be
used for one or more of the following purposes:
a) ensuring the effective availability of the allocated
capacity and/or
b) maintaining or improving interconnection capacity
through investments in the network, in particular in new
interconnections
c) where revenues cannot be effectively used for the
abovementioned purposes, they may, subject to the
approval of the regulatory authorities of the Member
States concerned, be used as revenues to be taken
into account by the national regulatory authorities when
approving the methodology for calculating network tariffs
and/or setting network tariffs.
Taking into account industry practice, as well as
the fact that the revenue from auctions is received
from third parties (auction organisers), who are not
customers within the meaning of IFRS 15, and without
a direct counter-performance obligation, the Group
and the Parent Company account for this revenue by
applying IAS 20 Accounting for Government Grants and
Disclosure of Government Assistance by analogy.
In compliance with the provisions of the Regulation,
revenue from congestion management that is not
used to prevent congestion and congestion in the

transmission network during the reporting year is
reflected in the balance sheet as deferred income.
After these amounts are used to finance a specific
long-term investment project determined by the Public
Utilities Regulatory Commission, the deferred income is
amortised, gradually recognising it in the current year’s
profit or loss (item "Other revenue") in accordance with
the depreciation/amortisation period of the long-term
investment created.
In accordance with the matching principle, revenues
from congestion management used to prevent
congestion in the transmission network are recognised
in profit or loss on a gross basis in the same period
as the related costs of congestion management and
prevention.
In accordance with the methodology for calculating
tariffs for electricity transmission system services
and the permission granted by the Public Utilities
Commission, in 2025, congestion fee revenues in the
amount of EUR 45,450 thousand (in 2024: EUR 4,492
thousand) are allocated to cover the costs of electricity
transmission system services. The related congestion
fee revenues are recognised in profit or loss under
"Revenue" (see Note 4).

2.7. EMPLOYEE BENEFITS
A) DEFINED CONTRIBUTION PLANS
The Group and the Parent Company make monthly
contributions to a closed defined contribution pension
plan on behalf of employees. The plan is managed by
the joint-stock company AS "Pirmais Slēgtais Pensiju
Fonds", in which the Parent Company has a holding.
The Group and the Parent Company, when making
contributions to the defined contribution pension plan,
do not have any additional legal or constructive
obligations to make additional payments if the plan
does not have sufficient funds to pay out all employee
benefits for the services provided by the employee
in the current or previous periods. Contributions are
made in the amount of 6% (in 2024: 6%) of the salary
of each pension plan participant. Pension contributions
are recognised as expenses on an accrual basis and
included in Personnel costs.

B) DEFINED BENEFIT PLANS
In addition to the above pension plan, the Group and
the Parent Company provide certain post-employment
benefits to eligible employees in accordance with
the Collective Bargaining Agreement, payable upon
termination of employment and for the remainder of their
lives. The benefit obligation is calculated annually, taking
into account the current salary level and the number
of employees who are due to receive payments, the
number of historical terminations of employment, as

well as actuarial assumptions. Independent actuaries
recalculate the defined benefit obligation annually.
The present value of the benefit obligation is determined
by discounting estimated future cash flows using
government bond market rates. Net interest costs are
calculated by applying a discount rate to the net balance
of the defined benefit obligation. These costs are
recognised in employee benefit expenses in the profit
or loss.
Actuarial gains and losses arising from experience
adjustments and changes in actuarial assumptions are
recognised in other comprehensive income in the period
in which they arise. They are recognised in equity under
a separate reserve, "Post-employment benefit plan
remeasurement reserve", and are not reclassified to
profit or loss.
Subsequently, these amounts are included in retained
earnings through the statement of changes in equity
and presented in the statement of financial position.
Changes in the present value of the defined benefit
obligation resulting from plan amendments or
curtailments are recognised immediately in profit or
loss as past service cost.

C) ACCRUALS OF UNUSED
ANNUAL LEAVE
The accruals of unused annual leave are calculated
for each employee by multiplying the number of
unused leave days at the end of the reporting year by
the average daily wage in the last six months of the
203


=== pdf p.204 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

reporting year and adding the employer’s share of the
mandatory state social insurance contributions.

2.8. CORPORATE
INCOME TAX

DEFERRED CORPORATE INCOME TAX
Deferred corporate income tax liabilities are recognised
in the consolidated financial statements for the Parent
Company’s subsidiaries’ undistributed profits, which will
be taxed when distributed in the foreseeable future. No
other deferred tax assets or liabilities arise, as there is
no difference between the carrying amounts of assets
and liabilities and their tax bases.

CORPORATE INCOME TAX
The corporate income tax included in the income
statement is the amount of tax that is paid on distributed
profit in accordance with the Corporate Income Tax
Law of the Republic of Latvia. Within the Group, the
Parent Company applies the dividend pass-through
exemption, which allows for the non-application of tax to
distributed profit received from the Subsidiary for which
the Subsidiary has paid Corporate Income Tax by paying
dividends.
Distributed profit is subject to a tax rate of 20% of its
gross amount or 20/80 of net expenses.
Corporate income tax on dividends is recognised in the
profit or loss statement as an expense in the reporting
period when the relevant dividends are declared.
Corporate income tax is not applied to undistributed
profit.

2.9. INCOME
RECOGNITION
Revenue from contracts with customers within the
scope of IFRS 15 represents the consideration received
for goods sold and services rendered in the ordinary
course of business. The Group and the Parent Company
account for a contract with a customer only when all the
criteria set out in IFRS 15 are met. In assessing whether
it is probable that the Group and the Parent Company
will collect the consideration, only the customer’s ability
and intention to pay that consideration when it is due
are considered.
A performance obligation is a promise in a contract with
a customer to transfer to the customer either a distinct
good or service, or a series of distinct goods or services
that are substantially the same and have the same
pattern of transfer to the customer.

The promised goods or services are separate
performance obligations if the good or service is distinct.
A promised good or service is considered distinct if the
customer can obtain the benefit of the good or service
separately or with other readily available resources
(i.e., individually distinct) and the good or service is
separately separable from other contractual promises
(i.e., distinct within the contract). Both of the above
criteria must be met for a good or service to be distinct.
All revenue is recognised over time as the continuous
delivery of those goods and services occurs over the
term of the relevant contracts. For such contracts,
revenue from completed performance obligations
is recognised over time if one of the following criteria
is met:
• The customer receives and consumes benefits at the
same time
• The customer controls the asset at the time the asset
is created or improved
• The activities of the Group and the Parent Company
do not constitute an asset with alternative uses but
are entitled to payment for a timely completed activity.
Revenue from the realisation of performance obligations
is recognised based on the determined transaction price
(net of value-added tax and discounts). The transaction
price represents the amount to which the Group and the
Parent Company are entitled under the current contract.
It is allocated to the different performance obligations
based on the stand-alone prices of the goods or
services promised in the contract. The Group and the
Parent Company allocate the transaction price to the

different performance obligations in proportion to their
observable stand-alone prices and recognise revenue
when the performance obligations are satisfied.
Payment terms, in accordance with the terms of the
contracts for goods or services transferred to customers,
are 20 to 45 days after the services are rendered or the
goods are sold.
A) Electricity transmission system service (IFRS 15)
Revenue from transmission system services is based on
tariffs approved by the Public Utilities Commission and
is recognised in accordance with IFRS 15. The Parent
Company is entitled to reimbursement from the customer
for an amount that directly corresponds to the value that
the customer obtains from the Parent Company’s timely
completed activities.
The Parent Company’s revenue from transmission
system services provided is recognised at the end
of each month based on automatically read meter
readings.
B) Revenue from electricity sales (balancing
electricity, balancing capacity and regulating
electricity) (IFRS 15)
Under Section 11, Paragraph Two of the Electricity
Market Law, a transmission system operator may
participate in electricity trading if the purchase and
sale of electricity or capacity is necessary for system
balancing, purchase of ancillary services, covering
electricity transmission losses, the transmission system
operator’s own consumption or if there is a deviation
204


=== pdf p.205 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

from the normal operating mode or an accident in the
system.
When participating in electricity trading, the transmission
system operator shall act in accordance with open, nondiscriminatory and market-based procedures, except
in cases where there is a deviation from the normal
operating mode or an accident in the system. In cases
where there is a deviation from the normal operating
mode or an accident in the system, the transmission
system operator shall act in accordance with the
provisions of the Network Code.
Revenue from the sale of electricity/capacity is
recognised based on acceptance-transfer deeds and
invoices issued each month for the electricity/capacity
delivered in the relevant month in accordance with
mutually concluded contracts. The Parent Company
believes that it acts as a principal when selling electricity
within the framework of the balancing service, as
the Parent Company is responsible for balancing the
electricity transmission system.
C) Connection fee to the electricity transmission
system (IFRS 15)
AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls" provides system participants
with the necessary connection to the transmission
system or an increase in the permitted load of existing
connections in accordance with the system participants’
connection rules issued by the Public Utilities
Commission.
The connection fee to the transmission system is a nonrefundable advance payment that customers pay for a

secure connection to the transmission network; such
fees are not separate operating obligations, as they are
closely related to the transmission system services.
The connection fee partially compensates the costs of
the infrastructure to be built, which is necessary for the
connection of the relevant customer to the network. The
connection fee to the transmission system is calculated
in accordance with the methodology established by the
Public Utilities Commission.
Transmission system connection fees are recognised
in the statement of financial position as deferred
income, classified as current and non-current liabilities,
depending on the expected timing of recognition in
profit or loss. These fees are amortised on a straightline basis over the estimated period of the commercial
relationship with the customer (i.e. 20 years) and
recognised in profit or loss over that period.
D) Revenue from natural gas transmission (IFRS 15)
The natural gas transmission service is considered a
single performance obligation under IFRS 15. Trading of
natural gas transmission capacity products is a regulated
service that the Group provides to transmission system
users at approved tariffs. Short-term (quarterly, monthly,
daily and intraday capacity) and long-term (annual
capacity) natural gas transmission capacity products
are offered. Revenue from transmission capacity trading
products, which by their nature involve the provision of
transmission infrastructure and do not change over time
for each capacity unit according to the selected product,
is recognised in the income statement for each reporting
month in proportion to the period of the relevant
transmission capacity product reserved by the user.

E) Revenue from natural gas storage (IFRS 15)
The natural gas storage service is considered a single
performance obligation under IFRS 15. The Group
provides the Inčukalns underground gas storage
capacity trading service at approved tariffs to storage
users who have reserved natural gas storage capacity
during the storage season. Revenue from the trading
of storage capacity, which by its nature means the
provision of the Inčukalns underground gas storage
infrastructure and does not change over the course of
the storage season, is recognised for each reporting
month in accordance with the storage tariff and in
proportion to the remaining months until the end of the
storage season.
F) Revenue from natural gas balancing (IFRS 15)
The Group maintains information on the amount
of natural gas injected into and withdrawn from the
transmission system by transmission system users and
calculates the imbalance. The daily imbalance is the
difference between the input and output. If the amount
of natural gas withdrawn, based on accounting data,
is greater than the amount of natural gas traded, the
Group sells natural gas to cover the missing amount of
natural gas in the system. In the financial statements,
the income from balancing is presented in the item
"Revenue" in gross value, taking into account that the
Subsidiary considers itself to be the principal. The
natural gas transmission service is considered a single
performance obligation under IFRS 15. Trading of
natural gas transmission capacity products is a regulated
service that the Group provides to transmission system

users at approved tariffs. Short-term (quarterly, monthly,
daily and intraday capacity) and long-term (annual
capacity) natural gas transmission capacity products
are offered. Revenue from transmission capacity trading
products, which by their nature involve the provision of
transmission infrastructure and do not change over time
for each capacity unit according to the selected product,
is recognised in the income statement for each reporting
month in proportion to the period of the relevant
transmission capacity product reserved by the user.
In order to comply with the principle of profit neutrality,
the Group calculates a neutrality fee. The neutrality
fee is a fee that the transmission system operator
pays to or receives from transmission system users
in connection with the balancing of the transmission
system. This fee is the difference between the
transmission system operator’s costs and revenues
from balancing. The neutrality fee can be both positive
and negative. In the case of a negative neutrality fee,
the transmission system operator pays the neutrality
fee to the transmission system users. In the case of a
positive neutrality fee, the transmission system operator
collects the neutrality fee from the transmission system
users. The Regulations issued by the PUC on the
Harmonisation of the Balancing Rules of the Unified
Natural Gas Transmission Entry-Exit System stipulate
that the financial neutrality of the transmission system
operator is ensured by a neutrality fee.
G) Electricity transit revenue (IFRS 15)
A compensation mechanism (inter - transmission system
operator compensation mechanism (ITC)) has been
established between transmission system operators
205


=== pdf p.206 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

(TSOs) in accordance with the requirements of
Regulation (EU) 838/2010 of 23 September 2010, which
provides for the establishment of a single compensation
mechanism for TSOs. The ITC mechanism provides
for uniform compensation principles for ensuring the
flow of transit electricity and ensuring the availability of
infrastructure necessary for transit flows. On the basis
of the ITC mechanism, the perimeter fee is paid to the
ITC Member State through which trade with a third
country takes place. In addition, revenue is generated
from ensuring the availability of infrastructure necessary
for the transit of electricity through the network owned
by the Parent Company. Revenue is recognised ad
gross value in the period, when Parent company has
performance obligation is satisfied and the right to
receive payment is formed.
H) Reactive electricity revenue (IFRS 15)
In addition to transmission services, a fee is charged
for the transfer of reactive power to the network. The
fee for the transfer of reactive power is calculated in
accordance with the Cabinet Regulation of the Republic
of Latvia No. 635 Regulations on the Trade and Use of
Electricity. The amount of reactive power transferred to
the network is determined based on meter readings. The
fee is charged on a monthly basis and is calculated by
applying the tariff of EUR 0.013 per kVArh to the volume
of reactive power transferred to the network.
I) Other services (IFRS 15)

for the services provided are made monthly in
accordance with mutual agreements.

FINANCING COMPONENT
The Parent Company and the Group do not enter into
contracts under which the period between the agreed
delivery time of goods and services and the customer’s
payment is longer than a year, respectively, the financing
component is not included in the transactions.

2.10. BORROWINGS AND
LOANS

2.11. RELATED PARTIES
Parties are considered related if one party has the ability
to control the other or has significant influence over the
other party in making financial and operating decisions.
Related parties of the Group and the Parent Company
are the shareholder who controls the company in making
economic decisions, members of the management
boards of the Group companies, members of the
supervisory board of the Parent Company, members of
the supervisory board of the Parent Company - the Audit
Committee - and close family members of any
of the above-mentioned persons, as well as companies
over which these persons have control or significant
influence.
Since all shares of AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls" are 100%
owned by the Republic of Latvia, state-controlled capital
companies are also considered as related parties.

Borrowings and loans are classified as current liabilities
unless there is an unconditional right to defer settlement
of the obligation for at least 12 months after the balance
sheet date.
General and specific borrowing costs that are directly
attributable to the acquisition or construction of the
underlying asset, i.e. assets that are expected to be
used or sold for a substantial period of time, are added
to the cost of constructing or acquiring the asset until the
asset is ready for its intended use.

Revenue from Other Services includes services
provided by a technical laboratory, equipment
maintenance services, and similar services. Payments

ASSESSMENT ON THE AMOUNT
OF CONGESTION MANAGEMENT
REVENUE TO BE RECOGNIZED TO

AS WELL AS THE PRESENTATION OF
NET CONGESTION CHARGE INCOME
RECEIVED IN CASH FLOW
According the decision of the PUC No. 64 of 22 May
2023 ''On tariffs for electricity transmission system
services of the joint-stock company "Augstsprieguma
tīkls", AS ''Augstsprieguma tīkls'' is permitted to use
previously obtained congestion fee income in the
amount of up to EUR 62,070,100 to cover the costs
related to the provision of electricity transmission
system services in 2023-2025. In accordance with
he decision of the State Electricity Regulatory
Commission No. 116 of 27 November 2025 ''On tariffs
for electricity transmission system services of the
AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls", it is permitted to divert
previously obtained congestion income in the amount of
up to EUR 44,411 thousand in 2026-2028 to cover the
costs related to the provision of electricity transmission
system services. The amount of congestion fee revenue
to be recognized in a particular year is determined
based on the costs approved in the current electricity
transmission system service tariff project, as well as
the permitted revenues. Also, taking into account the
fact that the received congestion fee is accounted for
analogy to the grants received and will be mainly used
for investment activities, they are reflected in the cash
flow under investment activities.

CARRYING AMOUNTS OF INTANGIBLE
ASSETS AND PPE, RIGHT-OF-USE ASSETS

the depreciation of PPE, amortisation of intangible
assets and impairment of assets.

analysis is not disclosed.

Impairment of assets:

For more detailed information, see Note 10.2.

The management of the Group and the Parent Company
assess the carrying amount of intangible assets, PPE
and right-of-use assets and assesses whether there is
any indication that the recoverable amount of the assets
is lower than the carrying amount. The management
of the Group and the Parent Company calculate and
recognise impairment losses for intangible assets,
property, plant and equipment and right-of-use assets
based on estimates of their future use, disposal or sale.

Revaluation of property, plant and equipment:

The Group and the Parent Company do not calculate
depreciation on emergency spare parts. The rationale
is based on experience that the possible depreciation
charge for such items would not be material in the
context of the financial statements. In addition, where
necessary, an impairment loss is recognised for
damaged goods, thus considering changes in the
recoverable amounts of spare parts.

In assessing impairment, management uses various
cash flow estimates that arise from the use of assets,
sales, maintenance and repairs of property, plant
and equipment, as well as inflation and interest rate
increases.

The Group’s management assesses annually
whether the carrying amount of the revalued PPE
differs significantly from their fair value at the balance
sheet date.

The estimates are based on forecasts of the general
economic environment, consumption and electricity
sales prices. If the actual situation changes in the future,
an additional impairment loss would be recognised, or
the recognised impairment loss could be partially or
completely reduced. Impairment costs are recognised
in the relevant reporting period. Impairment losses on
assets carried at revalued amount are recognised as
comprehensive income within the revaluation reserve
for non-current assets, but impairment losses on assets
carried at cost less depreciation and impairment, or on
assets carried at revalued amount if the impairment loss
exceeds the previously recognised residual revalued
amount, are recognised in the income statement within

For the property, plant and equipment classes that are
carried in revalued amounts revaluation is performed
by independent, external and certified valuation experts
using the income approach with the discounted cash
flow method. The valuation is performed based on the
current use of the PPE, which is assessed as the most
efficient and best use of these assets.

More detailed information on the revaluation of PPE is
provided in Note 10.2.
Depreciation and amortisation:
In accordance with the Group’s accounting policy, the
Group’s companies make estimates of the useful lives
and residual values of PPE. These estimates are based
on experience and industry practice and are reviewed
at the end of each reporting year. Historical experience
has shown that the actual useful lives sometimes
have been longer than the estimates. The values of
fully depreciated intangible assets and property, plant
and equipment are presented in Notes 10.1 and 10.2.
Quantifying the impact of possible changes in the useful

3. OPERATING SEGMENTS
Determination of segments is based on the Group’s
internal organisational structure, which forms the basis
for monitoring and controlling the operating results of the
segments, which is carried out by the decision maker of
the operating segment - the management of the Group
company operating in each of the segments. The Parent
Company’s board reviews the financial results of the
operating segments.
The segments to be presented in the report are
operating segments or groups of segments that meet
certain criteria. An operating segment is a component
of the Group that is engaged in economic activities
from which it can earn revenues and cover expenses
(including income and expenses related to transactions
with other components of the Group), the results of
which are regularly reviewed by the chief operating
209


=== pdf p.210 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

decision maker in order to make decisions about the
resources allocated to the segment, assess its operating
results and for which separate financial information is
available.
The profit indicator monitored by the chief operating
decision maker is mainly EBITDA, but it also monitors
operating profit. In separate financial statements,
operating profit excludes dividend income and interest
income from Subsidiaries.
The Group divides its operations into three segments:
electricity transmission, natural gas storage and natural
gas transmission. The Parent Company divides its
operations into one main operating segment – electricity
transmission. The Group operates geographically only in
the territory of Latvia.

ELECTRICITY TRANSMISSION
The electricity transmission segment provides electricity
transmission system services and ensures balancing
and stability in the transmission network.
The electricity transmission network consists of
interconnected networks and facilities, including crossborder connections with a voltage of 110 kV and above,
used to transmit electricity from electricity producers to
the relevant distribution network or end users.

NATURAL GAS TRANSMISSION

cycle lasts from 1 May to 30 April of the following year.

Conexus is the only natural gas transmission and
storage operator in Latvia, which ensures the
maintenance of the natural gas transmission system,
its safe and continuous operation, and interconnections
with the transmission systems of other countries,
enabling system users to use the natural gas
transmission system for natural gas trading.

The table below presents information on the revenue,
results and profit of the Group’s operating segments,
as well as the assets and liabilities of the Group and the
Parent Company. Inter-segment revenues are eliminated
on consolidation and are presented in the "Adjustments
and eliminations" column. Transactions between
segments are carried out at regulated tariffs, where
applicable, or on an arm’s length basis.

The natural gas transmission segment generates
revenue from the trading of capacity for both natural
gas consumption in Latvia and international natural gas
transportation.

Segment information is provided only for the Group,
as the Parent Company is considered to be a single
operating segment – the electricity transmission
segment.

NATURAL GAS STORAGE
The natural gas storage segment provides storage of
natural gas required for the heating season and other
needs of system users at the Inčukalns UGS.
The task of the gas storage is to ensure a constant
supply of gas to consumers regardless of seasonal
changes in consumption, by pumping natural gas in the
summer and withdrawing it in the winter. Inčukalns UGS
is the only functioning underground gas storage facility
in the Baltic States, ensuring the stability of gas supply
in the region since 1968.
The natural gas storage segment generates revenue
from the reservation of storage capacities, which are
allocated to system users in storage capacity auctions
within the framework of the storage cycle. The storage

4.1. CONTRACT BALANCES
The Group’s contract assets arise from the recognition
of estimated revenue from fulfilled contractual
obligations, including transit losses under the ITC
mechanism (see note 2.12). Contract liabilities arise
from connection fees related to property, plant and
equipment construction, as the cumulative payments
received from customers at each balance sheet date
do not match the amount of revenue recognised in
the contracts (see note 2.9 (c)).

4.2. CONTRACT
PERFORMANCE
OBLIGATIONS
The majority of the Group’s contracts are for the delivery
of goods and services within the next 12 months,
which are subject to the practical expediency referred
to in paragraph 121(a) of IFRS 15. For capacity
reservation and connection contracts, the initial contract
term exceeds 12 months. As a result, the Group’s
right to consideration does not correspond directly
to its performance. The amount of revenue expected
to be recognised in future periods as the remaining
performance obligations are fulfilled is analysed
as follows:

ANNUAL REPORT
PROPERTY, PLANT AND EQUIPMENT
OF THE ELECTRICITY TRANSMISSION
SEGMENT
The following groups of property, plant and equipment
of the electricity transmission segment are revalued
regularly, but not less than once every five years, and
are presented as:

that the fair value of property, plant and equipment does
not differ significantly from their carrying amount.
If the WACC were 6.72%, the carrying amount of
property, plant and equipment for revalued class would
decrease by EUR 8,784 thousand. If the WACC were
4.72%, the carrying amount of property, plant and
equipment for revalued class would increase by

• electricity transmission structures;
• transmission lines, technological equipment;
• other electricity transmission equipment.
The above classes of property, plant and equipment
were revalued in 2025 at their fair value as of
1 September 2025. The revaluation was performed
by an independent external certified appraiser using
the income approach discounted cash flow method,
which was considered the most appropriate valuation
technique for the Group. Due to the specific nature and
use of the assets, Level 3 inputs were applied in the
valuation, as the inputs are not directly observable in the
market for the relevant type of asset.
The valuation was performed in accordance with IFRS
Accounting Standards and reflects the current use of
the property, plant and equipment, which is considered
as the best and the most effective use. As part of the
valuation, the discounted cash flows of each asset
were determined using the rate of return on capital
approved by the Public Utilities Commission: 2.72% for
2025, 5.82% for 2026–2028 and 5.72% for subsequent
periods, that represent the weighted average cost of
capital (WACC) and was discounted with discount rate
of 5.72% (WACC). The results of the valuation indicate

EUR 10,809 thousand.

of the revalued asset groups, assuming that the assets
are carried at cost.:

PROPERTY, PLANT AND EQUIPMENT OF
THE NATURAL GAS TRANSMISSION AND
NATURAL GAS STORAGE SEGMENTS

equipment were initially recognised at fair value in the
consolidated statement of financial position upon the
establishment of the Group. Accordingly, any impairment
is recognised in profit or loss.

The following groups of property, plant and equipment
of the electricity transmission segment are revalued
regularly, but not less than once every five years, and
are presented as:
• Buildings and civil engineering structures
• Technological equipment and devices
• Wells
• Gas pumping and automatic control equipment
In 2024, a regular revaluation of property, plant and
equipment was performed. The valuation was based on
the income approach (discounted cash flow method).
The Company determined the fair value as at 31
December 2024 based on management’s estimates of
forecast economic activity over a 10-year period. The
discount rate applied in the valuation was 7.10%. A
decrease in the discount rate to 6.10% would result in a
lower reduction in the carrying amount of property, plant
and equipment, amounting to EUR 15,999 thousand.
Conversely, an increase in the discount rate to 8.10%
would result in a greater reduction in the carrying
amount of property, plant and equipment, amounting to
EUR 40,762 thousand. Due to the specific nature and
use of these assets, Level 3 inputs have been used in
the revaluation, as the inputs are not readily observable
in the market for the relevant type of asset.
In the consolidated financial statements, no revaluation
reserve had previously been recognised in respect of
these assets, as the Subsidiary’s property, plant and

=== pdf p.236 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

The assets of the subsidiary consist of two cashgenerating units: natural gas transmission and natural
gas storage. When determining the cash-generating
units, it is assumed that the infrastructure elements
that make up the natural gas transmission and storage
system are separable and generate largely independent
cash flows.
The calculation of the recoverable amount of the assets
is based on the value in use of the cash-generating
units, which is determined by discounting the expected
future cash flows that will arise from the continued use
of the cash-generating units. The cash flows are based
on the planned results of the cash-generating units and
management's forecasts for the development of the
cash-generating units' operations.
Considering the forecasted business volumes and
the potential market value of the assets, the Group's
management believes that no significant adjustments
to the value of intangible assets and property, plant and
equipment are necessary as of 31 December 2025.

In accordance with the laws and regulations of the Republic of Latvia, the profit for the reporting year is not subject to income tax.
Corporate income tax applies to the Group's Subsidiary for distributed dividends during the year, for what income tax is calculated.
For more information on income tax rates, see Note 2.8.
In 2025, the Subsidiary distributed EUR 19,495,184 in dividends (EUR 11,935,826 in 2024), for which EUR 4,873,796
(EUR 2,983,957 in 2024) income tax at a tax rate of 20/80 percent was calculated. Corporate income tax was not calculated for
the parent company's distributed profit in dividends of EUR 10,391,040 in 2025 and EUR 10,117,120 in 2024, as the pass-through
dividend exemption was applied. As of 31.12.2025, the Group has EUR 75,997,958 (31.12.2024: EUR 133,563,642) of retained
earnings, which could theoretically be distributed in dividends and as a result, Corporate Income Tax would be payable in
the amount of EUR 18,999,490 (EUR 28,048,365 in 2024). There are no potential income tax consequences for 2025 and
2024 that are practically undetermined

=== pdf p.246 ===
ANNUAL REPORT
DEFERRED TAX
Deferred tax of the Group is recognised as the future
corporate income tax payable on the Subsidiary’s
retained earnings that are expected to be distributed to
the Parent Company as dividends. The calculation is
based on the dividend policy set out in the Subsidiary’s

strategy, which provides for the distribution of 90%
of the Subsidiary’s profit, as well as a one-year lookback period. The portion of deferred tax attributable
to dividends payable to non-controlling interests is
recognised as part of non-controlling interests in equity.

ANNUAL REPORT

20. BORROWINGS AND
LEASE LIABILITIES
In October 2021, the Parent Company issued bonds with
a nominal value of EUR 100,000 thousand. The bonds
are listed on Nasdaq Riga, have a fixed coupon rate of
0.5% per annum, and mature on 20 January 2027.
The Parent Company has the option to redeem the
bonds in full, but not in part, within three months prior
to maturity at their nominal value together with accrued
interest. In addition to events of default, the bond terms
include a cross-default clause, which is triggered if
overdue payments of the Parent Company and its
Subsidiary, individually or in aggregate, exceed EUR
20,000 thousand.
To finance electricity transmission network infrastructure
projects, AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls" entered into a loan
agreement with Luminor Bank AS on 20 December
2024. Under this agreement, the Parent Company had
access to financing of up to EUR 80,000 thousand, of
which EUR 20,000 thousand was drawn in 2025, with a
maturity date of 15 December 2029. In order to optimise
the management of financial resources, on 7 November
2025, the Parent Company irrevocably waived the
undrawn portion of the loan amounting to EUR 60,000
thousand under the agreement.
In order to finance working capital and ensure a liquidity
reserve for the next 24 months, on 10 January 2025,
a credit line agreement was concluded between AS
"Augstsprieguma tīkls" and AS "SEB banka" with a limit

of up to 10,000 thousand EUR, with maturity 10 January
2027 and a base interest rate of 3-month EURIBOR
and an added interest rate of 0.59%. Additionally,
on December 11, 2025, a credit line agreement was
concluded between AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls" and AS
"SEB banka" with a limit of up to EUR 10,000 thousand,
with maturity date December 10, 2027 and base interest
rate of 3-month EURIBOR, and an added interest rate
of 0.49%. In addition to the liquidity reserve, taking
into account the existing green bond maturity date –
20 January 2027, on 11 December 2025, a credit line
agreement was concluded between AS "Augstsprieguma
tīkls" and AS "SEB banka" with a limit of up to EUR
90,000 thousand, with maturity date 10 March 2027
and base interest rate of 3-month EURIBOR and an
added interest rate of 0.48%. AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls"
has not received any loans under the aforementioned
credit line agreements during the reporting period. The
aforementioned credit line agreements are unsecured.
The financial resources at the disposal of the Parent
Company are sufficient to meet its financial needs. The
restrictions on financial covenants set out in the Parent
company's existing loan agreements were complied
during the reporting period and at the time the report
was authorised.

Financial ratio restrictions

rate (31 December 2024: 100%).
The Parent Company’s subsidiary natural gas operator
AS "Conexus Baltic Grid" raises external financing itself.
In the reporting year, AS "Conexus Baltic Grid" had
borrowings from both local and foreign credit institutions.
The weighted average interest rate of borrowings at
the end of the reporting year was 2.18% (31.12.2024:
2.38%). 36% of the loans received but not yet repaid
at the end of the reporting year were at a fixed interest
rate (31.12.2024: 39%). All of Conexus’ borrowings are
denominated in euros and unsecured.

non-current borrowing agreements in the amount of
EUR 36,000 thousand, as well as a bank overdraft

borrowings are denominated in euros and unsecured.

22. FAIR VALUE
CONSIDERATIONS
IFRS 13 establishes a fair value hierarchy that
categorises valuation techniques based on the
inputs used, distinguishing between observable and
unobservable inputs. Observable inputs are derived from
independent sources. Where inputs are not observable,
valuation techniques reflect the Group’s assumptions
about the assumptions that market participants would
use under current market conditions.
The fair value hierarchy prioritises the use of observable
market data whenever available. In measuring fair value,
the Group and the Parent Company maximise the use of
relevant observable market data and minimise the use of
unobservable market data.
The objective of fair value measurement, even where
markets are not active, is to determine the price at which
an orderly transaction would take place between market
participants at the measurement date under current
market conditions.
Fair value is determined using quoted prices or valuation
techniques that incorporate observable inputs and,
where necessary, unobservable inputs based on internal
models. Based on the fair value hierarchy, valuation
techniques are categorised into Level 1, Level 2 and
Level 3.

The level of the fair value hierarchy of a financial
instrument should be determined as the lowest level if a
significant portion of its value is derived from inputs at
lower level.
The classification of a financial instrument within a fair
value hierarchy is a two-step process:
1. Classify the inputs at each level to determine the fair
value hierarchy;
2. Classify the financial instrument itself based on the
lowest level if a significant portion of its value is derived
from lower-level inputs.

QUOTED MARKET PRICES – LEVEL 1
Level 1 inputs consist of unadjusted quoted prices in
active markets for identical assets or liabilities that
are readily available and represent actual market
transactions at the measurement date.

INPUTS USING OBSERVABLE MARKET
DATA – LEVEL 2
Level 2 inputs are inputs other than quoted prices
included within Level 1 that are observable for the asset
or liability, either directly or indirectly, including prices
and inputs derived from observable market data.

INPUTS USING UNOBSERVABLE MARKET
DATA – LEVEL 3
A valuation technique that uses market inputs that are
not based on observable market data (unobservable
market data) is classified as Level 3. Unobservable
market data are data that are not readily available in an
active market due to the illiquidity of the market or the
complexity of the financial instrument. Level 3 inputs
are generally determined based on observable market
data of a similar nature, historical observations or using
analytical approaches.

increase in interest rates in the market, the carrying
value of the bonds issued by the Parent Company
exceeds their market value. The risk premium applied
to the Group and the Parent Company has not changed
significantly.
Management assessed that cash and short-term
deposits, accounts receivable, accounts payable, bank
overdrafts, and other current liabilities approximate their
carrying values, mainly due to the short maturity of
these instruments

ASSETS AND LIABILITIES FOR WHICH
FAIR VALUE IS REPORTED, AND ASSETS
MEASURED AT FAIR VALUE
The fair value of borrowings from credit institutions
is determined by discounting future cash flows at
market interest rates. Since the interest rates applied
to borrowings from credit institutions were recently
determined and do not differ significantly from market
interest rates (although some are fixed), the fair value of
long-term liabilities approximates their carrying amount.
Although the bonds issued (included in the borrowings
position) are quoted in the market, transactions in these
securities occur infrequently and cannot be considered
indicative of an active market. Therefore, bonds are not
measured using Level 1 inputs. The fair value of the
bonds is determined by discounting future cash flows
using market interest rates. As the interest rates applied
to the bonds were set in 2021, taking into account the

23. FINANCIAL RISK
MANAGEMENT
The Group manages financial risk in accordance
with the Financial Risk Management Policy and its
subordinate Financial Risk Management Regulations.
The Group’s Subsidiary AS "Conexus Baltic Grid"
develops and approves its Financial Risk Management
Policies, which are aligned with the basic principles
included in the Group Policy.

and irrevocably available credit facilities for the next 24
months.
The Group and the Parent Company maintain prudent
liquidity risk management by ensuring constant cash
flow monitoring, forecasting short-term and long-term
cash flows, and ensuring sufficient financial resources
are available to meet obligations as they fall due.
The management of the Group and the Parent Company
believes that the Group and the Parent Company will
have sufficient cash resources to ensure that their
liquidity is not threatened.

Financial resource management is aimed at ensuring
the financing of business activities and financial stability,
implementing conservative financial risk management.
Within the framework of financial risk management,
the Group and the Parent Company use financial risk
controls and take risk mitigation measures, reducing the
risk in open positions.

LIQUIDITY RISK
Liquidity risk is related to the ability of the Group and
the Parent Company to meet its obligations as they fall
due. In order to mitigate short-term liquidity risk and to
mitigate unpredictable cash flow fluctuations caused by
operational risk, the Group and the Parent Company
maintain a reserve in the form of cash or committed

INTEREST RATE RISK
Interest rate risk arises mainly from loans with a
variable interest rate, creating the risk that financial
costs will increase significantly due to an increase in
interest rates. To limit the risk, the Group’s and Parent
Company’s Financial Risk Management policy stipulates
that the proportion of fixed rates or rates with limited
increases in the loan portfolio may not be less than
35%. At the same time, the Financial Risk Management
policy stipulates that deviations from this indicator are
permitted in the restructuring process of liabilities taken
over in the process of reorganisation of ownership rights
of transmission assets.
The Parent Company’s loan portfolio as of 31 December
2025 consisted of bonds of EUR 99,972 thousand (loan

portfolio at 31.12.2024: EUR 99,946 thousand) (nominal
value EUR 100,000 thousand) with a term of 5.25 years,
a fixed annual interest rate (coupon) of 0.5% and a yield
of 0.527% and a loan from Luminor Bank AS Latvian
branch in the amount of 20,000 thousand EUR (at
amortised cost at 31.12.2025: EUR 19,989 thousand)
with maturity date of 15 December 2029. The share of
long-term loans with a fixed rate in the total loan portfolio
was 83% (100% in 2024).
In order to finance working capital and to ensure a
liquidity reserve for the next 24 months, on 10 January
2025, a credit line agreement was concluded between
AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls" and AS "SEB banka" with
a limit of up to EUR 10,000 thousand with maturity
10 January 2027, with a base interest rate of 3-month
EURIBOR and an added interest rate of 0.59%. In
addition, on 11 December 2025, a credit line agreement
was concluded between AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls"

and AS "SEB banka" with a limit of up to EUR 10,000
thousand, maturity date 10 December 2027, with
a base interest rate of 3-month EURIBOR and an
added interest rate of 0.49%. In order to comply with
the liquidity requirements of the rating agency and to
ensure the refinancing process of the existing bonds
of AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls", on 11 December 2025,
a credit line agreement was concluded between AS
"Augstsprieguma tīkls" and AS "SEB banka" with a limit
of up to EUR 90,000 thousand, with maturity 10 March
2027, with a base interest rate of 3-month EURIBOR and
an added interest rate of 0.48%. During the reporting
period, AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls" did not receive any
loans within the framework of the aforementioned credit
line agreements.

rate and EUR 62,226 thousand (in 2024: EUR 40,490
thousand) with a variable interest rate. The proportion
of non-current borrowings with a fixed rate in the total
borrowing portfolio was 67% (31.12.2024: 76%).

CREDIT RISK
Credit risk arises if the Group’s or Parent Company’s
partner is unable to fulfil its contractual obligations,
which results in losses for the Group or Parent
Company. Credit risk arises from the Group’s and
Parent Company’s cash, deposits in commercial banks
and receivables. Credit risk may be related to financial
counterparty risk and counterparty risk.
In the course of its operations, the Group and the Parent
Company cooperate with local and foreign financial
institutions. As a result, counterparty credit risk arises,
whereby the Group and the Parent Company may
incur losses in the event of insolvency or suspension
of operations of their counterparties. In respect of
external funding, this risk exists until the funds are drawn
and transferred to one of the Group’s or the Parent
Company’s partner banks.
Credit risk arising from the Group’s and Parent
Company’s cash in current accounts is managed
in accordance with the Group’s Financial Risk
Management Policy and Financial Risk Management
policy, balancing the allocation of financial resources.
In accordance with the Financial Risk Management
Policy, in cooperation with banks and financial
institutions, business partners with a minimum credit
rating of at least investment grade, set by the parent
bank or an international credit rating agency, are
accepted.

When carrying out economic activities, the Group and
the Parent Company cooperate with local and foreign
merchants. Consequently, there is a risk of business
partners or customers - in the event of insolvency or
suspension of operations of cooperation partners,
the Group or the Parent Company may suffer losses.
The International and National Sanctions Law of the
Republic of Latvia determines financial and civil law
restrictions for entities included in the sanctions list,
including the freezing of financial assets. Taking into
account the above, cooperation with an entity included
in the sanctions list creates a risk of non-fulfilment of
contractual obligations for the Group or the Parent
Company, as well as legal and reputational risks.
Although the Group and the Parent Company have a
significant concentration of debtor risk in relation to
one counterparty or a group of similar counterparties,
this risk is assessed as limited, taking into account
that the most significant counterparty is a stateowned commercial company - joint-stock company
"Latvenergo", as well as its group companies.
The credit risk associated with receivables is managed
in accordance with the risk management measures
specified in the Financial Risk Management Regulations,
monthly, but not less than quarterly, by performing an
analysis of receivables.

=== pdf p.274 ===
ANNUAL REPORT
The Group and the Parent Company continuously
monitor the balances of receivables to minimise the
possibility of uncollectible receivables. The Group
continuously analyses the possible impairment of
receivables from contracts with customers and other
receivables. The Group has received security deposits
of EUR 12,360,299 (in 2024: EUR 13,353,245) as
collateral for receivables.
The Group and the Parent Company have not
developed an internal credit rating system for assessing
receivables.
There were no significant changes in the expected credit
losses related to receivables in 2025. Receivables are
written off only if their recovery is not expected.
Indicators indicating the impossibility of recovery
include, among others, the debtor's inability to agree
on a repayment schedule, accompanied by the debtor's
insolvency, bankruptcy or liquidation. Although cash
and deposits are also subject to the ECL requirements
under IFRS 9, the amount of expected credit losses
determined is immaterial, given that cash and term
deposits are held in financial institutions that themselves
or their parent banks have investment-grade credit
ratings (predominantly A-level credit ratings).

ANNUAL REPORT

CAPITAL RISK
MANAGEMENT
The shareholder of the Parent Company is the
Republic of Latvia (100%) represented by the Ministry
of Climate and Energy. The objective of capital risk
management is to ensure the sustainable operation and
development of the Group and the Parent Company,
the financing necessary for the implementation of the
development plan in transmission assets, and the
fulfilment of the restrictive conditions set out in the loan
agreements. The restrictive conditions set out in the
loan agreements have not been violated. In order to
ensure the fulfilment of the restrictive conditions set out
in the loan agreements, a regular analysis of the equity
ratio is performed. In accordance with the Financial
Risk Management Policy of the Augstsprieguma tīkls
Group, equity must be maintained at a level of at least
35%. At 31 December 2025, the equity ratio of AS
"Augstsprieguma tīkls" in the balance sheet is 37%, and
that of the Group is 41% (31 December 2024: 38% and
42%, respectively).

CURRENCY RISK
Business activities the Group and Parent Company
are focused on transactions, assets and liabilities in
the functional currency of Group and Parent company,
which is the euro. The Group’s and Parent Company’s

currency risk is assessed as insignificant as they have
no balances in foreign currencies.

24. RELATED PARTY
TRANSACTIONS

The remuneration of the management of the Group
"Augstsprieguma tīkls" includes remuneration for the
members of the Management Board and the Council of
the Group’s companies. The remuneration of the Parent
Company’s management includes remuneration for the
members of the Management Board and the Council of
the Parent Company. Detailed information is disclosed in
Note 7.

Related parties are considered to be governmentrelated entities that are controlled, jointly controlled or
significantly influenced by the Latvian state.
Commercial transactions that occur in the ordinary
course of business with the Latvian government,
including its ministries and agencies, and transactions
between government-controlled entities and public
service providers are excluded from the scope of
quantitative related party disclosures based on the
provisions of paragraph 25 of IAS 24. The Group and
the Parent Company enter into transactions with many
of these entities on an arm's length prices. Transactions
with government-related entities include taxes and
other related services. Income taxes are disclosed in
the Statement of Financial Position and the Statement
of Profit or Loss as disclosed in Notes 14 and 15,
social security contributions are disclosed in Note 7,
and taxes payable are disclosed in Note 21. Significant
transactions are carried out with the energy supply
service provider AS "Latvenergo", the distribution system
operator AS "Sadales tīkls" and the public electricity
trader AS "Enerģijas publiskais tirgotājs".

25. FUTURE LIABILITIES
AND CONTINGENT
LIABILITIES
As of 31 December 2025, the Group had future
liabilities of EUR 91,411 thousand (31.12.2024: EUR
174,910 thousand) and the Parent Company had future
commitments of EUR 85,802 thousand (31.12.2024: EUR
156,560 thousand) in relation to capital investments for
which contracts have been concluded as of the balance
sheet date, but the liabilities have not yet occurred.
By the decision of the AST Management Board of 18
June 2025 On the Development Plan for the Electricity
Transmission System, the Development Plan for the
Electricity Transmission System for the period from 2026
to 2035 was approved.
The approved Development Plan determines the
development of the transmission system and the
necessary financial investments in the transmission
infrastructure for the next ten years, providing for an
investment of EUR 908 million in the development of the
electricity transmission system. Detailed information on
the further development of the electricity transmission
system, as well as system management and market
development, innovations and research is provided in
the Sustainability Report section "Operating segments".

the amount of EUR 5,609 thousand (Parent31.12.2024:
EUR 18,350 thousand).
In view of its participation in the Nord Pool electricity
exchange, a guarantor service agreement has been
concluded between the Parent Company and AS
"SEB banka", at the end of the reporting period in the
amount of 3,000 thousand EUR (31.12.2024: EUR 3,000
thousand).
As a result of the implementation of international
sanctions, the Parent Company is involved in one
legal proceeding relating to delayed payment. The
Arbitration Institute of the Stockholm Chamber of
Commerce (SCC) has initiated arbitration proceeding
against Baltic transmission system operators, including
AS "Augstsprieguma tīkls". The proceedings are
currently temporarily suspended. The proceedings
relate to the operators’ non-payment of certain
obligations in compliance with applicable international
sanctions. In accordance with the rules of the SCC,
detailed information on the proceedings is considered
confidential. In the opinion of management, the
recognised liabilities are adequate, and no additional
provisions are required to cover expected losses.

=== pdf p.286 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

BASIS FOR OPINION

KEY AUDIT MATTERS

In accordance with the ’Law on Audit Services’ of
the Republic of Latvia we conducted our audit in
accordance with International Standards on Auditing
adopted in the Republic of Latvia (ISAs). Our
responsibilities under those standards are further
described in the Auditors’ Responsibility for the Audit
of the Consolidated and Separate Financial Statements
section of our report.

Key audit matters are those matters that, in our
professional judgment, were of most significance in
our audit of the consolidated and separate financial
statements of the current period. These matters were
addressed in the context of our audit of the consolidated
and separate financial statements as a whole, and in
forming our opinion thereon, and we do not provide a
separate opinion on these matters.

We are independent of the Group and the Company
in accordance with the International Code of Ethics
for Professional Accountants (including International
Independence Standards) developed by the International
Ethics Standards Board for Accountants (IESBA Code),
as applicable to audits of the financial statements
of public interest entities, together with the ethical
requirements that are relevant to audits of the financial
statements of public interest entities in the Republic
of Latvia. We have also fulfilled our other professional
ethics responsibilities and objectivity requirements in
accordance with the IESBA Code and the ‘Law on
Audit Services’ of the Republic of Latvia.

We have determined the matters described below
to be the key audit matters to be communicated
in our report.

We believe that the audit evidence we have
obtained is sufficient and appropriate to provide
a basis for our opinion.

THE KEY AUDIT MATTERS

HOW THE MATTER WAS ADDRESSED
IN THE AUDIT

Revaluation of electricity transmission segment property, plant
and equipment in the consolidated and separate financial statements
The Group and the Company measures property,
plant and equipment at cost or revalued amount, less
accumulated depreciation and accumulated impairment
losses, if any.
As disclosed in Note 10.2 Property, plant and equipment
to the consolidated and separate financial statements, the
carrying amount of the Group's and Company’s electricity
transmission segment property, plant and equipment as at
31 December 2025 was
797 449 874 EUR.
The Group's management performed a revaluation
of these items of property, plant and equipment as at
31 December 2025 and estimated fair values applying
depreciated replacement cost method as well as
income method.
As a result, the carrying amount of property, plant and
equipment (electricity transmission structures, transmission
lines, technological equipment and other electricity
transmission equipment) was not revised, as the fair value
of property, plant and equipment determined using both
methods did not materially differ from the carrying value.

Our audit procedures included, among others:
Evaluating the Group's accounting policy for the
fair value measurement of the property, plant
and equipment against the requirements of
IFRS Accounting Standards adopted by the
European Union.
Gaining an understanding of management’s
process for measuring the fair value of the
specific fixed asset groups and assessing the
methodology and the assumptions used by
management in their internal assessment
of the property, plant and equipment groups.
Assisted by our own valuation specialists,
assessing whether the income method used
by management is appropriate to determine the
fair values of the property, plant and equipment
groups and challenging the reasonableness of
the discount rate used to discount the future
cash flows. Critically assessing the future cash
flow projections for the electricity transmission
segment property, plant and equipment groups,
including the growth rates applied.

286


=== pdf p.287 ===
ANNUAL REPORT

THE KEY AUDIT MATTERS

HOW THE MATTER WAS ADDRESSED
IN THE AUDIT

THE KEY AUDIT MATTERS

HOW THE MATTER WAS ADDRESSED
IN THE AUDIT

Revaluation of electricity transmission segment property, plant
and equipment in the consolidated and separate financial statements

Recognition of electrical energy congestion income in consolidated
and separate financial statements

The revaluation of property, plant and equipment
requires the use of significant assumptions and
judgments by the management. These include: the
selection of the most appropriate valuation approach
(both depreciated replacement cost method as
well as income method were selected). For income
method input data are future cash flows, growth rate
and discount rate. For depreciated replacement cost
method input data is physical obsolescence adjustment,
economic obsolescence adjustment, functional or
technological obsolescence adjustment.

As disclosed in Note 19 to consolidated and separate
financial statements, the Group and the Company have
recognized congestion next period income of
EUR 125 944 337 as at 31 December 2025 and, as
disclosed in Note 4 and 5, congestion revenue and
other income of EUR 61 794 168 and EUR 2 116 614,
respectively, for the year ended 31 December 2025.

Considering the subjective nature of the fair value
estimate and the fact that electricity transmission
segment property, plant and equipment represents
50% of the total assets, the revaluation of electricity
transmission segment property, plant and equipment is
one of the key audit matters.

Also assisted by our own valuation specialists,
assessing whether the depreciated replacement
cost method used by management is appropriate
to determine the fair values of the property, plant
and equipment groups. Furthermore, specialists
critically assessed the physical obsolescence
adjustments, economic obsolescence adjustments,
functional or technological obsolescence
adjustments used for the electricity transmission
segment property, plant and equipment groups.
Testing the mathematical accuracy of the models.
Recalculating the sensitivity analysis of the key
assumptions made by management and conducting
our own independent sensitivity analysis by
replacing the key assumptions with different
scenarios.
Considering whether the Group’s and the
Company’s disclosures regarding the fair value
estimates of property, plant and equipment
are in line with the applicable requirements of
IFRS Accounting Standards adopted by the
European Union.

Public Utilities Commission defines the use
of congestion income in Latvia following the provisions
of EC Regulations as described in Note 2.6
Due to significant judgement used in selecting the
accounting policy for congestion revenue recognition,
as disclosed in Note 2.12, as well as the significance
of next period congestion income, the recognition of
congestion income is one of the key audit matters.

Our audit procedures included, among others:
Evaluating the Group's and Company’s accounting
policy for the recognition of congestion income against
the requirements of IFRS Accounting Standards
adopted by the European Union and market practice.
Understanding the Group's and Company’s congestion
management process, including the recognition of
income received from the auctions concluded and the
allocation of deferred income to profit or loss.
Testing congestion income receipts through full
confirmation of turnovers with auction operators.
For a sample of transactions, evaluating whether
congestion revenue has been recognized in profit or
loss in line with the costs associated with congestion
management.
Considering whether the Group's and Company’s
disclosures regarding the congestion income
recognition are in line with the applicable requirements
of IFRS Accounting Standards adopted by the
European Union.

accompanying Group Consolidated Sustainability

www.ast.lv/lv/content/korporativas-parvaldibasmodelis.
Our opinion on the consolidated and separate financial
statements does not cover the other information
included in the Group’s Consolidated Sustainability
Report and Group’s and Company’s Annual Report, and
we do not express any form of assurance conclusion

engagement on the Group Consolidated Sustainability
Report that forms part of the Other Information and
provided a separate assurance practitioner’s conclusion
thereon that is set out on pages 165 to 168.
In connection with our audit of the consolidated and
separate financial statements, our responsibility is to
read the other information and, in doing so, consider
whether the other information is materially inconsistent
with the consolidated and separate financial statements
or our knowledge obtained in the audit or otherwise
appears to be materially misstated.
If, based on the work we have performed and in light

of the knowledge and understanding of the Group,
Company and their environment obtained in the course
of our audit, we conclude that there is a material
misstatement of this other information, we are
required to report that fact. We have nothing to
report in this regard.

RESPONSIBILITIES OF
MANAGEMENT AND
THOSE CHARGED WITH
GOVERNANCE FOR THE
CONSOLIDATED AND
SEPARATE FINANCIAL
STATEMENTS
Management is responsible for the preparation of the
consolidated and separate financial statements that give

a true and fair view in accordance with IFRS Accounting
Standards as adopted by the European Union and for
such internal control as management determines is
necessary to enable the preparation of consolidated and
separate financial statements that are free from material
misstatement, whether due to fraud or error.
In preparing the consolidated and separate financial
statements, management is responsible for assessing
the Group’s and Company’s ability to continue as
a going concern, disclosing, as applicable, matters
related to going concern and using the going concern
basis of accounting unless management either intends
to liquidate the Group and Company or to cease
operations, or has no realistic alternative but to do so.
Those charged with governance are responsible for
overseeing the Group’s and Company’s financial
reporting process.

AUDITORS’
RESPONSIBILITY FOR
THE AUDIT OF THE
CONSOLIDATED AND
SEPARATE FINANCIAL
STATEMENTS
Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance
about whether the consolidated and separate
financial statements as a whole are free from material
misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to
issue an auditors’ report that includes our opinion.
Reasonable assurance is a high level of assurance,
but is not a guarantee that an audit conducted in
accordance with ISAs will always detect a material
misstatement when it exists. Misstatements can arise
from fraud or error and are considered material if,
individually or in the aggregate, they could reasonably
be expected to influence the economic decisions of
users taken on the basis of these consolidated and
separate financial statements.
As part of an audit in accordance with ISAs,
we exercise professional judgment and maintain
professional scepticism throughout the audit. We also:

• Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement
of the consolidated and separate financial statements,
whether due to fraud or error, design and perform
audit procedures responsive to those risks, and
obtain audit evidence that is sufficient and appropriate
to provide a basis for our opinion. The risk of not
detecting a material misstatement resulting from fraud
is higher than for one resulting from error, as fraud
may involve collusion, forgery, intentional omissions,
misrepresentations, or the override of internal control.
• Obtain an understanding of internal control relevant
to the audit in order to design audit procedures that
are appropriate in the circumstances, but not for the
purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness
of the Group’s and Company’s internal control.
• Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies
used and the reasonableness of accounting estimates
and related disclosures made by management.
• Conclude on the appropriateness of management’s
use of the going concern basis of accounting and,
based on the audit evidence obtained, whether
a material uncertainty exists related to events or
conditions that may cast significant doubt on the
Group’s and Company’s ability to continue as a going
concern. If we conclude that a material uncertainty
exists, we are required to draw attention in our
auditors’ report to the related disclosures in the
consolidated and separate financial statements or,
if such disclosures are inadequate, to modify our
opinion. Our conclusions are based on the audit
evidence obtained up to the date of our auditors’
report. However, future events or conditions may
289

cause the Group and Company to cease to continue
as a going concern.
• Evaluate the overall presentation, structure and
content of the consolidated and separate financial
statements, including the disclosures, and whether
the consolidated and separate financial statements
represent the underlying transactions and events
in a manner that achieves a fair presentation.
• Plan and perform the group audit to obtain sufficient
appropriate audit evidence regarding the financial
information of the entities or business units within
the group as a basis for forming an opinion on the
group financial statements. We are responsible for
the direction, supervision and review of the audit work
performed for purposes of the group audit. We remain
solely responsible for our audit opinion.
We communicate with those charged with governance
regarding, among other matters, the planned scope
and timing of the audit and significant audit findings,
including any significant deficiencies in internal control
that we identify during our audit.
We also provide those charged with governance
with a statement that we have complied with relevant

matters that may reasonably be thought to bear on our

From the matters communicated with those charged

of most significance in the audit of the consolidated and
separate financial statements of the current period and
are therefore the key audit matters. We describe these
matters in our auditors’ report unless law or regulation
precludes public disclosure about the matter or when,
in extremely rare circumstances, we determine that
a matter should not be communicated in our report
because the adverse consequences of doing so would
reasonably be expected to outweigh the public interest
benefits of such communication.

REPORT ON
THE AUDITORS’
EXAMINATION OF
THE EUROPEAN SINGLE
ELECTRONIC FORMAT
(ESEF) REPORT
In addition to our audit of the accompanying
consolidated and separate financial statements,
as included in the consolidated and separate
Annual Report, we have also been engaged by the
management of the Group to express an opinion on
compliance of the consolidated and separate financial
statements prepared in a format that enables uniform
electronic reporting ("the ESEF Report") with the
requirements of the Commission Delegated Regulation
(EU) 2019/815 of 17 December 2018 supplementing
Directive 2004/109/EC of the European Parliament
and of the Council with regard to regulatory technical
standards on the specification of a single electronic
reporting format (the "RTS on ESEF").

We were appointed by those charged with
governance on 20 October 2025 to audit the
consolidated and separate financial statements
of Augstsprieguma tīkls AS for the year ended
31 December 2025. Our total uninterrupted period
of engagement is two years, covering the years
ending 31 December 2024 to 31 December 2025.

RESPONSIBILITIES
OF MANAGEMENT
AND THOSE CHARGED
WITH GOVERNANCE
FOR THE ESEF REPORT
Management is responsible for the preparation of the
consolidated and separate financial statements in a
format that enables uniform electronic reporting that
complies with the RTS on ESEF. This responsibility
includes:
• the preparation of the consolidated and separate
financial statements in the applicable xHTML format;
• the selection and application of appropriate iXBRL
tags, using judgment where necessary;
• ensuring consistency between digitised information
and the consolidated financial statements presented
in human-readable format; and
• the design, implementation and maintenance of
internal control relevant to the application of the
RTS on ESEF.
Those charged with governance are responsible for
overseeing the financial reporting process.

AUDITORS’
RESPONSIBILITY FOR THE
EXAMINATION OF THE
ESEF REPORT
Our responsibility is to express an opinion on whether
the ESEF report complies, in all material respects, with
the RTS on ESEF, based on the evidence we have
obtained. We conducted our reasonable assurance
engagement in accordance with International Standard
on Assurance Engagements 3000 (Revised), Assurance
Engagements Other than Audits or Reviews of Historical
Financial Information (ISAE 3000) issued by the
International Auditing and Assurance Standards Board.
A reasonable assurance engagement in accordance
with ISAE 3000 involves performing procedures to
obtain evidence about compliance with the RTS on
ESEF. The nature, timing and extent of procedures
selected depend on the auditor’s judgment, including the
assessment of the risks of material departures from the
requirements of set out in the RTS on ESEF, whether
due to fraud or error. Our procedures included, among
other things:

• evaluating the completeness of the Group’s tagging of
the consolidated financial statements;
• evaluating the appropriateness of the Group’s use of
iXBRL elements selected from the ESEF taxonomy
and creation of extension elements where no suitable
element in the ESEF taxonomy has been identified;
• evaluating the use of anchoring in relation to the
extension elements; and

OPINION
In our opinion, the ESEF Report of the Group and
Company as at and for the year ended 31 December
2025 has been prepared, in all material respects, in
accordance with the requirements of the RTS on ESEF.
The responsible sworn auditor on the audit resulting in
this independent auditors’ report is Armine Movsisjana.

• evaluating the appropriateness of the format of the

our opinion.
